# Milestone 9.1: Tiered testing: affected-set tiers, test scheduler, result cache, flake handling, bisect, stages

> Written 2026-09-29 against `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-tiered-testing-and-pr-delivery-design.md` ("TT" below, "TT §n" for its sections), which binds this milestone together with the parts of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-22-adaptive-orchestrator-design.md` ("the adaptive spec") that TT does not replace (TT §11). Written on branch `docs/m9.1-m9.2-briefs` at `04c6b68`, the head of `m9-orchestrator-and-subplanners`, which is what `main` is once the M9 pull request merges. Every `file:line` below is on `04c6b68`, relative to the repository root. "M8a", "M8b", "M8c" and "M9" are `docs/milestones/M8a-orchestration-engine-core.md`, `M8b-adaptation.md`, `M8c-live-run-view.md` and `M9-orchestrator-and-subplanners.md`; where their "Implementation notes" record that the code differs from the brief, the code is cited, because the code wins. PR #18's ten contradictions between TT and M8a ("C1"–"C10" below) are resolved against the code in decisions 60–69. "M9.1" in this brief always means **this milestone**; M9's own first task is written "M9's task M9.1".

The user's design rules, which bind every decision here and are never reinterpreted:

- **The engine is deterministic.** No model merges, approves a task or writes to the base branch. Nothing reaches the base branch until the user accepts the run. Every choice this milestone adds (which tests run, whether a cached result counts, whether a test was flaky, which merge a red suite blames, what a fix task owns) is computed by the engine from git state and command exit codes, never asked of a model.
- **Only the orchestrator is an interactive PTY.** Every other agent is headless. A bisect fix task is an ordinary headless task; nothing in this milestone opens a window.
- **TDD only when the task needs it.** `test_mode` is `tdd | check | none`, and `tdd` is the default for `kind = code` (adaptive spec §8). Tiers change which suite runs after the test proof, never the proof itself.
- **Headless agents load only the user's own settings, never a repository's.** Workers run sandboxed. Tier commands run confined exactly as M8a's checks do (F1c), with the same Linux refusal without `--unconfined-checks`.
- **Protected agent-config files** (`.claude/**`, `.mcp.json`, `.codex/**`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`) change only when a task's `owns` names them exactly. A bisect or sync fix task inherits that rule unchanged; its `owns` never gains a protected path it was not given exactly.
- **There is no repo-level profile file.** The profile lives in anthrex's data directory (M8b), every new key included, and profile changes stay human: a flaky test is *proposed* for quarantine, never written into the profile.
- **A profile without the new keys behaves exactly like M8a/M8b** (decision 6 defines "exactly" as the controller ruled it, and the regression task M9.1.21 proves it: M8a's and M8b's existing end-to-end scenarios run unchanged and pass).
- **Messages never interrupt a turn** and never change a task's `owns`, route, size, test mode or acceptance (M9 decision 42b); `message stage:<n>` (decision 56) inherits that unchanged. **`refresh` stays M9's**: a merge through M8a's hand-back, never a rebase, refused on a dirty worktree, and the commits it merges never count as the task's own (decision 15's range, the spill check and the signals of decision 40 all measure from the stage head).
- **9.1 touches no remote.** It pushes nothing, never force-updates a ref (every ref write is a compare-and-swap, decision 53) and never writes the base branch; pull requests are 9.2's, which never merges, approves or enables auto-merge.
- **Tier commands put `ANTHREX_SOCKET` and `ANTHREX_DATA_DIR` under the command's own `TMPDIR`**, so a test that starts an anthrex daemon can never reach the user's (AGENTS.md hard rule 1).
- **No blocking work under a lock.** Every git, test and process command this milestone adds runs on `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` or a dedicated thread, with a timeout, and never under `daemon::lock(&m)`, the engine lock or the scheduler's lock (AGENTS.md rules 2, 10 and 11). Every git invocation goes through M8a's `worktree::run_git` / `run::git` helpers, which pass `--no-optional-locks` and the scrubbed environment.
- **Tests use `fake-agent` only**, wait with deadline loops, and never reach a real agent (every fixture pins `ANTHREX_CLAUDE_BIN`, `ANTHREX_CODEX_BIN` and the decider binary to `fake-agent` or a nonexistent path).
- **Files stay under about 600 lines.** Five files this milestone must touch are at or over the line today: `crates/config/src/lib.rs` (608), `crates/daemon/src/run/engine/mod.rs` (600), `crates/cli/src/main.rs` (600), `crates/daemon/src/run/driver/ops.rs` (598) and `crates/daemon/src/run/git/worktrees.rs` (598); `run/orch/contract.rs` (593), `run/orch/mod.rs` (590), `config/src/orchestrator.rs` (591), `run/model.rs` (588) and `run/role_launch.rs` (586) are within 15 lines of it. Task M9.1.2 splits them before any feature lands ("File sizes this milestone must respect").

## Questions for the user

None open for this milestone. The one question this brief raised is ruled below.

## Controller rulings (2026-09-29)

The same three rulings are recorded in `M9.2-pr-delivery.md`.

1. **9.1: what "a profile without the new keys behaves exactly like M8a/M8b" means.** **Ruled:** the *gate behaviour* of an untiered profile is identical to M8a/M8b's — the same commands at the same gates in the same checkouts, with the same results, bounces, states, records and texts; no cache reuse, no flaky retry, no timing group, no idle full suite, no bisect, no test-weakening signals. The *resource controls* apply to every run, as this brief proposed: every engine command goes through the test scheduler's slots and gets the slot variables (`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, `NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS`, `ANTHREX_TEST_SLOTS`, `ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD`) and its own per-step `TMPDIR` with `ANTHREX_SOCKET`/`ANTHREX_DATA_DIR` under it, and workers get the capped variables. **The binding acceptance test is that M8a's and M8b's existing end-to-end scenarios run unchanged and pass as a regression suite** (decision 6; task M9.1.21 carries it).
2. **9.2 question 2 (`run discard` on a complete `pr`-mode run)** is left open for the user; 9.2's current behaviour (refused) stays the default until the user answers. No 9.1 change.
3. **9.2 question 1 (the scratch GitHub repository)** stays an outstanding manual check of 9.2. No 9.1 change.

| `scripts/pty-smoke.py` (1801) | Imports at `:44-47`; stage calls `run_engine_stage` (`:1717`), `adapt_stage` (`:1718`), `run_view_stage` (`:1719`), `orch_stage` (`:1720`), then `== stage 12` (`:1722`). `scripts/pty_smoke_run.py` (171) exports `RUN_WAIT` (`:23`), `RUN_CMD_TIMEOUT` (`:30`), `ACCEPT_CMD_TIMEOUT` (`:35`), `POLL` (`:38`), `_git` (`:43`), `_write_script` (`:65`), `WORKER` (`:74`), `REVIEWER` (`:79`), `_run_state` (`:104`). Stage `11g` is claimed by M9.5's brief (`M9.5-tuning.md:1178`); TT §9 gives 9.1 `11h`. |
| `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | One job, macOS and Ubuntu, `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`, then the smoke with `ANTHREX_GIT: "off"`. No `cargo-nextest`, no `.config/nextest.toml`. Nothing here depends on nextest (decision 12). |

## Goal

A run no longer runs the whole suite at every gate. A repository whose profile sets the new testing keys gets **four tiers**. Tier 0 is the worker's own loop, told in its prompt how to run its test and its module's tests, and capped so it cannot take every core. **Tier 1** replaces the task's check gate: after the unchanged test proof, `build_check`, then only the tests of the **affected set** — the modules the task's own change touches plus every module that depends on them — with slow tests left out. **Tier 2** replaces the merge candidate's check with the same kind of run on the merged tree, and is skipped outright when the **result cache** already holds a green result for that exact tree. **Tier 3** is the full `check`, sharded when the runner allows, run in the background when the merge queue has been idle and at completion, one stage at a time. Every tier command goes through one daemon-wide **test scheduler** that hands out slots by priority, exports the parallelism it granted, runs timing-sensitive tests alone, and gives each command its own `TMPDIR` with `ANTHREX_SOCKET` and `ANTHREX_DATA_DIR` inside it. A failing test step is re-run once; a test that then passes is recorded as **flaky**, costs nothing, and after three flaky runs in two weeks is proposed for quarantine in `anthrex run stats`. A red full suite is **bisected** over the stage's merges with only the failing tests, and the merge that turned it red gets an engine-made **fix task** with the culprit's `owns` and the next rung's route; when no single merge is to blame, the orchestrator is woken, and after two fix tasks the user is told. On `task_done` the engine looks for **tests weakened** to pass: a deleted test file bounces, and added skip markers or lost assertions go to the reviewer, who must justify each one. A plan can now be split into **stages**: each stage has its own branch, merges flow up the stack as **propagate** items through the same width-1 queue, each stage must be green on its own, and interface changes are planned additively, with at most one `atomic` hub task per stage as the escape hatch. Delivery stays **local**: the run completes when every stage's full suite is green, and `run accept` merges the last stage, as today. A profile that sets none of the new keys runs exactly the gates M8a runs. Everything is exercised in CI with `fake-agent` and temporary repositories whose "test suites" are shell scripts.

## Scope

In:

- TT §3.1's profile keys (`build_check`, `module_test`, `module_tests`, `module_graph`, `module_names`, `full_triggers`, `slow_tests`, `timing_tests`, `skip_markers`, `test_paths`, `full_shards`) and `toolchain_id`, in the stored profile, the plan's `[profile]` and `[orchestrator.profile]`, with validation, `profile show`/`edit`, and the onboarding scout's proposal and the engine's verification of them.
- The module graph: the built-in `cargo` detector (`cargo metadata --no-deps`, engine-run, cached by manifest hashes), a graph command, and "unknown".
- The affected set (TT §3.3), tier selection and degradation (TT §3.2), tiers 0–3.
- The test scheduler (TT §3.4): slots, priorities, sizing, the timing group, the slot and load variables, per-command isolation, worker caps.
- The result cache (TT §3.5).
- Flaky tests: failing-test extraction, one retry, the history record, the quarantine proposal (TT §3.6).
- Bisect of a red tier 3 and the bisect fix task (TT §3.7).
- Test-weakening signals (TT §3.8).
- Stages (TT §4): the plan fields, validation, stage branches with `integration` as the alias of the last, propagate items, the `sync` fix task for a propagate conflict, additive interfaces and `atomic` tasks in the contracts, the plan gate's stage groups and moving a task between stages.
- `local` delivery (TT §5) as the only mode: completion after every stage's tier 3; `run accept` unchanged.
- `message` to `stage:<n>` (M9 carried it and refused it; M9 Out table, `M9-orchestrator-and-subplanners.md:99`).
- `[testing]` config (TT §8), the history lines for tiers, flakes and bisects, `HISTORY_VERSION` 3, and the `ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD` rule in `docs/timing-budgets.md`.
- The snapshot, `run status`, the report and the run view for stages, tiers and fix-task origins, as far as a stage without a pull request needs (decision 55).
- Protocol 11.

Out:

| Out | Owner |
|-----|-------|
| `[delivery]`, `run start --delivery`, `pr` mode, `CodeHost`, pushing, opening, watching and retargeting pull requests, `run prs`, `run deliver`, `run watch` | 9.2 (TT §6) |
| Tier 3's trigger "(a) before a stage PR opens" | 9.2; M9.1 builds the trigger API it calls (decision 17, "Produces for 9.2") |
| Fix-task origins `ci` and `review`, the `reply_comment` edit, the CI and review caps | 9.2. M9.1 defines all five origins on the wire (decision 39) and creates `bisect` and `sync` tasks only |
| A `sync` fix task after the **base** branch moves | 9.2 (TT §6.6); M9.1's `sync` fix task covers propagate conflicts, which 9.2 reuses |
| Pausing the stages above a closed PR | 9.2 (TT §6.7) |
| The stage node's PR, CI and threads parts; the time a stage waits on human review | 9.2 (TT §7) |
| `stage_target_lines` as a config key | 9.2 (TT §8 lists it under `[delivery]`). M9.1 writes the default range into the planner contracts as text (decision 58) |
| Test impact analysis from coverage | Never (TT §1 non-goals); learning which tests catch which changes is M9.5 or later |
| Adaptive concurrency, refitting, the router | M9.5 |
| A `--ignored` CI job, or any change to this repository's own CI | Follow-up: nothing here needs it; this repository's own profile keys are the user's to set with `anthrex profile edit` |

## Design decisions

Numbered and final. If one proves wrong or impossible, stop work on it, record the evidence under "Implementation notes", and continue with the tasks that do not depend on it. Decisions 60–69 resolve PR #18's contradictions against the code.

### Structure

1. **Where the code lives.** In `crates/daemon/src/run/`:
   - `tiers/` (new, pure): `mod.rs` (the types: `TierProfile`, `Scope`, `Affected`, `ModuleGraph`, `Step`, `StepKind`, `TierPlan`), `affected.rs` (decision 22), `graph.rs` (parsing `cargo metadata` and a graph command's JSON into a `ModuleGraph`; decision 9), `command.rs` (placeholder substitution; decision 7), `steps.rs` (which steps a tier runs; decisions 20, 21, 25), `failing.rs` (failing-test names from runner output; decision 32), `weakening.rs` (test-weakening signals from a parsed diff; decision 40), `cache_key.rs` (decision 30's key).
   - `slots/` (new): `book.rs` (pure: `SlotBook`, the slot accounting of decisions 23–25), `mod.rs` (`TestScheduler`: the async wrapper that waits for a grant, and the environment of decision 26).
   - `test_cache.rs` (new, I/O: decision 30's file).
   - `driver/tier.rs` (new, I/O: the executor of `OpKind::Tier` and `OpKind::TestAt`), `driver/graph.rs` (new, I/O: running the graph detector and `toolchain_id`, with their caches), `driver/stage_ops.rs` (new, I/O: `CreateStageBranch` and `Propagate`).
   - `git/tiers.rs` (new: changed paths, tree ids, the `-U0` diff the signals read, `show --stat`), `git/refs_tx.rs` (new: decision 53's multi-ref compare-and-swap).
   - `engine/`: `tiers.rs` (tiers 1 and 2: emitting `Tier`, routing its result into M8a's gate and merge paths), `full.rs` (tier 3: triggers, which stage, completion), `bisect.rs` (decisions 35–38), `fixes.rs` (engine-made fix tasks and their ids; decision 39), `stages.rs` (stage creation, the stage a task builds on, decision 48's `tasks_in`), `propagate.rs` (decisions 50–52), `weakening.rs` (decisions 41–42's bounce and reviewer bookkeeping; **not** `signals.rs`, which is M8a's agent-signal file).
   - `model_stages.rs` (new): `StageRecord`, `StageMerge`, `FullState`, `BisectRecord`, `SyncState`, `TierRecord`, `WeakeningRecord`, included into `run::model` with `#[path]` and `pub use`, as `model_rounds.rs` is (`model.rs:23-25`).

   **Pure** (M8a decision 2's grep): `run/tiers/**`, `run/slots/book.rs`, `run/model_stages.rs`, `run/engine/{tiers,full,bisect,fixes,stages,propagate,weakening}.rs`. **I/O**: `run/slots/mod.rs`, `run/test_cache.rs`, `run/driver/{tier,graph,stage_ops}.rs`, `run/git/{tiers,refs_tx}.rs`. The engine never runs a command: it emits an op, and the driver's executor runs it on `spawn_blocking` (AGENTS.md rule 2). *(The files the logic would otherwise grow are at 485–600 lines.)*
2. **Protocol.** `PROTO_VERSION` becomes **11** (header). Every addition is listed in Interfaces "proto": `PlanTask.{stage, atomic, atomic_reason}`, `PlanEdit::AmendTask.stage`, `TaskOrigin`, `RepoProfile`'s and `ProfileSpec`'s new keys, `ProfileVerification`'s new checks, `TaskInfo.{stage, origin, fixes, tier, weakening}`, `RunInfo.{stages, test_slots}`, `StageInfo`, `TierInfo`, `FullInfo`, `HistoryLine::{Tier, Flaky, Bisect}`, `HistoryStats.flaky_proposals`. New enum variants are appended **last**; every new struct field is `#[serde(default)]` (`PlanTask.stage` with a default function returning 1), so an M9 `run.json`, plan, profile and snapshot still load. `RepoProfile` and `ProfileSpec` keep `deny_unknown_fields`: an M9.1 profile with a new key does not load in an M9 daemon, which the version bump already refuses. Every new message, variant and field gets a MessagePack and JSON round-trip test (AGENTS.md rule 4). Every client is updated in the same change as M9 did it: task M9.1.3 gives every exhaustive `match` in the TUI, the CLI and `anthrex mcp` the minimum arm so the workspace builds and speaks protocol 11; the behaviour follows in M9.1.5 (`anthrex mcp`'s onboarding schema) and M9.1.20 (the TUI and the CLI). *(AGENTS.md rule 4.)*
3. **Configuration.** A new top-level `[testing]` table (TT §8), in a new `crates/config/src/testing.rs`: `Config.testing: config::Testing`, one `read_testing` call in `parse` and one arm in `report_unknown_keys` (`lib.rs:555-569`). Keys, defaults and ranges are in Interfaces "config". `test_slots` and `test_cache_days` are daemon-wide: the daemon reads them once at start (`lifecycle.rs`) into the one `TestScheduler` and the one `TestCache`, and a change takes effect at the next daemon start. `full_idle_secs`, `bisect_fix_max`, `flaky_quarantine_after` and `flaky_window_days` are frozen into the run at start as `RunLimits.testing` (`#[serde(default)]`), so a later edit cannot change a live run's rules. `RunContext` (`driver/context.rs:14`) gains `testing: config::Testing`, set by a new `RunContext::with_testing` from `lifecycle.rs:282`, so its ten callers in tests need no change. *(TT §8. `[testing]` is a top-level table because the scheduler serves every run, not one orchestrator.)*
4. **Words.**
   - A **tier** is TT §2's testing level, 0–3. A **tier job** is one engine op that runs one tier for one tree (`OpKind::Tier`); it runs one or more **steps** (`build`, `tests`, `timing`, `shard`), each one shell command.
   - A **scope** is what a step's result may be reused for: `gate` (tiers 1 and 2, slow tests left out) or `full` (tier 3). Decision 30 explains why the cache is keyed by scope, not tier.
   - A **tiered profile** is one that sets at least one of decision 5's keys; an **untiered** one sets none (decision 6).
   - The **affected set** is TT §2's; `Affected::Full(reason)` means "run `check`".
   - A **stage** is TT §2's; a **stage head** is the commit its branch points at; the **stage layout** is decision 46's `single` or `multi`.
   - A **propagate** is TT §4.2's merge of stage *k* into stage *k+1*.
   - A **fix task** is a task the engine adds itself: `origin = bisect` (decision 37) or `sync` (decision 51) in this milestone. It is an ordinary task in every gate.
   - A **signal** is a test-weakening signal (TT §3.8). A **flake** is a test that failed and then passed on the retry of decision 33.

### The profile keys

5. **The new keys.** `RepoProfile` (`crates/proto/src/profile.rs:31`) and `ProfileSpec` (`crates/proto/src/run.rs:134`) gain TT §3.1's keys and one more, `toolchain_id` (TT §3.5 names `profile.toolchain_id` without listing it in §3.1). All are optional and default to **off**: an absent key never switches anything on, so decision 6 holds by construction.

   | Key | Type (stored / plan) | Absent means |
   |-----|------|------|
   | `build_check` | `Option<String>` | tiers 1 and 2 run `check` (TT §3.2 degradation) |
   | `module_test` | `Option<String>` | no per-module command |
   | `module_tests` | `Option<String>` | no whole-set command |
   | `module_graph` | `Option<String>`: `"cargo"`, `"none"`, or a command | no graph: every tier runs `check` |
   | `module_names` | `Option<ModuleNames>`: `"cargo"` \| `"dir"` | `"cargo"` when `module_graph = "cargo"`, else `"dir"` |
   | `full_triggers` | `Vec<String>` / `Option<Vec<String>>`, globs | none |
   | `slow_tests` | `Option<String>`, a runner filter expression | none |
   | `timing_tests` | `Option<String>`, a runner filter expression | none |
   | `skip_markers` | `Vec<String>` / `Option<Vec<String>>` | none (TT's example list is what the scout proposes for a language, never a default) |
   | `test_paths` | `Vec<String>` / `Option<Vec<String>>`, globs | none |
   | `full_shards` | `Option<u8>`, `1..=16` | 1 |
   | `toolchain_id` | `Option<String>`, a command | toolchain `"none"` in the cache key |

   The run's frozen `Profile` (`run/model.rs:61-91`) gains **one** field, `#[serde(default)] pub tiers: TierProfile` (the struct is in `run/tiers/mod.rs`, Interfaces), filled by `resolve_profile` (`run/plan.rs:119`) with M8a's decision 7 precedence: the plan's key, else `[orchestrator.profile]`'s, else absent; a present-but-blank string resolves to `None`, so a plan can switch a configured key off, as for `check`. The stored profile reaches the run through `RepoProfile::spec()` (`profile.rs:75`) and `profile::resolve::run_profile` (`resolve.rs:51`), which gain the keys. *(TT §3.1: "All are optional. A profile that sets none of them behaves exactly as today".)*
6. **Tiered and untiered profiles; what "exactly like M8a/M8b" means.** `TierProfile::is_tiered()` is true when any key of decision 5 is set. For an **untiered** profile every gate is exactly M8a's: the check gate emits M8a's `OpKind::Check`, the merge candidate runs `check` in `MergeCandidate` with `tier: None`, completion runs decision 37's final check and completes even when it is red, and none of the following happens: cache lookups or writes, the flaky retry, the timing group, the idle tier 3, a bisect, a test-weakening signal, a tier-0 prompt line. Same commands at the same gates in the same checkouts; same results, bounces, states, `CheckRecord`s, texts and history lines. **Resource controls are not behaviour and apply to every run** (controller ruling 1): every engine command (setup, proof, check, candidate check, final check) waits for its slots in the scheduler and gets decision 26's variables and decision 28's isolation, and workers get decision 27's caps. Stages (decisions 43–55) are a plan feature, not a profile feature, and work with either kind of profile; a plan with one stage and an untiered profile is an M8a run. The proof, and the ruling's binding acceptance test: task M9.1.21 runs every M8a end-to-end file listed in "Starting point" and M8b's (`run_e2e_adapt.rs`, `run_e2e_adapt_engine.rs`, `run_e2e_adapt_profile.rs`) **unedited, and they pass** (`git diff --stat main -- <those files>` is empty at the end of the milestone), and adds `untiered_profile_emits_m8a_ops`. *(User rule; TT §3.1, §9 last 9.1 item.)*
7. **Placeholders.** A tier command is a shell command with placeholders the engine substitutes before running it (`run/tiers/command.rs`, pure). Only these names are placeholders; any other text, `${VAR}` and shell brace expansion included, is left alone. Every substituted value is shell-quoted with M8a's quoting (`proof_command`'s, `run/proof.rs:84-86`).

   | Placeholder | Allowed in | Expands to |
   |-------------|-----------|------------|
   | `{module}` | `module_test` | one module name |
   | `{modules}` | `module_tests` | the affected module names, sorted, space-separated |
   | `{modules:<t>}` | `module_tests` | `<t>` once per module, `%` replaced by the name, joined by spaces (`{modules:-p %}` → `-p 'a' -p 'b'`) |
   | `{filter:<t>}` | `module_test`, `module_tests`, `check` | `<t>` with `%` replaced by decision 25's filter expression, shell-quoted; **empty** when no filter applies |
   | `{shard}`, `{shards}` | `check` | the shard number (1-based) and the shard count |
   | `{test}` | `single_test` | M8a's, unchanged |

   Inside a template, `%%` is a literal `%`, and `}` ends the template. TT's example `{modules:-p %}` is this syntax; its bare `{module}`/`{modules}` too. TT's example never shows where a slow-test filter goes; `{filter:<t>}` is that place (`cargo nextest run -p {module} --profile fast {filter:-E %}`; `pytest {filter:-m %}`), because a runner-neutral engine cannot append a flag it does not know. *(TT §3.1's placeholders, completed.)*
8. **Validation of the new keys** (`run/tiers/mod.rs::validate(tiers, check) -> Vec<(String, String)>`, pure, key and message), called by `profile::proposal::validate` (`proposal.rs:100`) for a stored profile and an edit, and by `run/plan.rs::resolve_profile`'s caller for a plan or config profile (a problem refuses the plan with `PlanError { field: <key>, rule: "profile", .. }`, as M8a's other profile problems do). Exact messages (the key is the prefix):
   - `module_test: must contain {module} exactly once`
   - `module_tests: must contain {modules} or {modules:<template>}`
   - `module_test: needs modules` (and the same for `module_tests`) when `modules` is empty
   - `<key>: {<name>} is not allowed here` for a placeholder outside decision 7's "Allowed in"
   - `module_names: cargo needs module_graph = "cargo"`
   - `slow_tests: module_test must contain {filter:<template>} to leave slow tests out` (and the same for `module_tests`, and for `timing_tests`)
   - `full_shards: must be between 1 and 16`; `full_shards: check must contain {shard} and {shards}` when it is above 1
   - `<key>: <glob> <reason>` from M8a's `run::globs::validate_glob` for `full_triggers` and `test_paths`
   - `skip_markers: at most 32 markers, each 1 to 64 characters`
   - `<key>: must not be blank` for a present blank command in a stored profile (a plan's blank resolves to `None`, decision 5)
   - `<key>: at most 2000 characters` for every command and filter

   `module_graph`'s own value is not validated beyond that: any value but `"cargo"` and `"none"` is a command. *(Adaptive spec §6: a proposed command is verified by the engine; a typed one is checked for shape.)*
9. **The module graph** (TT §3.1, §3.3 step 3).
   - **`"cargo"`** is computed by the engine: the tier executor runs `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --no-deps --offline` in the checkout the tier job runs in, after `materialize`, on its blocking thread, confined exactly as the job's commands, with a timeout of `GRAPH_TIMEOUT` = 60 s. `run/tiers/graph.rs::from_cargo_metadata(json, root)` keeps the packages whose `id` is in `workspace_members`, each with its name and its manifest directory relative to the workspace root, and its dependencies of **every kind** (normal, dev, build) that name another workspace member (by package `name`; a `rename` changes nothing). A dev-dependency counts because a change to it can break the dependent's tests (anthrex's `fake-agent` depends on the daemon only as a dev-dependency).
   - **A command** prints `{"<module>": ["<dep>", …], …}` on stdout; `from_command_json` parses it. Every dependency must be a key; otherwise the graph is unknown (`module graph: <dep> is not a module`).
   - **`"none"`**, an absent key, a failing or timed-out command, invalid JSON, or two modules with one name make the graph **unknown**, and every tier falls back to `check` (decision 22 step 3). The first time a run meets an unknown graph, its log and report say `module graph unknown: <reason>; every tier runs check` once (`Run.graph_note`).
   - **Cache.** `<repo_dir>/module-graph.json`: at most 32 entries, each `{key, graph}`, where `key` is the 64-bit FNV-1a (M8b's `Fnv1a64`, `store.rs:269-283`, which `store::fingerprint` (`:364`) uses per file; made `pub(crate)` for this) of every tracked `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` at the job's commit, path and content, in path order (for a command graph: of the command text and the files `manifests` lists). Read and written by `driver/graph.rs` on the job's blocking thread; a hit skips the command. The cache is advisory: a corrupt file is replaced.
   - The engine never builds a graph from a model's claim; the onboarding scout only proposes the key (decision 12).
10. **From a path to a module.** `modules` (M8a's existing key, `crates/*` in anthrex's profile) names module directories. A changed path belongs to the module M8a's sizing rule already gives a glob (`run::globs::glob_module`, `globs.rs:74-100`): the first `modules` pattern whose components match the path's leading components (`*` matching one component), the module directory being those leading components. A new `pub fn path_module(path, modules) -> Option<String>` beside it shares that code; where M8a's function answers `.` (no pattern matched), it answers `None`, and the path is in no module. So sizing and the affected set can never disagree about which module a file is in. Its **name** is, with `module_names = "dir"`, the directory's last component, and with `"cargo"`, the name of the graph's package whose manifest directory is that directory. A module directory with no package is `Affected::Full("no package for module <dir>")`. Names are what `{module}`/`{modules}` and a graph command use. *(TT §3.1 `module_names`.)*
11. **Toolchain and profile hash** (TT §3.5's key parts).
    - **Toolchain id:** when `toolchain_id` is set, the first tier job of a run runs it in its checkout (the directory decides rustup overrides), with a 30 s timeout, and returns `toolchain: Some(<16 hex FNV-1a of exit code and stdout>)` in its result; the engine stores it as `Run.toolchain` and passes it to every later job, so it runs once per run. A failure gives `toolchain: Some("unknown")`, and a job whose toolchain is `"unknown"` neither reads nor writes the cache. Without the key the toolchain part is `"none"`.
    - **Profile hash:** `Run.profile_hash`, the 16-hex FNV-1a of the canonical JSON of the run's frozen `Profile` (every field, `env` included), computed once in `build_run` (`plan.rs:312`). Pure.
12. **Onboarding proposes, the engine verifies, the user confirms** (TT §3.1 last bullets, adaptive spec §6).
    - `ONBOARDING_CONTRACT` (`scout/contract.rs:13-18`) gains one paragraph (Interfaces "Contracts"): what each key means, that `cargo nextest` commands may be proposed only when `cargo nextest --version` succeeds in the scout's checkout, and that nothing requires it.
    - The key set grows in the four places M8b keeps it: `RepoProfile`, `scout/report.rs::PROFILE_KEYS` and its limits (`report.rs:42-60`, `:107-157`), the MCP schema (`crates/mcp/src/tools_scout.rs:43-73`), and `profile/proposal.rs::EDIT_KEYS` (`:31-50`). `REVERIFY_KEYS` (`:19-27`) gains the command keys and what they depend on: `build_check`, `module_test`, `module_tests`, `module_graph`, `module_names`, `slow_tests`, `timing_tests`, `full_shards`, `toolchain_id`.
    - `profile/verify.rs::run_commands` (`:136`) runs, after M8b's three and in this order: `build_check` (must exit 0); `module_graph` (the graph must be known, decision 9); `module_test` for **one** real module (the first module name in sorted order, with `{filter:…}` expanded to leave slow and timing tests out) and `module_tests` for that same one module (each must exit 0); `toolchain_id` (must exit 0). Each has its own `CommandCheck` in `ProfileVerification` (new `#[serde(default)]` fields) and, when it fails, becomes a `DroppedCommand` through `apply_verification` (`:306`) as M8b's do. `module_test` and `module_tests` are dropped with `needs a module graph` when the graph is dropped and `module_names = "cargo"`. `full_shards` above 1 is kept only when the verified `check` contains `{shard}` and `{shards}`.
    - Confirming stays the user's (`anthrex profile confirm`); a changed key re-proposes the profile through M8b's `ProposalOrigin::Edit`, unchanged.
    - `profile::summary` (`profile/mod.rs:63`), which triage and `get_context` read, gains one line when the profile is tiered: `tiers: build_check, module tests by <cargo|command> graph, <n> slow filter, <n> full triggers` (the parts that are set). `proposal.rs::show_text` (`:409`) prints every new key.

### The tiers

13. **Tier 0: the worker's own loop.** Never a gate. With a tiered profile, `worker_head` (`run/contract.rs:92-125`) prints, after M8a's `Check command:` line, the lines of Interfaces "Prompts (exact)": the module test command, the modules the task's `owns` fall in (decision 10 applied to each glob's literal prefix; omitted when none maps), and `Run only these while you work; the engine runs the wider suites after task_done.` Its session is capped by decision 27. With an untiered profile the prompt is byte-identical to M9's (M8a's exact-text prompt tests pass unchanged). *(TT §3.2 tier 0.)*
14. **Tier 1: the task gate.** For a tiered profile, a task in `TaskState::Check` gets `OpKind::Tier { tier: 1, .. }` from `gates::start_check` (`gates.rs:152-177`) instead of `OpKind::Check`, in the same `<task>.proof` scratch checkout at the task's claimed head (`ScratchAt`), with the same `timeout_secs` per step. `next_gate`'s check condition (`gates.rs:40`) becomes "the profile has `check`, or it is tiered and has `build_check`, `module_test` or `module_tests`". The proof gate before it is unchanged: TT's "test proof → `build_check` → affected tests" is M8a's `Proof` state followed by this `Check` state. The result goes through `gates::check_done` (`:261`) as a check's does: green passes the gate; red appends a `CheckRecord { on_candidate: false, tier: Some(TierRecord), .. }` and calls `deciders::summarise(run, i, GateKind::Check, <the red step's command>, ..)` (`engine/deciders.rs:215`), so the bounce is M8a's `check_failed_message` with M8b's summary, naming the red step's command. Tier 1 is not reused for tier 2 (TT §3.2): the cache decides (decision 31). *(TT §3.2 tier 1; adaptive spec §11.3, replaced.)*
15. **Tier 1's changed paths** are `git diff --name-only -z --no-renames <stage head>...<task head>`, three dots, where the stage head is the head of the task's stage when the op is emitted (`Run::stage_head`, decision 47). This is M8a's net-diff and spill range (`git/done.rs:178-187`) with the stage head in place of the run head, so work merged in by a hand-back or a `refresh` never counts as the task's own. TT §3.2's `<task start>...HEAD` is not used (decision 60). *(C4.)*
16. **Tier 2: the merge candidate.** For a tiered profile, `MergeCandidate` (`engine/ops.rs:158-170`) carries `tier: Some(Box<TierSpec>)` and `check: None`; the executor (`driver/merge.rs::candidate`, `:15-155`) runs the tier-2 job where M8a runs `confined(check)` (step 4, `:90-108`), in the integration worktree on the materialized candidate. Its changed paths are `git diff --name-only -z --no-renames <stage head> <candidate>`, two dots: exactly what the merge adds to the stage it lands in (TT §3.2). The candidate's tree is `merge_tree`'s tree, known before `commit_tree`, so the cache is consulted **before** `materialize`: when every step of the plan hits, the executor skips `materialize`, runs no command, and goes straight to the second guard and the compare-and-swap (decision 31). A red step gives M8a's `OpResult::CandidateRed` with a new `#[serde(default)] tier: Option<Box<TierOutcome>>`, and the engine's `candidate_done` (`engine/merge.rs:153-175`) is unchanged: rung 1 for the task with `candidate_red_message`. A propagate item runs the same job (decision 50). *(TT §3.2 tier 2; adaptive spec §11.5 step 2, replaced; the rest of §11.5 unchanged.)*
17. **Tier 3: when it runs** (tiered profiles only; an untiered profile runs M8a's final check and nothing else).
    - **(b) Idle.** When a running run's merge queue has been idle for `full_idle_secs` (no queued task, no pending propagate, no `MergeCandidate` or `Propagate` op in flight; `Run.queue_idle_since`, set by the engine when the queue empties and cleared when an item enters), and a created stage's head has no green tier 3, tier 3 starts on the **lowest** such stage, at scheduler priority `FullIdle`. The engine's `Tick` event drives it (M8a's 1 s tick).
    - **(c) Completion.** Decision 19, at priority `FullStage`.
    - **(a) Before a stage PR opens** is 9.2's. M9.1 builds the call it will use, `engine::full::request(run, stage, FullWhy::Deliver, now, fx)`, which starts tier 3 on that stage at `FullStage` priority unless the stage head is already green or a tier-3 job or bisect is in flight for it (Produces for 9.2). For an **untiered** profile `request` emits nothing and returns `false` (tier 3 is tiered-only, decision 6); 9.2 adds its own untiered branch (running `check` once before a PR opens) in its task M9.2.7.
    - At most **one** tier-3 job or bisect per run at a time (`Run.full_op`). A job runs on the stage head as it was when it started; if the head moves meanwhile, the result still applies to the commit it ran on (`StageRecord.full`, decision 47), and the next idle or completion pass sees the new head without a green result.
18. **Tier 3: how it runs.** `OpKind::Tier { tier: 3, stage: Some(n), .. }` with `task_id = None`, in a run-level scratch checkout `Run::full_path()` = `<wt_dir>/runs/<run>/.full` (a task id cannot start with `.`, `validate.rs:49-57`), prepared at the stage head by M8a's `prepare_scratch_in` (`git/worktrees.rs:483-514`) with the setup marker, exactly as `<task>.proof` is. Its steps are decision 21's `full` plan: `check`, split into `full_shards` shard steps when above 1, each shard its own step that releases its slots and asks again (decision 24's "yield between shards"), plus the timing step. Scope `full`; the cache is consulted per step. Green sets the stage's `full.green_at` to that commit and appends a `tier` history line. Red, after decision 33's retry, starts decision 35's bisect. *(TT §3.2 tier 3, §3.4 sizing.)*
19. **Completion with a tiered profile** (replaces M8a decision 37's final check, TT §11). `complete::refs_verified` (`complete.rs:159-203`) keeps its guard; after `RefsOk`, instead of the final `Check`:
    - every created stage whose `full.green_at` equals its head is done; if all are, `complete()` runs as today;
    - otherwise tier 3 starts on the lowest stage lacking it (decision 17(c)), and the next pass after its green result repeats the check;
    - a red result leads to a bisect and a fix task (decisions 35–37), which leaves a task unfinished, so `complete_pass` waits;
    - a red result with no fix task to wait for (no culprit, decision 38; or the cap) leaves the stage `full.red_at == head`: `complete_pass` starts no tier 3 on a head that is already red, and the run waits, with the attention line, until the stage head moves (a fix task merged) or the user or orchestrator sends the `finish` edit;
    - with `run.finish_edit` set, a red stage no longer holds completion: the run completes with M8a's `final_check_failed = true` and the attention line `tier 3 red on stage <n>: <tests>` in place of `final check failed on the run head` (`snapshot.rs:177-221`).
    - **`local` is the only delivery mode in 9.1.** No `[delivery]` table and no `--delivery` flag exist yet (9.2). `run accept` merges `anthrex/<run>/integration`, which is the last stage's head (decision 53), onto the base with `--no-ff`, exactly as M8a's `git::accept` (`accept.rs:52-75`) does; it is untouched. *(TT §3.2 tier 3 (c), §5 `local`, §11 rows "§5.3 step 6" and "M8a decision 37".)*
20. **Degradation** (TT §3.2), for a tiered profile:

    | Profile has | Tier 1 and tier 2 run | Tier 3 runs |
    |-------------|------------------------|-------------|
    | `build_check`, a known graph, `module_test` or `module_tests` | `build_check`, then the affected set's tests (decision 21) | `check` |
    | no `build_check` | `check` (the whole tier) | `check` |
    | no `module_test` and no `module_tests`, or an unknown graph | `build_check`, then `check` in place of the tests | `check` |
    | no `check` | `build_check` and module tests as above; `Affected::Full` runs the module tests of every module when a module command exists, else `build_check` alone | nothing: the run is **unverified** (M8a's `Run.unverified`, `plan.rs:371`, and reviews one size up, `validate.rs:222-223`, unchanged) |

    An untiered profile is decision 6's M8a.
21. **The steps of a tier job** (`run/tiers/steps.rs::plan(tier, affected, profile) -> TierPlan`, pure).
    - **Scope `gate`** (tiers 1 and 2):
      - `Affected::Modules(set)` with the set non-empty: a `build` step (`build_check`); then one `tests` step (`module_tests` with the whole set) or, without `module_tests`, one `tests` step per module in sorted order (`module_test`), each with decision 25's gate filter; then a `timing` step when `timing_tests` is set (decision 25).
      - `Affected::Modules(∅)` (a docs-only change, decision 22 step 2): the `build` step alone.
      - `Affected::Full(reason)`: one `tests` step running `check`, with the gate filter when `check` contains `{filter:…}`; then the timing step when `check` has `{filter:…}` and `timing_tests` is set. `build_check` does not run: `check` is the full gate by definition (TT §3.1's comment on `check`).
    - **Scope `full`** (tier 3): `check`, as `full_shards` `shard` steps when above 1, with the full filter; then the timing step when `check` has `{filter:…}` and `timing_tests` is set.
    - The steps run in order and stop at the first red one (after its retry).
    - **Log lines** (task history for tiers 1–2, run log for tier 3, and the history record's `full_reason`): `tier <n>: <k> modules (<names, first 5>)`, `tier <n>: build only (no module affected)`, `tier <n>: full suite (<reason>)` (TT §3.2's example `tier 1: full suite (full trigger: Cargo.lock)`), `tier <n>: cached (<k> steps)`.

### The affected set

22. **`affected(changed, tiers, hub, source, graph) -> Affected`** (`run/tiers/affected.rs`, pure, no I/O; TT §3.3):
    1. A path matching `full_triggers` or the profile's `hub` → `Full("full trigger: <path>")` / `Full("hub file: <path>")`.
    2. Each path maps to its module (decision 10). A path in no module is **ignored** when it matches no `source` glob and no `test_paths` glob and `source` is not empty (a file nothing executes, TT's docs exception); otherwise → `Full("unowned path <path>")`. An empty `source` never ignores a path, because then nothing says what executes.
    3. An unknown graph → `Full("module graph unknown: <reason>")`.
    4. The closure over reverse dependencies: the changed modules plus every module that depends on one of them, transitively (cycles allowed).
    5. The closure is every module of the graph → `Full("every module affected")`.
    6. Otherwise `Modules(closure)`, which is empty when every path was ignored.

    The first matching rule wins, in that order, and names the first path (in sorted order) that triggered it. For anthrex, `crates/cli` (package `anthrex`, C8) depends on every crate but `anthrex-fake-agent` (`crates/cli/Cargo.toml:14-18`), so a change to any library crate includes `anthrex`, which is why `slow_tests` matters (TT §3.3's last paragraph).

### The test scheduler

23. **One scheduler per daemon, counted in slots.** `run::slots::TestScheduler`, created once in `lifecycle.rs` with `test_slots` slots (`[testing] test_slots`, else `std::thread::available_parallelism()` − 2, at least 1) and shared by every run through `RunService` (`Arc<TestScheduler>`). **Every engine command that runs repository code** goes through it (TT §3.4): `setup` (`driver/ops.rs:73-93`), the test proof, M8a's `Check` and the final check, the candidate check, every tier step, every bisect step, and profile verification's commands (`profile/verify.rs`, at the lowest priority, so onboarding never starves a run). Slots are held **per step**: a job asks, runs one step, releases, and asks again for the next. Nothing holds a slot while it waits for another. The wait is async (`TestScheduler::acquire(..).await`, a `tokio::sync::Notify` per waiter); the step itself runs on `spawn_blocking` as today (`driver/ops.rs:29-35`). `SlotBook`'s state is under a `std::sync::Mutex` taken with `daemon::lock`, held only to update counters, never across an `await` or a command (AGENTS.md rules 2, 3). *(TT §3.4 "Slots".)*
24. **Priority and sizing** (`run/slots/book.rs`, pure).
    - Classes, highest first: `Candidate` (a merge candidate's or propagate's tier 2, and M8a's candidate check), `Gate` (tier 1, M8a's check, the test proof, `setup`), `FullStage` (tier 3 at completion and, from 9.2, before a PR opens; bisect steps), `FullIdle` (tier 3 when the queue is idle), `Verify` (profile verification). Among equals: a task on the critical path first (`SlotRequest.critical`, set by the engine from M8a's critical path when it emits the op), then first come (a sequence number).
    - What a step asks for: `Candidate`, `FullStage` and `FullIdle` ask for **all** slots, `Gate` for **half** (`max(1, slots / 2)`), `setup` for **one**, a bisect step for half, `Verify` for half.
    - Granting: the waiting requests are ordered by (class, critical, sequence). Only the first may be granted: it gets `min(asked, free)` as soon as `free ≥ 1` (an exclusive step, decision 25, only when `free == slots`). The next is considered only after that. So a request never waits behind a lower-priority one, and waits for slots held by lower-priority work only until that work's current step ends — the "yield between shards" of TT §3.4, applied to every multi-step job.
    - Deadlock is impossible: no holder waits. `FullIdle` may wait long on a busy daemon, which is what idle means.
25. **The timing group and exclusive steps.** With `timing_tests` set, a job's `timing` step runs the tests matching it in their own invocation, and that step is **exclusive**: it is granted only when no other step holds a slot, and it gets every slot, so nothing runs beside it. The job's other test steps leave the timing tests out. Any step that would run timing tests without being able to leave them out (a `check` without `{filter:…}` while `timing_tests` is set) is exclusive as a whole.
    - **Filter expressions**, composed with the words `not`, `and` and parentheses only, which nextest filter sets, `pytest -m` and `pytest -k` all read: gate test steps `not (<slow>) and not (<timing>)`; gate timing step `(<timing>) and not (<slow>)`; full test and shard steps `not (<timing>)`; full timing step `(<timing>)`. A missing part drops its clause; with neither key set, `{filter:…}` expands to nothing.
    - *(TT §3.4 "The timing group".)*
26. **What a step's environment carries** (after M8a's `engine_env` scrub and the profile's `env`, so these win over a profile value of the same name): `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, `NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS` and `ANTHREX_TEST_SLOTS`, each the number of slots granted; `ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD`, the slots held daemon-wide right after this grant divided by `test_slots`, one decimal place (`0.5`); and decision 28's isolation variables. `exec::run_matching` (`exec.rs:165`) gains an `extra: &[(String, String)]` set after `env`, so no call site's scrub changes. `docs/timing-budgets.md` gains TT §3.4's rule (Interfaces "timing-budgets rule"). *(TT §3.4 "Slots", `ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD`.)*
27. **Workers are capped, not scheduled** (tier 0). `role_launch.rs::worker_env` (`:130-138`) adds, before its `TMPDIR`, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, `NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS` and `ANTHREX_TEST_SLOTS`, each `max(1, test_slots / max_writers)` with the run's `max_writers`. Reviewers, scouts and planners run no tests and get nothing. *(TT §3.4 "Workers' own test runs".)*
28. **Isolation** (TT §3.4 "Isolation", resolved against the code; C6):
    - **`TMPDIR`**: every step gets a **fresh** directory `<base>/s<op>-<step>`, made 0700 before the step and removed after it (on the step's blocking thread). `<base>` is the directory M8a already gives that checkout, `Repo::tmp()` of its repository directory (`confine.rs:139-174`, `git/tmp.rs:38`) — the confinement's writable `TMPDIR` when confined, and the same path, made with M8a's `private_dir`, when not. It is **never inside the checkout**: TT's `<worktree>/.anthrex-tmp` would be untracked content in the tree the tier judges.
    - **`ANTHREX_SOCKET=<TMPDIR>/d.sock` and `ANTHREX_DATA_DIR=<TMPDIR>/data`** on every step, confined or not. Confined, `engine_env` has already removed every `ANTHREX_*` (`exec.rs:147-156`) and the sandbox denies the daemon's socket (`seatbelt.rs:299-319`) while allowing Unix sockets under the writable set (`:246-250`), so a test that starts a daemon binds its own socket. Unconfined (Linux with `--unconfined-checks`), M8a passes the daemon's own `ANTHREX_SOCKET` through today; these variables replace it, which is what makes AGENTS.md hard rule 1 hold for tests on Linux. The path is short (`/private/tmp/ax-<uid>/<16 hex>/s<op>-<step>/d.sock`, under 64 bytes), well inside a Unix socket's 104-byte limit.
    - **`HOME` is never changed.** A profile cannot set it: it is reserved (`reserved_env.rs:150`), as `TMPDIR` is (`:157`). TT's "unless the profile's `[env]` sets it" cannot happen.
    - Network, sandbox and the Linux refusal are M8a's, unchanged: a confined tier step has no network unless the user's `confined_network`; a run on Linux still needs `--unconfined-checks` or `[orchestrator] unconfined_checks = true` (`confine.rs:74-81`).
29. **Durability** (TT §3.4 "Durability", resolved; C7). Scheduler state is not journaled. `OpKind::Tier` and `OpKind::TestAt` are reads, reconciled as `NotStarted` beside `Check` (`reconcile/mod.rs:212-220`); after a daemon restart the run comes back paused (`engine/restore.rs:121-125`), and `restore::lost` (`:165-236`) clears the task's `gate_op`, the run's `full_op` or the bisect's `probe`, so the first running pass after `run resume` issues a new op from the task's or stage's state, as M8a does for a lost `Check`. Nothing re-runs "from the journal". `CreateStageBranch` and `Propagate` write refs and have git reconcile rows (decision 53).

### The result cache

30. **Key, value, place** (TT §3.5).
    - **Key** (`run/tiers/cache_key.rs`, pure): `(tree id, scope, command after substitution, affected, profile hash, toolchain id)`, compared field by field (a lookup never trusts a hash alone). `scope` is `gate` or `full` (decision 4), **not** the tier number: TT's own example — a candidate on an unmoved stage head has the task's tree, "which tier 1 already passed" — needs tier 1's result to answer tier 2, and a key with the tier in it never would (defect 3). `affected` is the sorted module names joined by `,`, `full:<reason>`, or `-` for a `build` step, whose result does not depend on it. The tree id is `git rev-parse <commit>^{tree}` (tier 1 and 3) or `merge_tree`'s tree (tier 2).
    - **Value:** `ok` (always `true`, below), `secs`, `flaky` test names, the last 200 lines, `at`, the run and tier that wrote it.
    - **Where:** `<repo_dir>/test-cache.jsonl`, one JSON line per green step. `run::test_cache::TestCache` loads a repository's file once (on `spawn_blocking`), drops lines older than `test_cache_days` and rewrites the file atomically when it dropped any (M8b's `write_atomic`, `store.rs:102`), keeps an in-memory index, and appends each new green step with one `write_all` and `sync_all`. At most 20 000 lines per repository; the oldest go first. `test_cache_days = 0` turns the cache off.
    - **Only green results are written.** A red step, a timed-out step and a step the retry could not make green are never cached, so a flaky red is always run again. A step that passed on its retry is green, and is cached with its `flaky` names.
    - An **untiered** profile neither reads nor writes the cache (decision 6), and nor does a job whose toolchain is `"unknown"` (decision 11).
31. **Hits.** Every step of every tier looks itself up before it asks for slots. A hit is not run, is recorded in the job's outcome (`cached: true`), and appears in the history record's `cache_hit`. When every step of a tier-2 job hits, the candidate is not materialized (decision 16): **tier 2 is skipped**, TT §3.5's common case. The cache never skips the test proof, which runs `single_test` at two commits and is not a tier.

### Flaky tests

32. **Failing test names** (`run/tiers/failing.rs::names(output) -> Vec<String>`, pure) are read from a red step's whole output, streamed line by line by the executor (not only the 200-line tail), with built-in patterns for four runners: libtest (`test <name> ... FAILED`, and the names under `failures:`), nextest (`FAIL [ <time>] <binary> <name>` and `TIMEOUT [ … ]`), pytest (`FAILED <node id>`), and Go (`--- FAIL: <Name>`). Names are deduplicated in order and capped at 50. A red step with no name found has none, and is retried whole.
33. **Retry once** (tiered profiles only; TT §3.6).
    - A red **`tests`, `timing` or `shard`** step is run once more. A red `build` step is not (a build is not flaky). With names found, at most 10 of them, and the profile's `single_test`, the retry runs `single_test` once per name (`{test}` substituted, as the proof does), in the same checkout, holding the step's grant; otherwise it re-runs the whole step with a new grant.
    - **Passes on retry** → the step is green. Each name that failed first and passed on the retry is a **flake**: the job's outcome lists it, the task (or the run, for tier 3) gets the history line `tier <n>: flaky <name> passed on retry`, and a `flaky` history line is appended (decision 57). A flake costs no bounce and no rung, and a flaky tier 3 never starts a bisect.
    - **Fails again** → the step is red, and the tier's normal failure follows: rung 1 and up for tiers 1 and 2 (M8a's ladder, unchanged), a bisect for tier 3.
    - A timed-out step is not retried: a hang is not a flake.
34. **Quarantine is proposed, never applied** (TT §3.6). `run::stats::aggregate` (`stats.rs:83`) also reads the `flaky` lines and returns `HistoryStats.flaky_proposals`: every test recorded flaky in at least `flaky_quarantine_after` distinct runs within the last `flaky_window_days`, with its run count and last time. `anthrex run stats` prints each as `proposal: add <test> to slow_tests (flaky in <n> runs in the last <d> days)` and, under it, the offered fix task as a command the user can run: `anthrex run start --goal "Make the test <test> deterministic; it failed and then passed on retry in <n> runs"`. Nothing is written to the profile; the user adds the test to `slow_tests` with `anthrex profile edit` (M8b), which re-verifies. `run stats`' module doc, "Recording only" (`stats.rs:1-4`), gains "and proposing flaky tests for quarantine".

### A red tier 3: bisect

35. **When a bisect starts** (tiered profiles only). A tier-3 job on stage *n* at commit `H` is red after its retry (decision 33). Then, in order:
    - if the stage's `full.bisect_fixes` has reached `bisect_fix_max` → decision 38's "to the user", no bisect;
    - if the red step gave no failing test names, or the profile has no `single_test` → decision 38 with the reason `no failing test names to bisect with` / `the profile has no single_test to bisect with`; the suite is never re-run whole as a bisect step (TT §3.7 step 2);
    - otherwise a bisect starts: `StageRecord.bisect = Some(BisectRecord)`, `Run.full_op` held by it, a `tier` history line for the red job, and the run log line `stage <n>: tier 3 red (<tests, first 3>); bisecting <m> merges`.
    - The two checks above are made by the caller, `full::full_done`; `bisect::start(run, stage, head, tests, now, fx)` itself only sets up the record and emits the first probe, and needs `Run.full_op` free. So 9.2 can start a bisect for a CI failure through the same function without charging `bisect_fix_max` (Produces for 9.2).
36. **The algorithm** (`run/engine/bisect.rs`, a pure state machine over ops; TT §3.7 steps 1–2).
    - **The range.** A stage branch is a line of `--no-ff` merges the engine wrote, and the engine keeps that line itself: `StageRecord.merges: Vec<StageMerge>` (decision 47), one entry per commit it put on the branch (`Task { id, commit }`, `Propagate { from, commit }`). The candidates are the entries after the stage's last green tier 3 (`full.green_at`), or after the stage's creation point when it has none, up to `H`, in order: `M1 … Mm`, with `G` the green or creation commit before them.
    - **Probes.** One probe is `OpKind::TestAt { dir: run.full_path(), commit, commands }` (a read; `NotStarted` on restart): `materialize` at `commit`, then `single_test` once per failing name (at most 10, the first ones), at priority `FullStage`, half the slots. A probe is **red** when a command fails twice (the retry of decision 33, inside the probe), so a flaky test cannot move the bisect.
    - **Order.** First `G`: red → no culprit, `the failing tests already fail at <G7>`. Then `H`: green → no culprit, `the failing tests pass alone at <H7>; they fail only with the whole suite`. Then binary search over `M1 … Mm` for the first red entry, with `G` green and `H` red as its ends; `⌈log2 m⌉` probes. The search assumes the failure, once introduced, stays; a non-monotonic history still ends at one entry, deterministically.
    - **The culprit** is that entry: a `Task` entry names the task; a `Propagate` entry is **no single culprit** (`the first red merge is the propagate of stage <k>`), because the failure lives in how two stages combine, and each lower stage was green on its own when it propagated.
    - A `bisect` history line records the stage, the range, the probes and the result (decision 57).
37. **A culprit gets a fix task** (TT §3.7 step 3), made by `engine/fixes.rs::add_fix(run, FixSpec, now, fx)`, the one function that adds an engine-made fix task (M9.2 adds `ci`, `review` and `sync`-after-base through it). For a bisect:
    - id `fix<n>` (decision 39); `origin = bisect`; `fixes = Bisect { culprit, stage, tests }`;
    - `stage` the culprit's stage; `epic` the culprit's; `kind = code`; `size` the culprit's current size; `owns` **the culprit's `owns`, copied exactly** (so a protected path the culprit named exactly is named exactly again, and nothing more); `deps` empty; `priority` 100;
    - `test_mode = check` with the reason `the failing tests already exist; they must pass`, which M8a's rules raise to `tdd` for a hub task (`validate.rs:153-155`), as for any task;
    - `route` = `roster::escalate(&run.roster, &culprit.route)` (`roster.rs:134`), the culprit's route one rung up (adaptive spec §10 rung 2), written as a full `RouteSpec`;
    - brief and acceptance from Interfaces "Prompts (exact)": the culprit's title and brief, the failing test names, the ≤ 40-line summary of the red step (M8b's `CheckSummary` decider, queued as for a red check, or its fallback), and `git show --stat --format=%h%x20%s <culprit merge>` read by the probe that found it;
    - resolved with `resolve_task_lenient` and given its branch and worktree exactly as M9's engine-made integration review task is (`engine/integration.rs:110-141`); if M8a's rules refuse it (rule 9, a peer runtime overlapping a running task's `owns`, is the one that can), the culprit's own route is tried, and if that is refused too, decision 38 applies with the rule's message;
    - **no approval**: it is not a new epic and not a promotion, so M9's gate holds do not apply (TT: "it stays inside an already-approved stage's `owns`");
    - the wake note `stage <n> tier 3 red (<tests, first 3>): bisected to <culprit>; added fix task <id>`, and `full.bisect_fixes += 1`.
    The culprit stays `merged`: merged is terminal, and a fix is always a new task (TT §3.7 last line).
38. **No single culprit, and the cap** (TT §3.7 step 4).
    - **No culprit** (decision 36's two early ends, a propagate entry, a refused fix task, or no names or no `single_test`): the stage's `full.red_at = H`, the run's attention line `tier 3 red, no single culprit: <tests, first 5> (stage <n>: <reason>)`, and the wake note `stage <n> tier 3 red, no single culprit: <tests, first 3>; plan a fix`. The orchestrator plans the fix with ordinary edits (a task in that stage). Without an orchestrator (a `--plan` or fast-path run) the attention line is the user's.
    - **The cap:** once a stage has had `bisect_fix_max` bisect fix tasks (`full.bisect_fixes`), its next red goes to the user: no bisect and no fix task; the attention line `stage <n>: tier 3 still red after <k> fix tasks: <tests, first 5>; fix it with a task, or end the run with the finish edit`, and the wake note with the same text, so an orchestrator raises it with the user (its contract rule 26).
    - Either way the run waits (decision 19) until the stage head moves or the `finish` edit.
39. **Fix-task ids and origins.**
    - `Task.origin: TaskOrigin` (`#[serde(default)]`, `Plan`), on the wire as `TaskInfo.origin`: `plan`, `bisect`, `sync`, `ci`, `review`, in that order. M9.1 creates `plan`, `bisect` and `sync` tasks; `ci` and `review` are defined now so 9.2 adds no variant.
    - `Task.fixes: Option<FixOf>` names what a fix task fixes; `TaskInfo.fixes` is its display text (`bisect of t4`, `propagate of stage 1 into stage 2`), which 9.2 extends (`CI run 123`, `thread by @user`).
    - Ids are `fix<n>`, `n` from `Run.fix_seq` (`#[serde(default)]`), the first free one at or above it. The pattern `^fix[0-9]+$` is reserved: a plan edit or plan file from **any** source that names such an id is refused with `PlanError { field: "id", rule: "id", message: "fix<n> ids are reserved for fix tasks the engine adds" }`. An M9 `run.json` holding such a task still loads; the rule applies to new edits.

### Protecting existing tests

40. **Signals** (TT §3.8), computed on `task_done` for a tiered profile with `test_paths` or `skip_markers` set, never otherwise (decision 6). `OpKind::VerifyDone`'s executor, after `verify_done`, reads `git diff -U0 --no-renames --no-color <stage head>...<head>` (decision 15's range) and, for the changed `.rs` files, `git grep -l -F '#[cfg(test)]' <head> -- <files>`, and passes both to `run/tiers/weakening.rs::signals(diff, cfg_test_files, test_paths, skip_markers) -> Vec<Signal>` (pure). `OpResult::DoneChecked` gains `#[serde(default)] signals: Vec<Signal>`.
    - `DeletedTestFile { path }`: a deleted file matching `test_paths`.
    - `SkipMarker { path, line, marker }`: an added line containing a `skip_markers` entry, `line` on the new side.
    - `AssertionLoss { path, line, removed, added }`: in a file matching `test_paths` or listed by the `git grep`, the removed lines containing an assertion marker outnumber the added ones; `line` is the first such removed line, on the old side. Markers by extension (`weakening::ASSERTION_MARKERS`): `.rs` `assert`; `.py` `assert`, `pytest.raises`; `.js .jsx .ts .tsx` `expect(`, `assert`, `should`; `.go` `t.Error`, `t.Fatal`, `assert.`, `require.`; any other `assert`, `expect(`, `should`.
    - At most 20 signals per claim, deleted files first; the rest are counted (`… and <n> more`).
41. **A deleted test file bounces** (TT §3.8). A `DeletedTestFile` whose path the task's `owns` does not name exactly (M8a's `run::globs::names_literally`, the protected-file rule) is a rung-1 done-gate failure, `ladder::gate_failure(.., GateKind::Done, ..)`, beside the protected and generated bounces in `engine/done.rs::checked` (`:321-430`), with `deleted_test_file_message` (Interfaces "Prompts (exact)"), which carries TT's `deleted test file <path>; restore it or own it exactly`.
42. **Every other signal goes to the reviewer** (TT §3.8).
    - The accepted claim's signals are kept on the task (`Task.signals: Vec<Signal>`, replaced by each accepted claim) and numbered `W1…`. `reviewer_prompt` (`run/contract.rs:168-236`) gains, after the check block, the `Test changes to justify:` block of Interfaces "Prompts (exact)", one line per signal with its id, file and line, and the instruction to answer each with a finding whose text starts with its id: `minor` with `<id> accepted: <reason>`, or `critical`.
    - **The check.** `review::submit` (`engine/review.rs:263-356`) refuses a `submit_review` whose findings do not start with every listed id, with `the review must address <ids>: add a finding whose text starts with each id`, so the reviewer answers in the same turn (TT's "re-run once", as an immediate refusal rather than a new session: cheaper, and the same session has read the diff). A second submission that still leaves an id out is accepted, and the engine adds one `important` finding per missing id, `W<n> (<path>:<line>) was not justified by the review`, so the verdict is `changes` and the worker must restore the test or explain (adaptive spec §11.4 step 2).
    - A reviewer contract line is not added: the instruction is in the prompt, where it is needed, and `REVIEWER_CONTRACT` (`contract.rs:34-40`) stays byte-identical.
    - The report's task section lists the signals and how each was answered.

### Stages

43. **Stages in the plan** (TT §4.1). `PlanTask` gains `#[serde(default = "first_stage")] stage: u16` (1), `#[serde(default)] atomic: bool` and `#[serde(default)] atomic_reason: Option<String>`; `PlanEdit::AmendTask` gains `#[serde(default)] stage: Option<u16>`. They reach every plan path unchanged: plan files, `run edit`, the orchestrator's `edit_plan` (`add_task`, `split_task`, `amend_task`) and a sub-planner's `submit_epic`, because those carry `PlanTask` and `PlanEdit` as they are. TT §6.9 lists "the task fields `stage`, `atomic`" under 9.2; they are 9.1's, because 9.1 builds stages (TT §10), and 9.2 needs nothing more (defect 12). An `amend_task` of `stage` is allowed only on a task that has not started, as M8a's route, size and test-mode amends are (`edits_state::not_started`, `edits_state.rs:12`), and is refused otherwise with M8a's per-state text.
44. **Validation** (added to `validate_graph.rs::validate_tasks_with`, `:66-177`, for every source; TT §4.1 "Validation"):
    - `stage` is 1 to 32: `(field "stage", rule "4.1") must be between 1 and 32`.
    - A task depends only on tasks in the same or an earlier stage: `(task, "stage", "4.1") stage <s> cannot depend on <dep> in stage <t>`, which displays as TT's `task <id>: stage: stage 2 cannot depend on t9 in stage 3` (`PlanError`'s display, `plan.rs:64-75`).
    - Stage numbers are contiguous from 1 and every stage has at least one task, counting tasks in any state (a stage whose tasks were all cancelled still exists): `(None, "stage", "4.1") stages must be numbered from 1 without gaps: stage <k> has no task`.
    - At most one `atomic` task per stage, counting tasks not cancelled: `(task, "atomic", "4.3") stage <n> already has the atomic task <other>`.
    - `atomic` needs a reason: `(task, "atomic_reason", "4.3") required when atomic is true`.
    - After approval, decision 46's layout rule.
45. **Reserved ids** (C10). `check_fields` (`validate.rs:259-314`) refuses, beside `integration` (`:28`) and `running` (`:31`), any id matching `^stage-[0-9]+$` with `(task, "id", "id") stage-<n> is reserved for stage branches`, so a task branch `anthrex/<run>/stage-1` can never collide with a stage branch; and decision 39's `^fix[0-9]+$`. Both for every source (M9's `-int<n>` reservation stays planner-only, `orch/rules.rs:35-41`).
46. **The stage layout is fixed when the plan is approved.** `Run.stage_layout: StageLayout { Single, Multi }` (`#[serde(default)]`, `Single`) is set when the run's plan is first approved (the gate's approval, or `--yes`): `Multi` when any task has `stage > 1`, else `Single`.
    - **`Single`** is M8a's layout exactly: stage 1's branch **is** `anthrex/<run>/integration`, and no `stage-*` ref exists. M8a's end-to-end tests assert that no other branch exists (`run_e2e_crash.rs:215-224`), and TT §4.1 says "a run with a single stage is today's run". After approval, a `Single` run's edits may name no stage but 1: `(task, "stage", "4.1") this run was approved with one stage; its tasks stay in stage 1`.
    - **`Multi`** has `anthrex/<run>/stage-<n>` for every stage and `anthrex/<run>/integration` as the alias of the highest created stage (decision 53). After approval a `Multi` run may gain stage *N+1* (contiguity still holds); a promoted fast-path run (M9 decision 29) is `Single`.
    - Before approval (`planning`, `awaiting_approval`) any stage may be set; worktrees pre-warmed during the gate from the base (M8a) are re-pointed by M8a's existing re-point when their stage is created from a later head (`git/worktrees.rs:256-321`).
47. **Stage records and stage heads.** `Run.stages: Vec<StageRecord>` (`#[serde(default)]`, in `model_stages.rs`; Interfaces), one per created stage, filled by `build_run` with stage 1 and by `restore` for an M9 `run.json` that has none (stage 1, `branch = run_branch()`, `head = run_head`). `Run.run_head` keeps its meaning — the head of `anthrex/<run>/integration` — and is now always the head of the **highest created stage**; `set_stage_head(run, n, commit)` (`engine/stages.rs`) is the one writer of both. Every **task-context** use of `run.run_head` becomes `run.stage_head(task.stage())`, and the rest stays:

    | Site | Change |
    |------|--------|
    | `engine/dispatch.rs:158-171, :249-253` (`PrepareWorktree.from`), `:338-340` (`start_commit`), `contract.rs::worker_head`'s start fallback | the task's stage head |
    | `engine/done.rs:176-219` (`VerifyDone.run_head`, `start` fallback) | the task's stage head |
    | `engine/review.rs:52-90` (`PrepareReview.base_ref`) | the task's stage head |
    | `engine/merge.rs:34-83` (`MergeCandidate.expected_run_head`, `run_branch`), `:255-280` (`HandBack.run_head`) | the task's stage head and branch |
    | `engine/holds.rs`, `engine/worker_messages.rs` (`HandBack` for M8a ruling N5's hold and M9's `refresh`), `engine/fallback.rs` (`CountCommits`), `engine/ladder.rs` (`DiffSoFar`), `engine/history.rs` (`MeasureDiff`) | the task's stage head |
    | `engine/integration.rs:128` (`review_target`) | the head of the highest stage holding one of the epic's tasks |
    | `engine/merge.rs:105-108` (`run_head`, `last_green_candidate` on `Merged`) | `set_stage_head`; `last_green_candidate` per stage (`StageRecord.last_green_candidate`), with `Run.last_green_candidate` kept equal to the highest stage's for decision 37's untiered final check |
    | `engine/complete.rs` (`VerifyRefs`, accept), `run_worktrees` (`:305-321`) | every created stage ref in the guard (decision 53); accept unchanged; `.full` added to the salvaged worktrees |
    | `engine/restore.rs:323`, `engine/merge.rs:482-526` (`run resume --rebaseline`) | every stage head from its ref, then `run_head` from the highest |
    | `snapshot.rs`, `RunInfo.{run_branch, run_head}` | unchanged: the integration branch and head |

    Task M9.1.12 greps `run_head` in `crates/daemon/src/run` (186 lines today) and records every remaining use with the reason it is not task-context. For a `Single` run every one of these is the same commit as before, so the change is invisible to M8a's tests.
48. **Creating a stage branch, and which merged work a task sees** (TT §4.2 table).
    - A task is runnable when M8a's rule holds **and** every dependency's merge is in its stage's head: a dependency in the same stage always is; one in an earlier stage is when it is in `StageRecord.tasks_in` of the task's stage. `tasks_in` is the set of task ids whose merges the stage head contains: a task merge adds its own id; a propagate from stage *k* adds the ids stage *k* held at the head it propagated (`Propagate.tasks`, captured when the op is emitted); a new stage starts with its parent's. A `reported` dependency (M9's research and review tasks) merges nothing and is always satisfied.
    - Stage *n* is created from stage *n−1*'s head when the first task of stage *n* would be runnable in it (its dependencies are all in stage *n−1*'s `tasks_in`), and not before stage *n−1* exists. `OpKind::CreateStageBranch { root, branch, from }` creates `refs/heads/anthrex/<run>/stage-<n>` with a create-only `update-ref <ref> <from> ""` through the `GitQueue`; stage *n* then becomes the highest created stage, and `anthrex/<run>/integration`, already at that commit, needs no write.
    - Stage 1 of a `Multi` run is created at approval from `run_head` (the base, nothing having merged).
49. **The merge queue names a target stage** (TT §4.2). It stays width 1 across the run (`merge::merging`, `engine/merge.rs:26-30`, is true while a `MergeCandidate` or a `Propagate` op is in flight). `Run.merge_queue` stays the FIFO of task ids; each task's candidate targets its own stage (branch and expected head from decision 47). `Run.propagate_due: BTreeSet<u16>` (`#[serde(default)]`) holds the stages that need a propagate from the stage below. `start_merge` (`:34-83`) takes the **lowest** due propagate first, when it is not waiting (decisions 51, 52), and M8a's queue head otherwise: a propagate keeps the upper stages current and makes waiting dependents runnable soonest.
50. **Propagate** (TT §4.2 "Flowing changes up the stack"). Whenever stage *k*'s head moves and stage *k+1* exists, `k+1` enters `propagate_due`. The item emits `OpKind::Propagate` (Interfaces), executed by `driver/stage_ops.rs` exactly as `driver/merge.rs::candidate` executes a candidate: guard; `merge_tree(<k+1 head>, <k head>)`; `commit_tree` with parents `[<k+1 head>, <k head>]` and the message `anthrex: propagate stage-<k> into stage-<k+1>`, `--no-ff` by construction; tier 2 on it (a tiered profile; changed paths `<k+1 head> <candidate>`) or M8a's `check` (an untiered one) in the integration worktree; guard; decision 53's compare-and-swap; reattach. `Merged` sets stage *k+1*'s head, appends `StageMerge::Propagate { from: k, commit }`, adds the propagated `tasks_in`, and enters *k+2* into `propagate_due` when it exists, so changes flow up the stack one stage at a time. A propagate whose stage-*k* head stage *k+1* already holds (`StageRecord.synced_from`) is dropped without an op. `from: 0` is reserved and never emitted by 9.1: 9.2 queues a **base sync** as a `Propagate` with `from: 0` and the base branch's head as `from_head`, `propagate::done` routes a `from: 0` result to 9.2's handler (9.2 adds that one arm), and the resulting `StageMerge::Propagate { from: 0, .. }` entry is, like every propagate entry, no single culprit to decision 36's bisect.
51. **A propagate conflict becomes a `sync` fix task** (TT §4.2; C5). `Propagate`'s `Conflict { files, tree }` (the conflicted tree `merge-tree --write-tree` prints even on a conflict) makes `fixes::add_fix` add, to stage *k+1*: id `fix<n>`, `origin = sync`, `fixes = Propagate { from: k, to: k+1, head }`, `owns` = the conflicted files, each named exactly, `kind = code`, `size = M`, `test_mode = check` with the reason `a merge resolution; the tiers check it`, `route` from policy (`RouteSpec::default()`), no dependencies, priority 100, and the brief of Interfaces "Prompts (exact)". `Task.sync = Some(SyncState { onto: <k head>, base_tree: tree, tasks: <stage k's tasks_in at that head> })`.
    - **Its worktree already contains the conflicted merge.** After `PrepareWorktree` (from stage *k+1*'s head) and before its first session, the engine emits M8a's `OpKind::HandBack { worktree, run_head: <k head>, task_head: <k+1 head>, list_merged: false }`, executed by `run::git::hand_back` (`git/handback.rs`): `merge-tree`, `commit-tree`, the engine-written `MERGE_HEAD` and the markers, **never `git merge`** (M8a "Summary of deviations", decisions 25/36). The first prompt lists the conflicted files.
    - **Its spill check is against the conflicted tree**, not the stage head: `VerifyDone` gains `#[serde(default)] spill_base: Option<String>`, and with it the executor classifies `git diff --name-only -z --no-renames <spill_base> <head>` (what the worker changed relative to the engine's merge) instead of the three-dot range, so stage *k*'s merged work is not counted as the task's. Tier 1's changed paths stay decision 15's range, which includes stage *k*'s work — exactly what the resolution must be tested with.
    - While a `sync` task of stage *k+1* is unfinished, stage *k+1*'s propagate waits. When it merges, stage *k+1* holds `onto`: its `tasks_in` gains `SyncState.tasks`, `synced_from = onto`, and a newer stage-*k* head re-enters `propagate_due`.
    - 9.2 reuses this task, with `fixes = Base { .. }`, for a conflict when the base branch moves (TT §6.6).
52. **A red propagate** (tier 2 red after its retry, or M8a's check red for an untiered profile) has no single owner. Stage *k+1* records `propagate_red = Some(<k head>)`; the attention line `propagate of stage <k> into stage <k+1> is red: <tests, first 5>` and the wake note `propagate of stage <k> into stage <k+1> is red: <tests, first 3>; plan a fix in stage <k> or <k+1>` follow; the propagate is not retried until stage *k*'s or stage *k+1*'s head moves, which re-enters it. Dependents that need stage-*k* work wait. No automatic fix task: the orchestrator plans it.
53. **Refs: guard, compare-and-swap, alias.**
    - **Guard.** `git::guard_refs` (`git/merge.rs:220-286`) takes a list of `(branch, expected)` pairs instead of one run branch: every created stage branch and `integration` for a `Multi` run, `integration` alone for a `Single` one (unchanged). Its halt texts are M8a's per ref. `work_on_base` (`:315-343`) already covers every `refs/heads/anthrex/<run>/…` ref.
    - **Compare-and-swap.** `git::refs_tx::cas(git, root, updates: &[(ref, new, old)], timeout)`: one `git update-ref --no-deref --stdin` transaction (`start`, one `update` per ref, `prepare`, `commit`), through the `GitQueue`, all or nothing. A merge into the highest stage of a `Multi` run moves that stage's ref and `integration` together; into a lower stage, only its own ref; a `Single` run keeps M8a's `cas_update` (`:143-171`).
    - **The alias.** `integration` always points at the highest created stage's head, so `run accept`, `RunInfo.run_branch`/`run_head`, the M8c view and M8a's accept code keep working unchanged in local mode (TT §4.2). M8a's `reattach` (`:174-196`) after each swap keeps the integration worktree on it.
    - **Reconcile.** `CreateStageBranch`: the ref at `from` → `Replay(Ok)`; absent → `NotStarted`; elsewhere → `Replay(RefMoved)`. `Propagate`: as M8a's `MergeCandidate` row (`reconcile/git.rs:122-162`) — target ref unchanged → reattach and `NotStarted`; its head's parents are `[expected_to_head, from_head]` → `Replay(Merged)`; else `Replay(RefMoved)`.
54. **Additive interfaces and `atomic` tasks** (TT §4.3).
    - **Contracts.** `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT`'s rule 12 becomes "interfaces first **and additive**" and gains the new "Stages" rules; `PLANNER_CONTRACT` gains two rules (decision 58, exact in Interfaces).
    - **Reviewer.** For a task with `interface_change = true`, in a `Multi` run or with a tiered profile, `reviewer_prompt` adds TT's line `Check that the old interface still works: callers outside this task must still build.` (never for a `Single` untiered run, decision 6).
    - **Tier 2 enforces it mechanically**: a non-additive interface task's candidate fails `build_check` against the stage head.
    - **`atomic = true`** marks the escape hatch (a protocol bump that must update every client, AGENTS.md rule 4). The engine makes it a hub task (`Task.hub = true`: it runs alone, `tdd` for code by rule 8.2, reviewed at `Frontier`), does **not** apply the rule-7.2.2 raise to L for spanning modules with `interface_change` (`validate.rs:100-138`), so it keeps its size (at least M, as every hub task), and allows one per stage (decision 44).
55. **The plan gate, the view and the snapshot** (TT §4.1's plan gate, and the stage parts of TT §7 that need no pull request).
