    - **Snapshot.** `TaskInfo.stage`, `origin`, `fixes`, `tier` (the last tier record: tier, affected summary, steps, cached, flaky, secs) and `weakening` (the signals and whether each was answered); `RunInfo.stages: Vec<StageInfo>` (Interfaces) with each stage's branch, head, counts, tier-3 state, bisect state, fix tasks and `propagate_red`; `RunInfo.test_slots`. TT §7's `pr` field is 9.2's (`#[serde(default)]` then).
    - **Run view (M8c).** When `stages.len() > 1`, a **stage node** (`NodeKey::Stage { run, n }`) sits between the root's tier and its tasks, and each task hangs under its stage. Its content row is `<glyph> stage <n>/<N>  tier 3 <✓|✗|…|·>` (9.2 appends `#<pr>  ci <…>  <k> threads`). The stage inspector shows progress (`<merged>/<tasks> merged`), the tier-3 line (state, duration, shards, flaky), and fix tasks by origin. A `Single` run's view is M8c's, unchanged.
    - **Task inspector.** Rows `stage`, `origin` (with `fixes` for a fix task, TT §7's `fixes: bisect of t4`) and `tier` (`tier 1: 3 modules · 2m10s · 1 cached · flaky t_x`).
    - **The plan gate** groups tasks by stage node, and M8c's edit form (`crates/tui/src/run_edit.rs`) gains `EditField::Stage`, shown while the task has not started, cycling from 1 to the highest stage plus one; it sends `AmendTask { stage }`, and the daemon's validation (decision 44) answers an invalid move inline, as for every field.

### Messages, history, contracts and reports

56. **`message` to `stage:<n>`** (TT §12.1; M9 Out table, `M9-orchestrator-and-subplanners.md:99`). `MessageTarget::Stage(n)` resolves at acceptance to every unfinished task of stage *n*, and each gets M9 decision 42b's per-state rule. A stage with no task answers `stage <n> has no task`. The refusal `stage recipients arrive with milestone 9.1` (`run/edits_orch.rs:124`) is removed, and the three tests that assert it (`engine/tests/worker_messages.rs:410`, `crates/cli/tests/run_cli_message.rs:101, :142`) now assert delivery.
57. **History** (TT §8). `HISTORY_VERSION` becomes 3 (`history.rs:18`). `HistoryLine` appends `Tier(TierRunRecord)`, `Flaky(FlakyRecord)` and `Bisect(BisectLine)` (Interfaces), tagged by the existing `type` key (`tier`, `flaky`, `bisect`): TT §3.6's `{"kind":"flaky", …}` names the tag `kind`, but M8b's tag is `type` (PR #18's note; defect 9). Each is appended through M8b's journaled `OpKind::AppendHistory` (`engine/history.rs:40`) with a stable `record_id` (`<run>/tier/<op>`, `<run>/flaky/<op>/<test>`, `<run>/bisect/<stage>/<n>`), so recovery never duplicates one (`history_io::contains_record`, `:74`). Per-stage PR records are 9.2's. `run stats`' task and budget aggregates ignore the new lines (`stats.rs:83`), as they ignore M9's `role_route`; decision 34 reads the `flaky` ones.
58. **Contracts and prompts** (exact texts in Interfaces).
    - `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT`: rule 12 amended; rules 34–37 appended under "Stages and testing": stage per reviewable unit that builds and passes alone, `stage_target_lines` of 300 to 800 changed lines as a target, one epic usually one stage on the large path, dependencies only on the same or earlier stages, additive interface changes (expand, migrate, contract), `atomic` with a reason at most once per stage, bisect and sync fix tasks are the engine's and are not to be cancelled without cause, a red tier 3 with no culprit is the orchestrator's to plan. The numbering of rules 1–33 does not move (M9 cites them).
    - `PLANNER_CONTRACT`: rules 11 and 12 (stages; additive interfaces).
    - `WORKER_CONTRACT` and `REVIEWER_CONTRACT` are unchanged; tier 0's lines are in the prompt (decision 13), the signals in the reviewer prompt (decision 42).
    - `ONBOARDING_CONTRACT`: decision 12's paragraph.
    - The digest (`run_status`) gains `stages` (the snapshot's `StageInfo`, trimmed like the other lists) and each task's `stage` and `origin`; `get_context` shows the profile's tiers line (decision 12).
    - `stage_target_lines` is text, not a key, in 9.1: TT §8 puts the key in `[delivery]`, which is 9.2's (defect 17).
59. **CLI and report.**
    - `anthrex run status` prints, for a `Multi` run, one line per stage (`stage 2/3  anthrex/<run>/stage-2  4/5 merged  tier 3 green (41m)`), and each task's `stage` and `origin` in `--json` (the snapshot); `anthrex run stats` prints decision 34's proposals; `anthrex profile show` and `edit` know the new keys (M8b's code, extended by decision 12). No new subcommand.
    - `REPORT.md` (`run/report.rs`, `report_task.rs`) gains a "Testing" section: per stage, tier-3 results with duration, shards and flaky tests; every bisect with its range, probes and culprit; every fix task with what it fixed; the unknown-graph note (decision 9). Each task's section gains its last tier-1 line and its signals with their answers.

### PR #18's contradictions, resolved against the code

PR #18 (`gh pr view 18`) lists ten contradictions between TT and what M8a built. M9 resolved C1, C2, C3, C5 (for `refresh`) and C9 for §12 (M9 decisions 42b, 42c, 42e; its defects 23–28). These ten decisions record each one's resolution for this milestone.

60. **C4, the tier-1 diff base.** TT §3.2 (L77) says `<task start>...HEAD`; TT §12.2 (L516) and M8a decision 32 say `<run head>...HEAD`. With `<task start>`, work merged into the task by a hand-back or a `refresh` would count as its own and widen its affected set. **Resolved:** tier 1 uses `<stage head>...<task head>` (decision 15): M8a's range, with the task's stage head in place of the run head, which for a `Single` run is the same commit.
61. **C5, the hand-back is not `git merge`.** M8a's workers commit on a detached HEAD in standalone checkouts; only the engine moves `anthrex/<run>/<task>`, by compare-and-swap after an object import, and the hand-back is `merge-tree` plus `commit-tree` with an engine-written `MERGE_HEAD` (`git/handback.rs`). TT §4.2's propagate conflict "whose worktree already contains the conflicted merge" is **resolved** by M8a's `OpKind::HandBack` executed by `run::git::hand_back` before the sync task's first session (decision 51); stage merges and propagates are `merge-tree` + `commit-tree` + CAS like M8a's candidates (decision 50). Nothing in this milestone runs `git merge`, `git rebase` or a force update.
62. **C6, `TMPDIR` and `HOME`.** M8a gives confined commands a per-checkout `TMPDIR` outside the checkout; `TMPDIR` and `HOME` are reserved keys a profile cannot set (`reserved_env.rs:150, :157`); confined commands lose every `ANTHREX_*` and the sandbox denies the daemon socket; a `.anthrex-tmp` inside the checkout would be untracked content in the tree under test; and TT never mentions the network-off confinement or the Linux refusal. **Resolved** (decision 28): a fresh per-step directory under M8a's per-checkout `TMPDIR`, outside the checkout; `ANTHREX_SOCKET` and `ANTHREX_DATA_DIR` under it on every step, confined or not (which is what closes the hole on unconfined Linux runs); `HOME` never changed; confinement, network and the Linux refusal unchanged.
63. **C7, what a restart re-issues.** TT §3.4 says running test ops "are re-issued from the journal, exactly as M8a re-issues a `Check` op today". M8a does not: it reconciles a pending `Check` as `NotStarted`, the run comes back paused, and `run resume`'s first running pass issues a new op from the task's state (M8a decisions 44–45; `reconcile/mod.rs:212-220`, `engine/restore.rs:165-236`). **Resolved** (decision 29): `Tier` and `TestAt` behave as `Check` does; a lost tier-3 job or bisect probe is re-issued from `Run.full_op` and `StageRecord.bisect` by the next running pass.
64. **C8, the cli package is `anthrex`.** TT §3.1's example `slow_tests` names `package(anthrex-cli)`; the package is `anthrex` (`crates/cli/Cargo.toml:2`), and `crates/cli` depends on every crate but `anthrex-fake-agent` (TT §3.3 says "every crate"). **Resolved:** this brief's examples, the smoke stage and the manual check write `package(anthrex)`; decision 22's note states the real dependency set. Nothing in the engine names a package.
65. **C10, `stage-<n>` against task ids.** Task ids `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,15}$` reserve only `integration` (and, since M9, `running`), so a task `stage-1` would own `anthrex/<run>/stage-1`. **Resolved** (decision 45): `^stage-[0-9]+$` is reserved for every source; so is `^fix[0-9]+$` (decision 39).
66. **C1, the task states** (touching 9.1 through fix tasks and `message stage:<n>`). TT's `waiting` is `pending` and its `blocked(other)` is the five non-question reasons (M9 decision 42b). **Resolved:** a fix task is created `pending` and moves through M8a's states like any task; `message stage:<n>` applies M9's per-state table to each recipient (decision 56). No state is added.
67. **C2, `amend_task` cannot change `owns`.** A fix task's `owns` are set once when the engine creates it (decisions 37, 51), and nothing in this milestone amends `owns`; moving a task to another stage is the new `AmendTask.stage`, for a task not yet started (decision 43). A scope change is still `cancel_task` or `split_task` plus `add_task` (M9 contract rule 28).
68. **C3, run-wide pause and resume.** `PlanEdit::Pause`/`Resume` are run-wide unit variants (M8a decision 45). **Resolved:** 9.1 adds no per-stage pause. A stage waiting on a red propagate or a red tier 3 is waiting on its state, not paused; the run-wide edits keep their meaning. Pausing the stages above a closed PR (TT §6.7) is 9.2's, and will need its own mechanism.
69. **C9, what bounds a turn.** Not a 9.1 matter for agents; for tier commands, every step has M8a's `check_timeout_secs` (default 1800, `plan.rs:32`) plus `OUTPUT_GRACE`, and the module graph and toolchain commands their own timeouts (decisions 9, 11). A slot waiter has no timeout of its own: it waits only while higher-priority steps, each bounded, run (decision 24).

### `config` (`crates/config/src/testing.rs`, new)

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Testing {
    pub test_slots: Option<u32>,          // None: available_parallelism() - 2, at least 1
    pub full_idle_secs: u64,
    pub test_cache_days: u32,
    pub flaky_quarantine_after: u32,
    pub flaky_window_days: u32,
    pub bisect_fix_max: u8,
}

pub(crate) const KNOWN_TESTING_KEYS: &[&str];
```

| Key (`[testing]`) | Default | Range | Frozen into the run? |
|-----|---------|-------|------|
| `test_slots` | absent: logical cores − 2, at least 1 | 1..=256 | no: daemon-wide, read at start |
| `full_idle_secs` | 120 | 10..=86 400 | yes (`RunLimits.testing`) |
| `test_cache_days` | 14 | 0..=365 (0 = off) | no: daemon-wide |
| `flaky_quarantine_after` | 3 | 1..=100 | yes |
| `flaky_window_days` | 14 | 1..=365 | yes |
| `bisect_fix_max` | 2 | 0..=10 | yes |

Out-of-range and wrong-typed values use M6's problem format (`"{key}: {message} (using {default})"`, `lib.rs:109`); an unknown key in `[testing]` is `testing.<key>: unknown key, ignored`. `RunLimits` (`run/model.rs:124`) gains `#[serde(default)] pub testing: TestingLimits { full_idle_secs, bisect_fix_max, flaky_quarantine_after, flaky_window_days }`, built by `run_limits` (`plan.rs:159-191`) from `RunContext.testing`.

impl SlotBook {
    pub fn new(slots: u32) -> Self;

    pub fn load(&self) -> String;                    // held / slots, one decimal
}

impl TestScheduler {

    pub fn slots(&self) -> u32;
    pub async fn acquire(&self, req: SlotRequest) -> SlotGrant;   // SlotGrant releases on drop
}

pub fn default_slots() -> u32;                                                              // cores - 2, at least 1

Every method is called only from `spawn_blocking` (it may read or write the file).

```

and extends, with `#[serde(default)]` fields: `MergeCandidate { .., tier: Option<Box<TierSpec>>, guarded: Vec<(String, String)>, also_integration: bool }`, `VerifyDone { .., spill_base: Option<String>, signals: Option<SignalsSpec> }` (`SignalsSpec { test_paths, skip_markers }`, `None` for an untiered profile or neither key set). `OpResult` appends `Tier(Box<TierOutcome>)`, `TestAt { red: bool, failing: Vec<String>, tail: String, show: Option<String> }` (`show`: `git show --stat` of the probed commit when it is a merge), `StageCreated`, and extends `Merged { .., tier: Option<Box<TierOutcome>> }`, `CandidateRed { .., tier: Option<Box<TierOutcome>> }`, `Conflict { .., tree: Option<String> }`, `DoneChecked { .., signals: Vec<Signal> }`. The payload structs live in `run/tiers/mod.rs` and `model_stages.rs`, so `engine/ops.rs` (511) grows only by the variants.

                             spec: &TierSpec) -> OpResult;       // acquire per step, blocking per step
pub(crate) async fn run_test_at(ctx: &OpCtx, sched: &TestScheduler, queue: &GitQueue, git: &OsStr, spec: &TestAtSpec) -> OpResult;
// driver/graph.rs
pub(crate) fn module_graph(git: &OsStr, dir: &Path, repo_dir: &Path, tiers: &TierProfile, modules: &[String],
                           confine: Option<&ConfineSpec>) -> GraphState;           // blocking thread only
pub(crate) fn toolchain(dir: &Path, command: &str, confine: Option<&ConfineSpec>) -> String;   // blocking thread only
// git/tiers.rs
pub fn changed_paths(git: &OsStr, dir: &Path, range: &str, timeout: Duration) -> Result<Vec<String>, String>;
pub fn tree_of(git: &OsStr, dir: &Path, commit: &str, timeout: Duration) -> Result<String, String>;

pub fn cfg_test_files(git: &OsStr, dir: &Path, head: &str, files: &[String], timeout: Duration) -> Result<Vec<String>, String>;

// git/refs_tx.rs

// git/merge.rs changes: merge_tree returns the tree on a conflict too (CandidateStep::Conflict { files, tree });
// guard_refs takes guarded: &[(String, String)] in place of (run_branch, run_head).
// exec.rs: run_matching (and run_shell, run_confined, confine::confined) gain `extra: &[(String, String)]`.
```

Every `git` call above goes through M8a's `run_git` helpers (`--no-optional-locks`, scrubbed environment, AGENTS.md rule 11), and every write (`CreateStageBranch`, the propagate's `commit_tree`, `materialize`, `refs_tx::cas`, `reattach`) through `GitQueue::write` keyed by the run's project, as M8a's candidate does (`engine/ops.rs:145-157`).

### Prompts (exact)

The texts live in a new `run/contract_tiers.rs` (pure), so `run/contract.rs` (571) gains only the calls.

**Tier 0, in `worker_head` after `Check command:` (tiered profiles only; decision 13).** `<command>` is `module_test` with `{filter:…}` expanded to the gate filter and `{module}` kept; without `module_test`, `module_tests` with `{modules…}` expanded to the task's modules. The `Your modules` line is left out when no `owns` glob maps to a module.

```
Module test command: <command>
Your modules: <names joined ", "> (put one in place of {module})
While you work, run your own test and these module tests only; after task_done the engine runs the wider suites.
```

**Bisect fix task** (decision 37). Title: `Fix <first failing test> after <culprit>` (`… and <k> more` inserted after the test when there are more), cut to 120 characters. Acceptance: one item `<test> passes` per failing test (at most 10; with more, the single item `every failing test listed in the brief passes`), then `no test is weakened, skipped or deleted`. Brief:

```
[anthrex] Fix task <id>: the full test suite of stage <n> fails, and bisecting the stage's merges found that it started failing with the merge of task <culprit> ("<culprit title>").
Failing tests: <names joined ", ">
Make these tests pass without weakening them. <culprit>'s work stays merged; fix it here.

Summary of the failure:
<the ≤ 40-line summary>

The merge that introduced it:
<git show --stat --format=%h%x20%s of the culprit's merge>

The brief of <culprit>:
<culprit brief>
```

**Sync fix task** (decision 51). Title: `Resolve the merge of stage <k> into stage <k+1>`. Acceptance: `no conflict markers remain`, `both stages' changes are kept`. Brief:

```
[anthrex] Fix task <id>: merging stage <k> into stage <k+1> conflicted. Your worktree already holds that merge, with conflict markers in:
- <file>
Resolve every conflict so that both stages' work is kept, commit, and call task_done. Change nothing else.
```

**Deleted test file** (`deleted_test_file_message(paths, start)`, decision 41), one `deleted test file` line per path:

```
[anthrex] task_done rejected:
deleted test file <path>; restore it or own it exactly
Restore it (git checkout <start7> -- <path>, then commit) and call task_done again. If this task must delete it, call task_blocked with kind question and ask for the plan to be amended.
```

**Reviewer block** (decision 42), after the check block of `reviewer_prompt`, one line per signal:

```
Test changes to justify:
- W1 <path>: deleted test file
- W2 <path>:<line>: added skip marker <marker>
- W3 <path>:<line>: <removed> assertion lines removed, <added> added
Answer each with a finding whose text starts with its id: minor, as "W1 accepted: <why the change is right>", or critical when it weakens a test.
```

A capped list ends `- … and <n> more (see git diff)`. The refusal of an incomplete `submit_review`: `the review must address <ids joined ", ">: add a finding whose text starts with each id`. The engine's finding after a second incomplete one: `<id> (<path>:<line>) was not justified by the review` (`important`, `file` and `line` set). The interface line (decision 54): `Check that the old interface still works: callers outside this task must still build.`

**Log, attention and wake texts** (decisions 9, 19, 21, 33, 35–38, 51, 52):

| Where | Text |
|-------|------|
| run log, report | `module graph unknown: <reason>; every tier runs check` |
| task or run log | `tier <n>: <k> modules (<first 5 names>)`, `tier <n>: build only (no module affected)`, `tier <n>: full suite (<reason>)`, `tier <n>: cached (<k> steps)`, `tier <n>: flaky <name> passed on retry` |
| run log | `stage <n>: tier 3 red (<first 3 tests>); bisecting <m> merges` |
| attention | `tier 3 red, no single culprit: <first 5 tests> (stage <n>: <reason>)` |
| wake | `stage <n> tier 3 red, no single culprit: <first 3 tests>; plan a fix` |
| wake | `stage <n> tier 3 red (<first 3 tests>): bisected to <culprit>; added fix task <id>` |
| attention and wake | `stage <n>: tier 3 still red after <k> fix tasks: <first 5 tests>; fix it with a task, or end the run with the finish edit` |
| attention | `propagate of stage <k> into stage <k+1> is red: <first 5 tests>` |
| wake | `propagate of stage <k> into stage <k+1> is red: <first 3 tests>; plan a fix in stage <k> or <k+1>` |
| wake | `propagate of stage <k> into stage <k+1> conflicted: added fix task <id>` |
| attention after `finish` | `tier 3 red on stage <n>: <first 5 tests>` |

Decision 38's reasons: `the failing tests already fail at <G7>`, `the failing tests pass alone at <H7>; they fail only with the whole suite`, `the first red merge is the propagate of stage <k>`, `no failing test names to bisect with`, `the profile has no single_test to bisect with`, `fix task refused: <message>`.

### Contracts (exact)

`ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT` (`run/orch/contract.rs:26-83`). Rule 12 becomes:

```
12. Split interfaces first, and keep them additive: an interface or hub change is its own task, first, and every task that uses it depends on it. The interface task adds the new form beside the old one, dependent tasks migrate the callers, and a later task, usually in a later stage, removes the old form. Split one level only; a piece that is still L goes back to whoever planned it, never deeper.
```

and a new section is appended after rule 33 (rules 1–33 keep their numbers):

```
Stages and testing
34. Most goals need one stage. When the work is large enough to review in parts, group tasks into stages with the stage field. A stage is a unit a person can review and the full test suite can judge on its own: it must leave the code building and its tests passing without any later stage. Aim for 300 to 800 changed lines per stage; on the large path, one epic is usually one stage. A task depends only on tasks in its own or an earlier stage. The stages are fixed when the plan is approved: a plan approved with one stage keeps one.
35. When an interface change cannot be additive, such as a protocol version bump that must update every client together, make it one task with atomic set to true and a one-line atomic_reason. It is a hub task: it runs alone and is tdd. At most one atomic task per stage.
36. The engine adds fix tasks itself, with ids fix1, fix2 and so on: when the full test suite of a stage fails and a bisect finds the merge that broke it, and when merging one stage into the next conflicts. They are ordinary tasks. Do not cancel one unless the plan no longer needs it.
37. When the full test suite of a stage fails and no single merge is to blame, or merging one stage into the next fails its tests, you are woken: plan a fix task in that stage, or tell the user and end the run with a finish edit if it cannot be fixed.
```

`PLANNER_CONTRACT` (`:86-96`) appends:

```
11. Set stage on every task: the stage of the interface tasks your epic depends on, or a later one. A task depends only on tasks in its own or an earlier stage, and each stage must build and pass its tests without the later ones.
12. Keep interface changes additive: add the new form beside the old one and migrate callers in dependent tasks; never remove an old form that a task outside your epic still uses.
```

`ONBOARDING_CONTRACT` (`scout/contract.rs:13-18`) gains a rule after its rule 3, and its rules 4 and 5 become 5 and 6:

```
4. Also find, when the test runner allows it: a command that builds and lints everything without running tests (build_check); a command that runs one module's tests, with {module} where the module's name goes and {filter:<flag> %} where a test filter expression goes (module_test), or several modules' at once with {modules:<flag> %} (module_tests); how modules depend on each other (module_graph: cargo for a Cargo workspace, otherwise a command that prints JSON mapping each module to the modules it depends on, or none); paths whose change must run every test, such as root manifests, lock files, CI configuration, toolchain files and build scripts (full_triggers); a filter expression that selects slow tests, such as end-to-end and terminal tests (slow_tests), and one for timing-sensitive tests (timing_tests); the markers that skip or disable a test in this repository's languages (skip_markers); the globs of test files (test_paths); into how many parts the check can be split with {shard} and {shards} (full_shards); and a command that prints the toolchain's version (toolchain_id). Propose cargo nextest commands only if cargo nextest --version succeeds here; nothing requires it.
```

`WORKER_CONTRACT` and `REVIEWER_CONTRACT` (`run/contract.rs:19-40`) are unchanged.

|-------|------|

### CLI

No new subcommand. `anthrex run status <run>` prints, for a `Multi` run, after the header and before the tasks:

```
stage 1/3  anthrex/<run>/stage-1  5/5 merged  tier 3 green (41m 12s)
stage 2/3  anthrex/<run>/stage-2  2/4 merged  tier 3 bisecting (fix1)
stage 3/3  not created
```

and each task line gains ` [stage <n>]` for a `Multi` run and ` (fix: <fixes>)` for a fix task. `--json` is the snapshot. `anthrex run stats` prints, after M8b's table (`stats.rs:170`):

```
Flaky tests (at least <n> runs in the last <d> days):
proposal: add <test> to slow_tests (flaky in <runs> runs in the last <d> days)
  fix: anthrex run start --goal "Make the test <test> deterministic; it failed and then passed on retry in <runs> runs"
```

or nothing when there is no proposal. `--json` carries `flaky_proposals`.

### The timing-budgets rule (`docs/timing-budgets.md`, standing rule 5)

```
5. **Load scaling.** A command anthrex's test scheduler runs gets `ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD`: the share of the machine's test slots in use when it started, one decimal place, from 0.0 to 1.0. A wall-clock bound may be multiplied by `1 + ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD` (read once at the start of the test; absent means 0), which at most doubles it. Scaling adds margin on a loaded machine; it never replaces rule 1: the unscaled bound must already exceed the code's own legal worst case.
```

## Tasks

Do them in this order: M9.1.1 to M9.1.22. Each task's tests are written first and must fail before its change (AGENTS.md rule 6), except those marked **pinning**, which pin behaviour that is already true and may pass at once. One commit per task.

**Shared test helpers**, added in the task that first needs them:

- **A tiered test repository** (`crates/cli/tests/support/run_tiers.rs`, new, M9.1.21): `tier_repo_files() -> Vec<(&str, &str)>` — three modules `mods/a`, `mods/b`, `mods/c` (`c` depends on `b`, `b` on `a`), a `docs/` directory, and shell scripts `graph.sh` (prints `{"a":[],"b":["a"],"c":["b"]}`), `build.sh`, `test.sh <module> [--filter <expr>]`, `check.sh [--filter <expr>] [--shard <k>/<n>]`. Every script appends one JSON line per run to `$TIER_LOG` — `{script, args, cwd, start, end, env: {CARGO_BUILD_JOBS, NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS, RUST_TEST_THREADS, ANTHREX_TEST_SLOTS, ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD, TMPDIR, ANTHREX_SOCKET, ANTHREX_DATA_DIR}}` — where `TIER_LOG` is `<RunHarness::cache_dir()>/tier-log.jsonl` (`run_harness.rs:214`), the one directory outside the checkout a confined command may write (F1c round 3). A test makes a script fail or flake with marker files the worker's scripted commits add (`mods/b/FAIL`, `mods/b/FLAKY` read with a counter file in `$TIER_LOG`'s directory), so no test depends on timing. `tier_profile(extra: &str) -> String` is the plan's `[profile]` block: `modules = ["mods/*"]`, `module_graph = "sh graph.sh"`, `module_names = "dir"`, `build_check = "sh build.sh"`, `module_test = "sh test.sh {module} {filter:--filter %}"`, `check = "sh check.sh {filter:--filter %}"`, `single_test = "sh test.sh --one {test}"`, `test_passed = "PASS {test}"`, `source = ["mods/**"]`, plus `extra`. `tier_log(h) -> Vec<Value>` reads the log. `RunHarness::with_config(orchestrator, extra_toml, files)` (in `run_harness.rs`, +10 lines) writes `extra_toml` (for example `[testing]`) after the harness's own tables.
- **`TIER_WAIT`** = `RUN_WAIT` (a tiered run's longest path is M8a's plus at most one tier-3 job and one bisect of three probes, each bounded by the harness's `check_timeout_secs = 10`; M9.1.21 adds the derivation row to `docs/timing-budgets.md`).
- **Linux CI** (clippy 1.98 on macOS and Linux): every new end-to-end test runs with the harness's `unconfined_checks = true` off macOS (`run_harness.rs:151-156`), every shell script reads `$1` defensively under dash, every repository sets its own `user.name`/`user.email`, and scripts are written before the daemon starts (ETXTBSY). Check with `cargo +1.98 clippy` for the host and for `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` before pushing.
- **Tests never reach real agents**: every fixture pins `ANTHREX_CLAUDE_BIN`, `ANTHREX_CODEX_BIN` and the decider binary to `fake-agent` or a nonexistent path; nothing changes program selection. No test signals a process it did not start; every daemon a test starts is stopped by its harness.

### M9.1.1 Verify facts, names and counts

**Files.** `docs/milestones/M9.1-tiered-testing.md` ("Implementation notes" only). No code. Run everything in a scratch repository under `/tmp`, never through a live `anthrex` (AGENTS.md rule 1).

**Checks.** Record the command, the tool version and the observed output (trimmed) for each:

1. **`PROTO_VERSION` on `main`** is 10 (`crates/proto/src/lib.rs:50`). If not, stop (header).
2. **Names.** Every row of "Names taken from earlier briefs" exists as cited; record each difference and the name used instead.
3. **Counts.** `wc -l` of every file in "File sizes this milestone must respect".
4. **`git merge-tree --write-tree` on a conflict** prints the conflicted tree's id on its first line and exits 1, with `--name-only --no-messages -z` as `git/merge.rs:57-94` calls it (decisions 51, 53). Record the git version (M8a requires 2.38 or later).
5. **`git update-ref --no-deref --stdin`** with `start`, two `update` lines, `prepare`, `commit` is all-or-nothing when one old value is wrong (decision 53).
6. **`cargo metadata --format-version 1 --no-deps --offline`** in a two-crate workspace with a path dependency and a dev-dependency: `workspace_members`, `packages[].manifest_path`, `packages[].dependencies[].{name, path, kind, rename}` are as decision 9 reads them; it needs no network and writes nothing but `Cargo.lock` when absent (record whether it writes it; if it does, the graph step runs after `materialize` in a scratch checkout, which `clean -fd` resets).
7. **Unix sockets under a per-step directory**: a confined command (`sandbox-exec` with M8a's profile for a scratch checkout) binds and connects a Unix socket at `<task tmp>/s1-1/d.sock` (decision 28). macOS only; record it.
8. **Failing-test output shapes** of libtest, `cargo nextest` (when installed; never required), pytest and `go test` for one failing and one passing test, so decision 32's patterns are written against real output. Record the versions; commit the four outputs as fixtures in M9.1.6.
9. **The regression set** (decision 6, controller ruling 1): no test in the M8a or M8b end-to-end files of "Starting point" uses `interface_change = true` (decision 54's reviewer line), and none asserts the environment a check sees beyond what decisions 26 and 28 add (record the grep).

**Acceptance.** Each check has a recorded result. No file other than this brief changed.

**Commit.** `docs: record the facts, names and counts milestone 9.1 builds on`

### M9.1.6 The pure tier logic

**Files.** Create `crates/daemon/src/run/tiers/{affected.rs, graph.rs, command.rs, steps.rs, failing.rs, weakening.rs, cache_key.rs}` and their `*_tests.rs`, and `crates/daemon/src/run/tiers/fixtures/` (M9.1.1 check 8's outputs; a `cargo metadata` output of a four-crate workspace shaped like anthrex's).

**Tests first.** All pure; no I/O.
- `affected_leaf_crate_is_itself`, `affected_crate_with_dependents_includes_them_transitively`, `affected_hub_file_is_full`, `affected_full_trigger_is_full`, `affected_unowned_path_is_full`, `affected_docs_only_change_is_empty_and_plans_build_only`, `affected_every_module_is_full`, `unknown_graph_is_full_and_plans_check`, `empty_source_never_ignores_a_path`, `first_rule_wins_and_names_the_first_path`.
- `cargo_metadata_keeps_workspace_members_and_every_dependency_kind` (the fixture: `anthrex` depends on every crate but `anthrex-fake-agent`; `anthrex-fake-agent`'s dev-dependency on the daemon is an edge), `command_graph_with_an_unknown_dep_is_unknown`, `duplicate_module_names_are_unknown`.
- `placeholders_substitute_and_quote` (every row of decision 7's table; `%%`; `${VAR}` untouched; a name with a quote in it), `filter_is_empty_without_slow_or_timing`, `filter_expressions_per_scope_are_exact` (decision 25's four).
- `gate_plan_for_modules_is_build_tests_timing`, `gate_plan_prefers_module_tests_over_module_test`, `full_plan_shards_and_timing`, `no_build_check_plans_check_alone`, `check_without_filter_is_exclusive_when_timing_is_set`.
- `failing_names_from_libtest_nextest_pytest_and_go` (the fixtures), `failing_names_are_deduplicated_and_capped`.

### M9.1.8 The test scheduler, worker caps and the timing-budgets rule

**Files.** Create `crates/daemon/src/run/slots/{mod.rs, book.rs, book_tests.rs, mod_tests.rs}`. Modify `crates/daemon/src/run/mod.rs` (`pub mod slots;`), `crates/daemon/src/run/driver.rs` (`RunService` holds one `Arc<TestScheduler>`), `crates/daemon/src/lifecycle.rs` (built from `[testing] test_slots` or `default_slots()`), `crates/daemon/src/run/role_launch.rs` (one call: `worker_caps` appended to the worker's environment), `docs/timing-budgets.md` (standing rule 5).

**Tests first.**
- `book_grants_by_priority_then_age` (pure): with 4 slots, a `FullIdle` asked first and a `Candidate` asked second, the `Candidate` is granted first; equal priorities are granted in ask order.
- `exclusive_step_waits_for_every_slot_and_blocks_later_ones` (pure): with 4 slots, 2 held, an `Exclusive` ask is not granted; a later `One` ask is not granted either (strict ordering, decision 25); releasing both grants the exclusive with all 4; releasing it grants the `One`.
- `half_and_one_round_as_decided`: `Half` of 1, 3, 4, 7 slots is 1, 2, 2, 4; `One` is 1.
- `grant_env_carries_the_slot_variables_and_load`: `SlotGrant::env()` names exactly `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, `NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS`, `ANTHREX_TEST_SLOTS` equal to the granted slots and `ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD` equal to `SlotBook::load()` at grant time (for example `0.5` with 2 of 4 held).
- `worker_env_carries_the_caps`: `worker_caps(8, 3)` is the four slot variables at `2` (`max(1, 8/3)`); `worker_caps(2, 4)` at `1`; and a worker launched by `role_launch` (M8a's pure launch-spec builder, `role_launch.rs`) carries them, while a planner, reviewer, decider or orchestrator launch does not.
- `acquire_waits_without_blocking_the_runtime` (tokio, `current_thread`): two `acquire`s on a 1-slot scheduler; the second resolves after the first grant drops; a concurrent `tokio::time::timeout(… , async {})` completes at once (the runtime is not blocked). Deadline loop, no fixed sleep.
- `default_slots_is_cores_minus_two_at_least_one` (with `available_parallelism` injected).

**Acceptance.** `SlotBook` is pure (`grep -n "tokio\|std::sync\|std::time" crates/daemon/src/run/slots/book.rs` prints nothing). `TestScheduler`'s mutex is taken with `daemon::lock` (AGENTS.md rule 3) and never held across an `.await`. Standing rule 5 in `docs/timing-budgets.md` states: a test that asserts a wall-clock bound on work that goes through the scheduler derives its bound with the scheduler's wait counted as zero only when the test's own daemon holds no other slot, and names that in its row. The five AGENTS.md commands pass.

**Commit.** `feat(daemon): add the test scheduler and the worker slot caps`

## Verification

Run on the branch after M9.1.22, in this order:

```bash
cargo build --workspace --all-targets
cargo test --workspace
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo +1.98 clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo +1.98 clippy --workspace --all-targets --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu -- -D warnings
cargo fmt --all --check
python3 scripts/pty-smoke.py
```

Then these checks, each printing nothing unless noted:

```bash
# the engine, the pure tier logic and the slot book do no I/O
grep -rnE "std::process|std::fs|tokio::|std::thread" crates/daemon/src/run/engine crates/daemon/src/run/tiers crates/daemon/src/run/slots/book.rs
# the client's state stays pure (AGENTS.md rule 5)
grep -rnE "std::fs|std::process|tokio::" crates/tui/src/app.rs crates/tui/src/app crates/tui/src/ui
# no .lock().unwrap() (AGENTS.md rule 3)
grep -rn "\.lock()\.unwrap()" crates
# every new git call is --no-optional-locks with a scrubbed environment (AGENTS.md rule 11): prints only lines through run_git
grep -n "Command::new" crates/daemon/src/run/git/tiers.rs crates/daemon/src/run/git/refs_tx.rs
# the M9 refusal is gone
grep -rn "stage recipients arrive with milestone 9.1" crates
# M8a's and M8b's end-to-end files are unedited (decision 6, controller ruling 1): prints nothing
git diff --stat main -- crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_{basic,gates,review,merge,safety,sessions,crash,finish,base,settings,confine,unconfined,worker_pins,codex_config,early_events,adapt,adapt_engine,adapt_profile}.rs
# file budgets (AGENTS.md rule 8): every line at or below 600
git diff --name-only main | grep '\.rs$' | xargs wc -l | sort -n | tail -15
# Linux-only failure modes: run the new e2e files once with git's global config out of the way and dash as sh (in a Linux container or CI)
GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null cargo test -p anthrex --test run_e2e_tiers --test run_e2e_tiers_signals --test run_e2e_stages --test run_e2e_bisect
# nothing of ours is left running (AGENTS.md rule 1)
pgrep -fl "anthrex daemon"
```

Per the test scope policy, rounds run only the touched tests; the full suite and the smoke run happen before the merge to `main`.

## Manual check

With `ANTHREX_SOCKET=/tmp/ax91/d.sock` and `ANTHREX_DATA_DIR=/tmp/ax91/data` set for every command, never without them (AGENTS.md rule 1):

1. Make a scratch cargo workspace under `/tmp/ax91/repo` with three crates, `c` depending on `b` depending on `a`, one test each, and a commit.
2. `anthrex profile edit` in it: set `build_check = "cargo build --workspace --all-targets"`, `module_test = "cargo test -p {module} {filter:-- --skip %}"`, `module_names = "cargo"`, `source = ["crates/**"]`, `hub = ["Cargo.toml"]`. `anthrex profile show` lists them and the summary has the tiers line.
3. `anthrex run start --plan` with a two-stage plan (stage 1: a change in `a`; stage 2: a change in `c`). In `C-b T`, watch: stage 1's tier-1 log line names `a b c`; stage 2's names `c`; both stage branches exist; `integration` equals stage 2's head.
4. Add a test to crate `b` that fails one time in two (a counter file in `/tmp/ax91`). Watch it pass on retry, the `flaky` line in the task log, no bounce.
5. Make the stage-1 task's change break a test only the full suite runs (a `slow_tests` test). At completion, tier 3 is red; watch the probes and `fix1` appear with the culprit's `owns`.
6. `anthrex run stats` in the repository after three such runs shows the quarantine proposal.
7. `anthrex daemon stop` with the same variables; `pgrep -fl "anthrex daemon"` shows nothing of yours.

## Review focus

- **Nothing blocking under the lock or on a worker thread** (AGENTS.md rules 2, 10, 11): graph, toolchain, cache file, step directories, every git call; each on `spawn_blocking` with a timeout. `TestScheduler`'s mutex never held across an `.await`.
- **Decision 6 holds**: an untiered profile's op sequence is M8a's; M8a's and M8b's end-to-end files unedited and passing; only the resource controls differ (controller ruling 1).
- **Every head read goes through `stage_head`** and every write through `set_stage_head` (decision 47's site table). A missed site is a run that merges onto the wrong stage.
- **Ref writes are all-or-nothing** (`refs_tx::cas`) and journaled; reconcile rows cover a crash between the two refs.
- **The cache stores only green** and keys on scope, not tier; a flaky-green is stored with its note.
- **Engine-made tasks go through `fixes::add_fix` only**, pass the same validation as planned tasks, and use reserved ids.
- **Exact texts** match "Prompts (exact)", "Contracts (exact)" and "Error texts (exact)" byte for byte.
- **Tests**: constructed inputs for every decision (memory: defects come from constructed inputs, not reading); fixtures pin every agent binary to `fake-agent`; no kill by name.

## Risks and gotchas

- **A tier command that starts a daemon outlives its step.** Isolation puts its socket under the step's `TMPDIR`, but a daemon it spawns is detached. The executor kills the step's process group on timeout; a daemon that called `setsid` escapes it. The step directory's removal then fails with the socket in use: log `step <k>: its directory is still in use; left for cleanup` and leave it (the TMPDIR follow-up). Never kill by name.
- **Cache staleness.** A test that reads anything outside the tree (network, a system library, a file under `$HOME`) can be green in the cache and red now. The key's toolchain id and profile hash cover the common cases; the rest is documented in the report's tiers section (`cached results assume tests read only the checkout`).
- **FullIdle starvation.** Strict ordering (decision 25) with a steady stream of candidates starves tier 3 at idle; completion's tier 3 is `FullStage` and is not starved by `FullIdle`. Acceptable: idle tier 3 is an optimisation.
- **Bisect assumes one red boundary on the first-parent line.** A test that is red, green, red again finds the first boundary; the fix task's brief names the tests, so the worker sees the real failure.
- **The `run_head` refactor touches many sites.** Do it in one task (M9.1.12) with the grep in its acceptance, before any feature relies on stages.
- **Nextest filter syntax** (`not`, `and`, parentheses, `test(=name)`) is assumed from nextest's documentation; M9.1.1 records the installed version's behaviour when nextest is present, and a profile without nextest never gets a filter expression it cannot parse (the `{filter:…}` placeholder is empty without `slow_tests`/`timing_tests`).
- **Linux**: dash as `sh`, no seatbelt (`unconfined_checks = true` in the harness), git without a global identity, ETXTBSY on freshly written scripts: the shared helper handles all four.
- **Merge-tree's conflicted tree** is a git ≥ 2.38 behaviour already required by M8a; M9.1.1 check 4 confirms it.

- No item in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-17-anthrex-foundation-followups.md` is assigned to 9.1. Two are related and stay open: the empty-TMPDIR pile-up (`:1519-1523`), which per-step directories would make worse without the removal in decision 28; and the ignored-test note (`:193-200`), which tier 3's `check` does not change.

New follow-ups M9.1.22 adds to that file, under 9.5: **stray step directories** left by a step whose daemon escaped the process group (swept at daemon start, older than a day); **an `--ignored` job** (a tier that runs ignored tests, which no profile key names yet).

## Spec and brief defects

Found while writing this brief; each is resolved by the decision named.

1. PR #18 C4: tier 1's range was `base...head`; it is the stage head (decision 60, decision 15).
2. PR #18 C6: TT §3.5's `TMPDIR` isolation assumed confinement; unconfined Linux commands inherited the daemon's `ANTHREX_SOCKET` (decision 62, decision 28).
3. TT §3.3 keys the cache on the tier; tiers 1 and 2 then never share a result for the same tree. Keyed on scope (decision 30).
4. PR #18 C7 (decision 63).
5. PR #18 C8 (decision 64).
6. PR #18 C10: `stage-<n>` and `fix<n>` ids were not reserved (decision 65, decision 39).
7. TT §3.1 has no placeholder for the slow-test filter; `{filter:<t>}` added (decision 7).
8. TT §3.3's key names the toolchain but no key produces it; `toolchain_id` added (decisions 5, 11).
9. TT §3.6 tags history lines `kind`; M8b's tag is `type` (decision 57).
10. TT gives `sync` fix tasks to 9.2 but propagate conflicts to 9.1; propagate's sync task is 9.1, base sync stays 9.2 (decision 51, Scope).
11. PR #18 C5: a propagate by `git merge` in a worktree; done with merge-tree and commit-tree like a candidate (decision 61, decision 50).
12. Adaptive spec §6.9 lists plan-task fields without `stage`/`atomic` (decision 43).
13. TT §7's stage node versus TT §4.1's plan-gate groups: one stage node, used by both (decision 55).
14. Bisect over a first-parent line that contains propagate merges: a propagate culprit is "no culprit" (decisions 36, 38).
15. TT §3.2 tier-3 trigger (a) exists only with PR delivery; the entry `full::request(.., FullWhy::Deliver)` is built, the caller is 9.2 (decision 17).
16. TT §3.4's docs exception with an empty `source` would skip everything; an empty `source` never ignores a path (decision 22).
17. `stage_target_lines` belongs under `[delivery]` (9.2); the planner rule states the range in words (Scope, contract rule 34).
18. TT §3.8's "review incomplete" had no mechanism; one refusal, then engine findings (decision 42).
19. TT §3.4 has no degradation for a profile without `check` and a full affected set; it runs every module test (decision 20).
20. TT §3.6's "offered fix task" for a quarantine was undefined; a printed `run start --goal` command (decision 34).
21. TT §3.2 does not size the proof or setup step's slots; the proof asks for half, `setup` for one (decision 24).
22. TT §3.6 does not say whether a timed-out step is retried; it is not (decision 33).

Names 9.2 relies on; each is `pub(crate)` or wider (or `pub(super)` in `run::engine`, which 9.2's `run::engine::delivery::*` can see) and has a test in this milestone. 9.2's brief was reconciled against this list on 2026-09-29; the names are exact.

- `proto::TaskOrigin` (with `Ci` and `Review` already present, so 9.2 adds no variant), `proto::StageInfo` (9.2 adds `pr`), `proto::FullState`, `proto::FullInfo`, `TaskInfo.{origin, stage, fixes}` (9.2 adds new `fixes` texts, no field), `RunInfo.stages`.
- `run::model_stages::{FixOf, StageRecord, StageMerge, StageFull, SyncState, StageLayout, BisectRecord}` (9.2 appends `FixOf::{Ci, Review, Base}` and `BisectRecord.ci`); `Run::{stages, stage_layout, stage, stage_head, stage_branch, full_path, propagate_due, fix_seq, full_op}`; `Task::{origin, fixes, sync}`.
- `run::engine::fixes::{add_fix, FixSpec, next_fix_id}` (`add_fix` returns `Err(<rule message>)` when M8a's rules refuse the task; the caller chooses a fallback route); the reserved `^fix[0-9]+$` and `^stage-[0-9]+$` ids.
- `run::engine::full::{request, completion, FullWhy::{Idle, Completion, Deliver}}` (`request` emits nothing for an untiered profile, decision 17); `slots::Priority::{Candidate, Gate, FullStage, FullIdle, Verify}`.
- `run::engine::bisect::start(run, stage, head, tests, now, fx)` and its ends (decisions 36–38), which 9.2 routes to its CI handler through `BisectRecord.ci`.
- `run::engine::stages::{set_stage_head, ready_in_stage}`; `run::engine::propagate::{start, done}` (9.2 adds the base-sync item and the `from: 0` arm); `merge::start_merge`'s propagate-first order (decision 49).
- `OpKind::{Tier, TestAt(Box<TestAtSpec>), CreateStageBranch, Propagate(Box<PropagateSpec>)}` with `PropagateSpec.from = 0` reserved for 9.2's base sync (decision 50); `OpResult::{TestAt { red, failing, tail, show }, Conflict { files, tree }}`; `run::git::refs_tx::cas`; the guard list (`MergeCandidate.guarded`).
- The sync task's mechanics (`HandBack` before the first session, `VerifyDone.spill_base`), which 9.2's base-sync reuses.
- `run::tiers::failing::names` (to read CI failure logs), `run::tiers::steps::plan`, `run::test_cache::TestCache`.

## Implementation notes

### Task M9.1.1

Run on 2026-09-29, branch `m9.1-tiered-testing` at `80b7460` (`origin/main` `566a653` plus the ROADMAP commit). Tools: macOS 26.2 (25C56), `git version 2.50.1 (Apple Git-155)`, `cargo 1.92.0 (344c4567c 2025-10-21)`, `rustc 1.92.0`, pytest 8.3.4 on Python 3.13.5. Every experiment ran in scratch repositories under `/tmp/ax-m911/` with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and the rule-11 variables unset; no `anthrex` binary or daemon was started; `/tmp/ax-m911/` was removed at the end.

**Contradictions with the brief found here (decisions unchanged, corrections proposed; the controller rules):**

- **C-1, decision 6's "unedited" regression set cannot compile unchanged.** Two files of the set name types this milestone extends, exhaustively:
  - `crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_merge.rs:115-135` (`fn half`) builds a full `PlanTask { .. }` literal (no `..`; `PlanTask` derives no `Default`, `proto/src/run.rs:164`). M9.1.3 adds `stage`, `atomic`, `atomic_reason`, so this literal stops compiling.
  - `crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_adapt_profile.rs:248-252` matches `HistoryLine` exhaustively (`Task`, `Run`, `Revert | RoleRoute`); `HistoryLine` is not `#[non_exhaustive]` (`proto/src/history.rs:22-30`). M9.1.3 appends `Tier`, `Flaky`, `Bisect`, so this match stops compiling.

  So `git diff --stat main -- <those files>` cannot be empty. **Proposed correction:** allow exactly these two compile-forced edits (the three new fields at their defaults `stage: 1, atomic: false, atomic_reason: None` in `half`; `| HistoryLine::Tier(_) | HistoryLine::Flaky(_) | HistoryLine::Bisect(_)` added to the ignore arm), made in M9.1.3 with the other forced literal updates, and restate decision 6's acceptance as "no assertion, scenario, plan text or script in those files changes; the diff is the two compile-forced lines, listed in M9.1.3's commit message". Keeping the files byte-identical is not possible: a `Default` for `PlanTask` does not help a literal without `..`, and `#[non_exhaustive]` on `HistoryLine` would break the existing match too.
- **C-2, check 8: `cargo nextest` and `go` are not installed** on this machine (`cargo nextest --version` → `error: no such command: nextest`; `go` → `command not found`). Only the libtest and pytest outputs are real. **Proposed correction for M9.1.6:** write the nextest and Go fixtures by hand from their documented formats (`FAIL [ 0.004s] <binary> <name>`, `TIMEOUT [ … ]`; `--- FAIL: <Name> (0.00s)`), with a first-line comment saying they are hand-written, and replace them with captured output when a machine with the tools runs them. Nothing was installed.

**4. `git merge-tree --write-tree` on a conflict** (git 2.50.1 ≥ 2.38). A repository with `f.txt` changed on both `left` and `right`:

```
$ git --no-optional-locks merge-tree --write-tree --name-only --no-messages -z left right   # NULs shown as |
7e3b62e0107d3879ce4f163e9e07fe0a3237ac76|f.txt|         exit=1
$ git cat-file -t 7e3b62e…   → tree   (its f.txt holds the <<<<<<< left / >>>>>>> right markers; g.txt is right's)
clean merge (left, clean):  381983993d43b45c22aa776589d4a63637f8b523|   exit=0
bad name (left, nosuch):    stdout empty, exit=1, stderr "merge-tree: nosuch - not something we can merge"
```

**Holds** for decisions 51 and 53: the first NUL field is the conflicted tree, exit 1, the names follow; a bad name also exits 1 but with nothing on stdout, which `merge_tree` (`git/merge.rs:57-94`) already tells apart by the tree test.

**5. `git update-ref --no-deref --stdin` transaction.** `stage-1` and `integration` at `C1`; input `start`, `update refs/heads/anthrex/r/stage-1 <new> <old>`, `update refs/heads/anthrex/r/integration <new> <old>`, `prepare`, `commit`:

```
A: integration's old wrong  → "start: ok" / "fatal: prepare: cannot lock ref 'refs/heads/anthrex/r/integration': is at 7b161b8… but expected 0c15721…"  exit=128; both refs still C1
B: stage-1's old wrong      → same for stage-1, exit=128; both refs still C1
C: both right               → "start: ok" "prepare: ok" "commit: ok", exit=0; both refs at C2
D: a symbolic ref updated with --no-deref → replaced by a plain ref, its target untouched
```

**Holds** (decision 53): all or nothing, the refusal names the ref. Note for `refs_tx::cas`: success prints `start: ok`/`prepare: ok`/`commit: ok` on stdout, so success is the exit status, not empty output; a refusal is exit 128.

**6. `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --no-deps --offline`** (cargo 1.92.0) in a workspace `crates/{a,b,c}`: `ax-b` path-depends on `ax-a`; `ax-c` depends on `ax-b` renamed `renamed_b`, and on `ax-a` as a dev- and a build-dependency. Run with `HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY` pointed at a closed port:

```
exit=0; files before/after (find . -type f): identical — no Cargo.lock, no target/
workspace_members: ['path+file:///private/tmp/ax-m911/c6/ws/crates/a#ax-a@0.1.0', …b…, …c…]
package ax-c  manifest_path=/private/tmp/ax-m911/c6/ws/crates/c/Cargo.toml
  dep {name: 'ax-b', path: '/private/tmp/…/crates/b', kind: None,    rename: 'renamed_b'}
  dep {name: 'ax-a', path: '/private/tmp/…/crates/a', kind: 'dev',   rename: None}
  dep {name: 'ax-a', path: '/private/tmp/…/crates/a', kind: 'build', rename: None}
resolve: None
```

A registry dependency absent from every cache, with no `Cargo.lock`, still gives exit 0 with `--no-deps` (without `--no-deps`: exit 101, "no matching package … offline"). **Holds** (decision 9), with three facts for `from_cargo_metadata`: a normal dependency's `kind` is JSON `null`, not `"normal"`; `name` is the package name even when renamed (`rename` is separate), so "a rename changes nothing" is right; `manifest_path`, `path` and `workspace_root` are absolute and canonical (`/private/tmp/…` for a checkout reached as `/tmp/…`), so the module directory must be made relative to the output's own `workspace_root`, never to the checkout path the engine holds. **It writes no `Cargo.lock`**, so the conditional in check 6 does not arise; the graph step still runs after `materialize` as decision 9 says. On anthrex itself (a `git archive` copy under `/tmp`): seven members; `anthrex` (`crates/cli`) depends on every member but `anthrex-fake-agent`; `anthrex-fake-agent` has only dev-dependencies (`config`, `daemon`, `proto`); `anthrex-daemon` dev-depends on `anthrex-mcp`; `anthrex-mcp` names `anthrex-proto` both as normal and dev. That confirms M9.1.6's fixture shape. Both outputs are saved for M9.1.6 as `cargo-metadata-three-crates.json` and `cargo-metadata-anthrex.json`.

**7. A Unix socket under `sandbox-exec` with M8a's confined profile** (macOS only). The profile was built by the daemon's own code: a scratch binary depending on `anthrex-daemon` by path calls `daemon::run::seatbelt::profile(&Grants { writable: [checkout, task tmp, objects], network: false, unix_sockets: [], localhost_ports: [], daemon_socket, checkout: Some(checkout) })`, the grants `ConfineSpec::for_checkout` builds (`confine.rs:139-174`) and `Confinement::profile` passes (`confine.rs:188-199`). The 104-line profile's socket rules come from `seatbelt.rs:246-253` (`allow system-socket AF_UNIX`; `network-bind`/`network-outbound` `unix-socket (subpath …)` per writable path) and `:302-319` (the daemon socket and launchd's denied last). The command ran as `exec.rs:196-209` runs it: `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec -p <profile> /bin/sh -c '{ … } 2>&1'` in the checkout, with `TMPDIR=<task tmp>/s1-1` (made 0700 beforehand, outside the sandbox, as decision 28 says) and `ANTHREX_SOCKET=<TMPDIR>/d.sock`, `ANTHREX_DATA_DIR=<TMPDIR>/data`:

```
bind    <task tmp>/s1-1/d.sock             -> bound (path 59 bytes)
connect <task tmp>/s1-1/d.sock             -> connected, echoed b'ping'
bind    <task tmp>/s1-2/d.sock (dir made inside the sandbox) -> bound
bind    a path outside the writable set    -> PermissionError 1 Operation not permitted
connect the (stand-in) daemon socket       -> PermissionError 1 Operation not permitted
control, unconfined: both of the last two succeed
```

**Holds** (decision 28), and the daemon-socket deny holds with the variables pointed elsewhere. The task tmp here was `/private/tmp/ax-m911/c7/ax-501/<16 hex>` rather than `tmp_root()`'s `/private/tmp/ax-<uid>/<16 hex>`, to stay out of the live tmp root; the rule is a `subpath` of whatever that directory is.

FAILED`, then **two** `failures:` headers: the first is followed by `---- tests::adds_two_and_two stdout ----` blocks (panic text, `thread '…' (201395) panicked at src/lib.rs:12:9:` — rustc 1.92 prints a thread id), the second by the names indented four spaces (`    tests::adds_two_and_two`), then `test result: FAILED. 1 passed; 1 failed; …` and cargo's `error: test failed, to rerun pass `--lib``. ### Task M9.1.2

Five moves, no behaviour change. Line counts before → after (budget): `config/src/lib.rs` 608 → 515 (−80), `run/engine/mod.rs` 600 → 523 (−70), `run/driver/ops.rs` 598 → 487 (−110), `run/orch/contract.rs` 593 → 475 (−100), `run/model.rs` 588 → 520 (−45). New files: `config/src/runtimes.rs` 100, `engine/effect.rs` 85, `driver/gate_ops.rs` 130, `orch/contract_messages.rs` 133, `model_limits.rs` 82. `git diff --color-moved=zebra` shows every moved body as moved; the only changed lines are the new files' headers and `use` lines, the module declarations and re-exports, and the lines listed below.

Deviations:

- **Three of the new files are `#[path]` child modules, not siblings.** `driver/gate_ops.rs` is declared in `driver/ops.rs` (`#[path = "gate_ops.rs"] mod gate_ops;`), `orch/contract_messages.rs` in `orch/contract.rs` (`mod messages`), and `model_limits.rs` in `model.rs` (`mod limits`), the way `model_rounds.rs` and `orch/digest_trim.rs` already are. So `driver.rs` and `orch/mod.rs` (budget +0) are untouched, and the moved code keeps reaching its old module's private helpers without widening them (`setup_output`, `adapt::decider_mode_absent`). `engine/effect.rs` is `mod effect;` in `engine/mod.rs` and `config/src/runtimes.rs` is `mod runtimes;` in `lib.rs`, like `git.rs`.
- **Visibility widened where the move needs it, and nowhere else:** `read_runtimes`, `report_unknown_runtimes` (called from `lib.rs`), `proof` and `check` (called from `ops.rs`) are `pub(super)`.
- **Re-exports:** `engine::Effect` (`pub use effect::Effect`), `model::{ClaudeAuth, RunLimits}`, and every public text of `contract_messages.rs` (`pub use messages::*`), so every existing path stays.
- **`orch/contract.rs` moves three more private helpers** than the brief's `:487-593`: `message_kind_label`, `sender` and `hh_mm`. Only the moved texts use them, and without them the file ends at 500 lines, 7 over its −100 budget.
- **`model.rs` moves `ClaudeAuth` and its two `From` impls** with `RunLimits`: its doc says it exists because `RunLimits` is persisted, and moving `RunLimits` alone leaves `model.rs` at 546, 3 over its −45 budget (the brief's range is 45 lines with its doc and blank line, so the budget left no room for the module declaration).
- **One moved line changed its path:** `RunLimits.orch`'s type, `super::orch::OrchLimits`, is `crate::run::orch::OrchLimits` in `model_limits.rs`, because `super` there is `model`, not `run`.

### Task M9.1.3

`PROTO_VERSION` 10 → 11 and `HISTORY_VERSION` 2 → 3, with every type of Interfaces "proto" (`proto/src/tiers.rs` new; `PlanTask.{stage, atomic, atomic_reason}`, `AmendTask.stage`, the twelve profile keys in `RepoProfile` and `ProfileSpec`, `ModuleNames`, the five `ProfileVerification` checks, `TaskInfo.{stage, origin, fixes, tier, weakening}`, `RunInfo.{stages, test_slots}`, `CheckInfo.tier`, `HistoryLine::{Tier, Flaky, Bisect}`, `TaskRecord.{stage, origin}`, `HistoryStats.{flaky_proposals, window_days, quarantine_after}`, `FlakyProposal`). The regression files take exactly C-1's two compile-forced edits (`run_e2e_merge.rs`'s `half`, three field lines; `run_e2e_adapt_profile.rs`'s ignore arm, three variants).

Deviations and choices:

- **File budgets.** Growth over the counts of M9.1.1: `proto/src/run.rs` +41 (budget +12; the twelve `ProfileSpec` keys are 24 lines with their `#[serde(default)]`s), `profile.rs` +57 (+40), `run_info.rs` +25 (+12), `history.rs` +77 (+50: the three record structs, one field per line as rustfmt writes them), `lib.rs` +10 (+3: the derivation paragraph every bump on this line has). Every file stays far under 600 (531, 268, 397, 363, 147); nothing later in the brief grows these five.
- **Where the profile keys sit.** In `RepoProfile` and `ProfileSpec` the new keys are placed before `env`, the last field, rather than after it, because `env` is a TOML table and a stored `profile.toml` is TOML.
- **`TaskRecord.stage` defaults to 0** (Interfaces: `#[serde(default)]`), so a version-2 line reads as "written before stages"; `TaskInfo.stage` defaults to 1 (Interfaces: "1 when absent").
- **The daemon's minimum arms fill `stage` from the task's spec**, not a constant: `snapshot::task_info` and `history::task_record` write `t.spec.stage` (always 1 until M9.1.11 validates other values) and `origin: Plan`; `tier`, `fixes`, `weakening`, `stages` and `test_slots` stay empty until M9.1.18 and M9.1.20. `history_io::record_id` and `engine::history::append` read the new lines' `record_id` (a real arm, since each carries one); `stats::aggregate` ignores them. `scout/report.rs` and `profile/verify.rs` leave the new keys and checks `None` until M9.1.5.
- **`amend_task` in `edits.rs` destructures with `..`**: until M9.1.11, an amend naming only `stage` is refused as `nothing to amend`, never silently accepted. `edit_log::describe_one` names a move: `amend t4 stage 2` (asserted in `describe_names_every_edit_op`).
- **Fixtures written from M9's types:** `proto/src/m9_run_info.json` is `orchestrator_snapshot_fields_round_trip`'s run, and `proto/src/m9_history_v2.jsonl` is a `task` line (`a_task_record`) and a `role_route` line, each serialized by the pre-change code on this branch (a throw-away test, reverted from a backup) before any type changed.
- **Test placement:** the new tests are in `tiers_tests.rs` (`adapt_tests.rs` is at 581 lines). Its `old_run_info_still_decodes` shares a name with M9's M8c-fixture test in `run_tests_view.rs`, in a different module. M8b's `view_fields::old_run_json_loads` now strips and checks the three new `spec` keys at their defaults, as it does for each earlier addition.

#### Controller rulings on M9.1.3's review (2026-09-29)

- **Brief defect: decision 43's claim that `stage`, `atomic` and `atomic_reason` reach the orchestrator "unchanged" is false.**
  - The raw-argument allow-lists in `crates/daemon/src/run/orch/tools_bounds.rs:29-70` (`EDIT`, `TASK`) refuse any other key as `"<path>: unknown field"`.
  - The closed MCP schemas in `crates/mcp/src/tools_orch.rs:160-210` (`plan_edit`, `plan_task`), pinned by `crates/mcp/src/fixtures/orch_tools.json`, list none of the three.

  **Ruling: M9.1.11 owns the fix.** It adds those three files to its file list and extends both allow-lists, both schemas and the fixture with the three fields and their bounds: `stage` 1–`stages_max`, `atomic` a bool, `atomic_reason` ≤ 300 characters. It also adds a test that sends `stage` and `atomic` through `edit_plan` (`add_task` and `amend_task`) and `submit_epic` and sees them accepted.
- **Interim gap, fixed by M9.1.11: a stage combined with another amend field is dropped silently.**
  - Where: `edits.rs:356-367`, `edit_log.rs:102-107`.
  - Example: `{stage: 2, priority: 5}` on a working task applies the priority, drops the stage, and still logs `amend t1 stage 2`.
  - M9.1.3's note "never silently accepted" holds only for a stage-only amend.
  - M9.1.11's amend-stage tests must cover the combined case: a stage move of a started task is refused as a whole, and the edit log never describes a move that did not happen.

### Task M9.1.4

`[testing]` is `config/src/testing.rs` (169 lines, tests in `testing_tests.rs`): `Testing` with `Default` (none, 120, 14, 3, 14, 2), `read_testing` and `KNOWN_TESTING_KEYS`, wired into `lib.rs` by one field, one call and one `report_unknown_keys` arm (515 → 525, budget +12). The daemon freezes `TestingLimits { full_idle_secs, bisect_fix_max, flaky_quarantine_after, flaky_window_days }` into `RunLimits.testing` (`#[serde(default)]`); `RunContext.testing` defaults to `Testing::default()` in `RunContext::new` and is set by `with_testing` from `lifecycle.rs`, so the ten `RunContext::new` callers are unchanged. A config without `[testing]` loads exactly as before, and an M9 `run.json` loads with the defaults.

Deviations and choices:

- **`BuildContext.testing` is `&'a config::Testing`**, a reference like its `config` field. `BuildContext` has no `..` constructor, so every literal must name it: besides `driver/build.rs` (`&self.ctx.testing`), ten test-side literals gain `testing: &config::Testing::default()` (`run/test_support.rs`, `plan_tests_build.rs`, `triage_tests.rs`, `driver/adapt.rs`'s tests, `profile/tests.rs` ×2, `engine/tests/{fixture,orch}.rs`, `tests/history_io.rs`, `tests/run_journal/fixture.rs`). These are compile-forced, and none of them is in the regression set of decision 6.
- **`run_limits` takes `testing: &config::Testing`** as its second parameter. Its one other caller, `validate_tests.rs`, passes the defaults.
- **`view_fields::old_run_json_loads`** removes `limits.testing` and checks that it holds the defaults, as it already does for each earlier addition.
- **Problem texts.** A wrong-typed value gets the same `must be between A and B` message as an out-of-range one, because M6's integer readers (`read_u64_in_range`) do the same. So does a value too large for the field's type, such as `bisect_fix_max = 266` or `test_slots = 4294967297`. When `test_slots` is absent, its `(using …)` text is `logical cores minus 2, at least 1`. `testing = 5` gives `testing: expected a table (using table of defaults)`, the same as the other tables.
- **Additions the Interfaces entry does not list:**
  - `config` exports the six ranges (`TEST_SLOTS_RANGE`, …, `BISECT_FIX_MAX_RANGE`), as it already does `GIT_*_RANGE`.
  - `TestingLimits` is `Copy` and has struct-level `#[serde(default)]`.
  - Its `Default` is `From<&config::Testing>` applied to `Testing::default()`, so the two sets of defaults cannot drift apart.
- **Red/green.**
  - `testing_defaults` did not compile before this change, because `Config` had no `testing` field.
  - Against a stub that only declared the type and its defaults, `testing_keys_are_read_and_ranged` failed: `[testing]` was reported as an unknown top-level key.
  - `run_limits_freeze_the_testing_limits` did not compile before this change, because there was no `BuildContext.testing`, `RunLimits.testing` or `TestingLimits`.
  - The "a later config change does not move a live run's" part works like this. The test builds a run with a non-default `[testing]`, saves it with `journal::save_run`, and restores it with `journal::load_all`, which reads no config. The restored limits match the ones frozen at build time.

#### Controller ruling on M9.1.4's review (2026-09-29)

- **Untested wiring, for M9.1.21.** Nothing tests that the daemon's `[testing]` reaches a run's frozen limits. The path is `lifecycle.rs:288` `.with_testing(..)`, then `driver/build.rs:301` `testing: &self.ctx.testing`: replacing either with the defaults leaves every test green. **Ruling:** M9.1.21's end-to-end test that runs with a non-default `[testing]` also asserts the run's `run.json` `limits.testing` holds those values.

### Task M9.1.5

The tier keys reach every place M8b keeps a profile key: `run/tiers/mod.rs` (new: `TierProfile`, `GraphSource`, `is_tiered`, `resolve`, `from_repo`, `validate`, `profile_hash`, `Scope`, `ModuleGraph`, `ModuleInfo`, `GraphState`), `Profile.tiers` and `Run.profile_hash` (both `#[serde(default)]`), `resolve_profile`, `check_plan`'s validation (`PlanError { field: "profile.<key>", rule: "profile" }`, M8a's field spelling), `run::globs::path_module`, the stored profile's `validate`/`from_findings`/`apply_edit`/`apply_verification`/`show_text`, `EDIT_KEYS` (the twelve keys between `manifests` and `env`, which is also `profile show`'s order) and `REVERIFY_KEYS` (decision 12's nine), `profile::summary`, `resolve::set_keys` (a stored profile's run notes each ignored plan tier key, as M8b's keys), the onboarding report and its MCP schema, `ONBOARDING_CONTRACT`, and `[orchestrator.profile]`.

Deviations and choices:

- **Two of M9.1.6's files are created here**, because this task cannot work without them: `run/tiers/command.rs` (the placeholder parser that decision 8's `{<name>} is not allowed here` needs, `substitute` and `filter_expr`, which verification needs to run `module_test` "with `{filter:…}` expanded") and `run/tiers/graph.rs` (`from_cargo_metadata`, `from_command_json`, which verification needs to tell a known graph). Both follow Interfaces; `Placeholders` (unspecified there) is `{ module, modules, filter, shard, test }`, each `Option`, and a placeholder whose value is `None` is left as written (tier 0 keeps `{module}`), except `{filter:…}`, which is empty. M9.1.6 owns their full tests; its tests of these two files will pass at once (pinning). Left for it: `from_command_json` does not detect a duplicate key in the JSON object (`serde_json` keeps the last), and its unknown-dependency error is `<dep> is not a module` (the note wraps it as `module graph unknown: <reason>`).
- **Other files not in the task's list.** `profile_hash` is in `run/tiers/mod.rs`, not `plan.rs`, which is at its budget (490 + 25 = 515 exactly). `profile/store.rs`: `Fnv1a64` and its two methods are `pub(crate)` (decision 9 foresaw it; the profile hash needs it now). `run/mod.rs`: `pub mod tiers;`. For the budgets, new files: `profile/proposal_tiers.rs` (pure: the stored profile's tier problems, findings, verification drops and the summary line), `profile/verify_tiers.rs` (I/O: the tier commands of verification), `profile/tests_tiers.rs`, `scout/tests_tiers.rs` (`scout/tests.rs` is at 586), and `config/src/orchestrator/profile_tiers.rs` (a `#[path]` child of `profile.rs`, which would otherwise grow +64 against +45). Growth: `proposal.rs` +39, `verify.rs` +15, `profile/mod.rs` +5, `resolve.rs` +12, `scout/report.rs` +39, `scout/contract.rs` +1, `mcp/tools_scout.rs` +13, `config/orchestrator/profile.rs` +17, `plan.rs` +14 (515), `model.rs` +6.
- **`is_tiered`** is "differs from `TierProfile::default()`". A key set to what its absence means (`module_graph = "none"`, `module_names` equal to its resolved default, `full_shards = 1`) switches nothing on and leaves the profile untiered. `untiered_profile_is_not_tiered` sets each key to a value that is not its default.
- **Validation choices.** `check`'s placeholders are checked only for a tiered profile, so an untiered profile is never refused for a `check` M8a accepts. `build_check`, a graph command and `toolchain_id` allow no placeholder; `check` allows `{filter:…}`, `{shard}`, `{shards}` (so `{test}` in a tiered `check` is refused). A templated placeholder is named `{modules:<template>}` / `{filter:<template>}` in the message. The timing variant reads `timing_tests: module_test must contain {filter:<template>} to leave timing tests out`. `full_shards: check must contain {shard} and {shards}` is also given when there is no `check`.
- **`profile edit` refuses cross-key problems.** Besides the edited key's own problems (M8b), an edit is refused for any problem it introduces under another key, since the tier rules span keys (removing `module_test`'s filter is `slow_tests: …`). For M8b's keys, whose problems all carry their own key, nothing changes.
- **Scout and MCP limits** (the brief gives none): the seven commands and filters 1–2000 characters; `full_triggers` and `test_paths` at most 40 of 1–300 (as the other glob lists); `skip_markers` at most 32 of 1–64 (decision 8); `module_names` the enum `cargo`/`dir`; `full_shards` an integer 1–16. `from_findings` also filters `full_triggers` and `test_paths` by the glob rule, drops markers out of bounds (and past the 32nd) and an out-of-range `full_shards`.
- **Verification details.** A graph's JSON is one long line, longer than the 200 × 300-character tail an engine command keeps, so the graph command's stdout (`cargo metadata --format-version 1 --no-deps --offline` for `cargo`) is redirected to a file in the command's own `TMPDIR` — the confinement's when confined, this process's temporary directory when not, never the checkout — read (at most 16 MiB) and removed. Its bound is `min(GRAPH_TIMEOUT, verify_timeout)`; `toolchain_id`'s `min(TOOLCHAIN_TIMEOUT, verify_timeout)`. The module names are, with `dir` names, the last components of the checkout's directories matching `modules` (hidden ones skipped), and with `cargo` names the graph's packages whose manifest directory is a module directory (`path_module`); with no module directory, the module command gets a failed record whose tail is `not run: no module directory matches modules (<modules>)`. `module_graph = "none"` is not verified. A dropped `full_shards` is listed as a `DroppedCommand` (key `full_shards`, command the number, reason `needs a verified check that contains {shard} and {shards}`). Tier drops are listed after M8b's (`single_test` included).
- **M8b's `check` verification of a tiered profile** runs `check` with `{filter:…}` empty and `{shard}`/`{shards}` as 1 of 1, so the whole suite runs; otherwise a tiered `check` would be run with its placeholders as literal text and dropped. The record keeps the template as its command. An untiered profile's `check` runs exactly as before.
- **The summary line** names only the parts decision 12 lists, read as: `build_check`; `module tests by <cargo|command> graph` (only with a graph); `slow filter` (the brief's "`<n>` slow filter", one expression); `<n> full triggers`. A tiered profile with none of these (only `test_paths`, say) gets no line.
- **Not yet:** verification's commands do not go through the test scheduler (M9.1.8 builds it).
- **Fixture-literal updates** forced by the new fields: `view_fields::old_run_json_loads` strips and checks `profile.tiers` (default) and `profile_hash` (`""`); the `Profile` literals in `crates/daemon/tests/run_exec_env.rs` and `headless_env.rs` gain `tiers: Default::default()`; `scout_schema_limits` counts 29 profile properties, not 17. None is in decision 6's regression set.
- **Red/green.** Against the pre-change `scout/report.rs`, `scout/contract.rs`, `mcp/tools_scout.rs` and `config/orchestrator/profile.rs` (restored from backups afterwards), `onboarding_report_accepts_the_new_keys_and_refuses_unknown_ones` failed (`profile.build_check: not in the schema`), `onboarding_contract_is_exact` failed (no rule 4), `mcp_scout_schema_lists_the_new_profile_keys` failed (no such properties) and `the_profile_tier_keys_are_read` failed (unknown keys). The other new tests (`untiered_profile_is_not_tiered`, `new_key_validation_messages_are_exact`, `plan_and_config_profiles_carry_the_new_keys_with_decision_7_precedence`, `profile_hash_changes_with_any_profile_field`, `path_module_agrees_with_glob_module`, `verification_runs_the_new_commands_in_order_and_drops_failures`, `profile_show_prints_every_new_key`, `summary_has_the_tiers_line_only_when_tiered`, plus `a_plan_with_a_bad_tier_key_is_refused_with_the_key`, `placeholders_used_by_verification_substitute_and_quote`, `a_module_command_with_no_module_directory_is_not_verified`, `edits_of_tier_keys_are_validated_across_keys_and_reverify`) did not compile before the change: `run::tiers`, `path_module`, `Profile.tiers`, `Run.profile_hash` and the verification constants did not exist.

### M9.1.5 review fixes

- **I1, a verified proposal kept a profile every run refused.** `proposal_tiers::apply_verification` now ends with `drop_invalid`, which re-runs decision 8's `tiers::validate` on what verification kept. Every key that still has a problem is dropped and listed as a `DroppedCommand` whose reason is the problem text, several joined by newlines. This repeats until nothing is left, and it cascades:
  - a round drops the keys that problems are about first (the module commands, `build_check`, `toolchain_id`, the graph command, `module_names`, `full_shards`, a tiered `check` with a placeholder it may not hold);
  - `slow_tests` and `timing_tests` are dropped only when nothing else is left, since dropping a module command can make a filter valid again (Repro 1 keeps `slow_tests` once `module_test` without `{module}` is gone);
  - `module_names = "cargo"` goes when the cargo graph went (Repro 2).

  Blank tier values are now refused in `scout/report.rs` (`profile.<key>: must not be blank`) and removed by `from_findings`. The invariant is tested by `a_verified_proposal_always_passes_the_tier_rules`. It covers both repros; `{module}`/`{shard}` in `build_check`, `toolchain_id` and the graph command; `module_tests` without `{modules}`; a module command that cannot take a timing filter; and a tiered `check` with `{module}`. For each case it runs `from_findings`, a verification, then `apply_verification`, and asserts that the result passes `proposal::validate` and that `build_run`'s `check_plan` accepts it as a plan's profile. `blank_tier_values_are_not_proposed` and `onboarding_report_refuses_blank_tier_values` cover blanks. Confirm stores the proposal as `apply_verification` left it, so no change to `service_requests.rs` was needed.
  - Fixture fixes in `profile/tests_tiers.rs`:
    - The shared `tiered()` profile's `module_tests` gained `{filter:--filter %}`. Without it the profile itself broke decision 8 (`slow_tests: module_tests must contain {filter:<template>} …`), and the cascade now drops it.
    - Its expectations were updated: the log line, `profile show` text, and one more drop (`module_names`) in the cargo-graph case.
- **m1.** Verification's module pick skips names starting with `-`, both from directories and from the cargo graph.
- **m3.** `path_module` answers `None` for a path whose module component contains a glob character (`*`, `?`, `[`). Such a component stops the literal prefix, so the path counts as unowned, which is the safe direction (the affected set runs `check`). Verification's directory listing now also skips such names, so it never picks a module `path_module` could not name. A literal pattern component now matches only the entry with exactly that name. Test: `verification_skips_option_like_and_glob_named_module_directories`, with `-rf`, `[a]`, `a*` and `?` beside `a` and `b`. It was red before: the pick was `-rf`.
- **m2 pins**, each shown red under its mutation (the code restored from a backup afterwards):
  - `an_untiered_check_is_verified_as_written`: red with `check_command`'s untiered guard removed.
  - `the_graph_output_file_is_in_tmpdir_not_the_checkout`: red with the file placed in the checkout.
  - `the_graph_timeout_is_the_shorter_bound`: red with the bound set to `GRAPH_TIMEOUT` alone, and red with it set to the verify timeout alone. The bound is now the function `verify_tiers::graph_timeout`, and the test also runs a `sleep 20` graph under a 1 s verify timeout and checks it timed out.
  - `a_plan_tier_key_under_a_stored_profile_is_noted`: red with `build_check` removed from `resolve::set_keys`.
- **Red before the fix:** `a_verified_proposal_always_passes_the_tier_rules`, `blank_tier_values_are_not_proposed`, `onboarding_report_refuses_blank_tier_values` and the m1/m3 test.
- New test file `profile/tests_tiers_review.rs`. `tests_tiers.rs`'s helpers are now `pub(super)` so it can use them. Growth: `scout/report.rs` 378 lines (+44 over M9.1.1's count, within +45), `proposal_tiers.rs` 271, `verify_tiers.rs` 269.

#### Controller ruling C-3 on M9.1.5's re-review (2026-09-29), and round-2 fixes

- **Ruling C-3.**
  - (a) Every command a confirmed profile keeps must expand at run time exactly as verification ran it. If a later drop changes a kept command's expansion, that command is dropped too. Its `DroppedCommand` reason names the key whose removal changed it.
  - (b) In particular, if the final profile is untiered and `check` holds any tier placeholder, `check` is dropped with the reason `check uses tier placeholders but no tier command was kept`. The proposal then has no check, and the existing no-check path handles it. The command is never rewritten.
  - (c) When the problem is a module command with no `{filter:…}` slot while filters are set, that command is dropped, not the filters. A filter is dropped only when no kept command can take it, and then (a) applies.
- **Fix.** `proposal_tiers::drop_invalid` now takes the verified proposal and loops over two steps.
  - It re-validates. A filter's problem (`slow_tests: module_test must contain {filter:<template>} …`) is attributed to the module command it names, and the reason keeps the filter prefix.
  - Once there is no problem left, `changed_expansions` compares each kept `check`, `module_test` and `module_tests` against how verification ran it (`verified_form`). A module command's form includes how its module is named. A command that changed is dropped with `it would no longer run as verified: <keys> was dropped`. A `check` that changed because nothing tiered is left is dropped with (b)'s exact reason.
  - Under (c) a filter is not dropped for a module command's missing slot. *(Superseded by ruling C-4 (3) below: a filter that no kept command can take is dropped.)*
  - To make one definition serve both sides, the expansions verification runs moved to the pure `proposal_tiers.rs`: `check_command`, `module_command`, and `verified_form` built on them. `verify.rs` and `verify_tiers.rs` now call these, so verification and the invariant cannot drift apart.
- **Tests, each red before the fix:**
  - `case_a_an_untiered_check_with_tier_placeholders_is_dropped` covers `module_test` without `{module}`, and a failing `build_check` that was the only tier key.
