  - `case_b_a_module_command_without_a_filter_slot_is_dropped_not_the_filters`: `module_tests` goes, both filters stay, and the kept `module_test` expands to the command verification ran, as seen in its log.
  - `a_kept_graph_command_keeps_the_module_commands_and_a_changed_naming_drops_them` (minor 1).
  - `a_partial_wildcard_component_names_no_module_for_verification_or_path_module` (minor 2).
  - `a_verified_proposal_always_passes_the_tier_rules` now also asserts, for every case, that each kept command's `verified_form` equals the proposal's. Under (c), two of its expectations changed: repro 1 lists `module_test` once, with both problems, and the timing case now drops `module_test` rather than `timing_tests`.
- **Minor 1.** `graph_kept` counts any kept graph, a command's as well as cargo's. Decision 12 drops the module commands only when the graph is dropped. With cargo names and a command graph, `module_names` goes for decision 8, and `module_test` then goes under (a) because its module naming changed. The reason no longer says `needs a module graph`.
- **Minor 2.** `module_dirs` treats a non-literal component as a wildcard only when it is exactly `*`, as `globs::glob_module` does. Any other component with a metacharacter (`mods/a*`) matches nothing, so verification and `path_module` agree.

#### Controller ruling C-4 on M9.1.5's second re-review (2026-09-29), and round-3 fixes

The re-review of `b3ae89c` approved it with three minor findings, ruled as C-4:

1. **C-3 (b) applies literally.** When the proposal was tiered, the final profile is untiered, and `check` holds any tier placeholder, `check` is dropped with `check uses tier placeholders but no tier command was kept`. This holds even when the expansion did not change: `substitute` leaves `{module}` as written, so `check = "sh check.sh {module}"` with a failed, dropped `build_check` used to stay. A never-tiered M8b check is untouched (decision 6). Test: `a_check_with_module_goes_when_the_profile_turns_untiered`, which also checks that an M8b `check` holding `{module} {shard}` stays.
2. **Filter attribution was parsed from free text.** A filter problem goes to a module command only when the message is `module_test(s) must contain {filter:…`; every other filter problem (`slow_tests: must not be blank`) drops the filter itself. Before this fix, that message was attributed to the key `must`, and the loop wrote 32 bogus drops. When a round's attributed key removes nothing, the problem's own key is dropped instead, so no round is a no-op. Test: `a_blank_filter_is_dropped_once`, one drop of `slow_tests` and a valid result.
3. **Overruled C-3 (c)'s "no filter is dropped".** Once nothing else is left to drop, `slow_tests` and `timing_tests` are dropped with `no kept command can take this filter` when no kept command has a `{filter:…}` slot: neither module command, nor `check` as a tiered check. Test: `a_filter_nothing_can_take_is_dropped`, where the only module command, lacking a slot, is dropped and then the filter goes; a tiered `check` with a slot keeps it. This updates two expectations: repro 1 and the timing case of `a_verified_proposal_always_passes_the_tier_rules` now also drop the filter, and the cargo case of `verification_runs_the_new_commands_in_order_and_drops_failures` drops both filters after `check` and the module commands have gone.

Each of the three tests failed before its fix.


### Task M9.1.6

The pure tier logic: `run/tiers/{affected,steps,failing,weakening,cache_key}.rs` (new) beside M9.1.5's `command.rs` and `graph.rs`, each with a `*_tests.rs` declared in `tiers/mod.rs`, and `run/tiers/fixtures/`. `mod.rs` gains `Affected` (with `key()`, decision 30's `affected` part), `StepKind`, `Step`, `TierPlan`, `CacheCtx`, `Signal` and the constants `FAILING_NAMES_MAX`, `RETRY_NAMES_MAX`, `SIGNALS_MAX`, `STAGE_MAX`; `CacheKey` is in `cache_key.rs`. `grep -rn "std::process\|std::fs\|tokio::" crates/daemon/src/run/tiers` prints nothing (M9.1.5's module doc named them; it is reworded).

**Stopped item (controller rules): decision 20's "no `check`" row cannot be planned with Interfaces' `plan` signature.** The row says `Affected::Full` "runs the module tests of every module when a module command exists". `steps::plan(tier, affected, tiers, check)` is given no module list, so it cannot name every module. Implemented: the row's other branch, `build_check` alone (nothing when there is no `build_check` either). Proposed: M9.1.9's executor, which holds the graph, passes `Affected::Modules(<every module>)` when the profile has no `check` and the set is `Full`; or `plan` gains the module names. Nothing here depends on the choice. *(Ruled by C-5 below.)*

Deviations and choices:

- **Fixtures.** All four captured outputs of M9.1.1 are committed (`libtest.txt`, `pytest.txt`, `cargo-metadata-anthrex.json`, `cargo-metadata-three-crates.json`). The graph test reads the real seven-member anthrex output instead of a hand-made "four-crate workspace shaped like anthrex's": captured beats constructed (the review-failure pattern), and it has every shape the brief names (`anthrex` depends on all but `anthrex-fake-agent`; `anthrex-fake-agent`'s dev-dependencies; `anthrex-mcp` naming `anthrex-proto` as normal and dev). The three-crate one covers a renamed dependency and a build-dependency. `nextest.txt` and `go.txt` are hand-written from the documented formats with a first-line comment saying so (ruling C-2); the followups file has the item to replace them.
- **`command.rs` (M9.1.5's): `${…}` is never a placeholder**, whatever the name inside, so `${module}` stays shell text (decision 7: "`${VAR}` … is left alone"). Validation follows: `${module}` no longer counts as `{module}`. Red before the fix: `placeholders_substitute_and_quote`.
- **`graph.rs` (M9.1.5's): a graph command naming one module twice is unknown**, `two modules are named <name>` (the text `from_cargo_metadata` already gives), found by reading the object's keys a second time with a key-only visitor, since `serde_json::Value` keeps the last. Red before: `duplicate_module_names_are_unknown`.
- **Affected set.**
  - `full_triggers`, `hub`, `source` and `test_paths` match with `OwnsMatcher` (`owns`' rules: a literal entry covers everything below it, `*` never crosses `/`). For one path, a trigger is checked before the hub.
  - Naming (decision 10) comes after rule 3, since `cargo` names need the graph: `no package for module <dir>`. With `dir` names, a module directory the known graph does not list gets `Full("module <name> is not in the module graph")` (the brief gives no text; `Full` is the safe direction).
  - Rule 5 needs a non-empty closure, so a docs-only change on an empty graph is `Modules(∅)`, not "every module".
  - A glob list that fails to build (validation refuses it earlier) makes the set `Full` (*superseded by the review fixes below; it used to match nothing*).
- **Steps.**
  - Decision 20's unknown-graph row ("`build_check`, then `check`") and decision 21's `Affected::Full` ("`build_check` does not run") disagree for an unknown graph, which decision 22 makes `Full`. Followed decision 21 and the brief's test name `unknown_graph_is_full_and_plans_check`: `check` alone.
  - No `build_check`: `check` is the whole tier for every affected set, a docs-only one included (decision 20, row 2, literally).
  - No module command and a non-empty `Modules` set: `build_check`, then `check` with the gate filter, then its timing step (row 3).
  - With `module_test` alone, the timing step is one per module (each exclusive), after every tests step; the brief says "a timing step", but a per-module command cannot run several modules in one invocation. With `module_tests`, one.
  - `{shard}`/`{shards}` in a `check` step that is not a shard expand to 1 of 1, as M9.1.5's verification runs it.
  - Exclusive: every timing step, and any other test step whose template has no `{filter:…}` while `timing_tests` is set (decision 25's rule, applied to module commands too, although validation keeps those from arising).
  - Tier 3's steps take their `affected_key` from the `affected` the caller passes. For tier-3 cache hits not to depend on a reason text, M9.1.14 should pass one fixed value.
- **Signals.**
  - Skip markers are read in every changed file (decision 40 names no file restriction); assertion loss only in a file matching `test_paths` or listed by the `#[cfg(test)]` grep.
  - A deleted file gives `DeletedTestFile` when it matches `test_paths` and nothing else.
  - Order: deleted test files first, then per file in diff order its skip markers in line order and then its assertion loss.
  - Paths come from the `---`/`+++` lines (C-unquoted, octal escapes included), else from `diff --git a/<p> b/<p>` (a binary file has no `---`/`+++`).
  - **Open for M9.1.16:** `signals_and_rest` also returns how many signals were cut (the prompt's `… and <n> more`), and `signals` is Interfaces' function over it; `OpResult::DoneChecked { signals }` has no field for the count, so M9.1.16 decides how it travels.
- **Failing names.** A nextest name is the test's name without its binary id (what `single_test` takes); a `TRY <n> FAIL` prefix and a `(<k>/<n>)` counter are accepted. libtest's names block is read only when indented lines follow a `failures:` line at once, so the `---- <name> stdout ----` block is never read.
- **Not added here:** `TierSpec`, `StepOutcome` and `TierOutcome` carry `Priority` and `ScratchAt`, which later tasks bring (M9.1.8, M9.1.9); they are added there. `Scope` now derives `Hash`.
- **Red/green.** Against stubs (the new functions returning empty values), 24 of the 34 tier tests failed: every new test but `command_graph_with_an_unknown_dep_is_unknown`, `cargo_metadata_keeps_workspace_members_and_every_dependency_kind`, `filter_is_empty_without_slow_or_timing` and `filter_expressions_per_scope_are_exact`, which pin M9.1.5's code (the brief says they pass at once), plus M9.1.5's six `validate_tests`. After the change all 34 pass, and the daemon's 1862 library tests pass.

#### Controller rulings C-5 and C-6 on M9.1.6's review (2026-09-29), and fixes

- **Ruling C-5 (the stopped item).** Decision 20's "no `check`, `Full`" case belongs to M9.1.9's executor. With no `check` and a known graph, the executor passes `Affected::Modules(<every module>)` to `plan`. With no `check` and an unknown graph, `build_check` runs alone, which is what `plan` already does for `Full` without `check`. `plan`'s signature is unchanged. On an unknown graph, decision 21 wins over decision 20: `check` alone.
- **Ruling C-6: failing names are never a strict subset of the real failures.** Fixed in `failing.rs`:
  - A name is the whole text between its markers, whitespace included: a doc-test's `src/lib.rs - f (line 3)`, and a pytest id up to the ` - ` that starts the message, where a ` - ` inside a parametrised id's brackets does not count (`test_a[a b]`, `test_b[x - y]`).
  - pytest `ERROR <node id>` lines are read. A `FAILED `/`ERROR ` line counts as pytest's only when its id names a `.py` file, so a program's own `ERROR …` log line is not taken for a test.
  - nextest: every capitalised status before a `[<time>]` except `PASS`, `SKIP`, `SLOW`, `LEAK`, `START` and `RETRY` is a failure: `FAIL`, `TIMEOUT`, `ABORT`, `SIGSEGV`, `SIGABRT`, `LEAK-FAIL` and any other.
  - **Safety net.** `names()` returns no names, so the step is retried whole, when any failure line has no readable name, or when a runner's own summary counts more failures than distinct names were read from that runner. The counts are libtest's `test result:` `<n> failed`, summed over every test binary; pytest's closing line `<n> failed` plus `<n> error(s)`; and nextest's `Summary` `<n> failed` plus `<n> timed out`. Go prints no count.
  - The cap of 50 stays (decision 32). A list that long is never retried name by name: decision 33 does that for at most 10.
  - libtest's `failures:` name list is read only from the last `failures:` block of a test binary's section, which a `test result:` line or the end of the output ends, and only when a `---- … stdout ----` block came before it. A test whose stdout prints `failures:` is not read as names. One existing assertion of `failing_names_are_deduplicated_and_capped` gained a stdout block for this reason.
- **Other findings fixed.**
  - `affected.rs`: a glob list that fails to build gives `Full("<list> globs are invalid: <err>")`, where the list is `full_triggers`, `hub`, `source` or `test_paths`, checked before rule 1 in that order. Before, it matched nothing, so a broken `source` let executing paths be ignored.
  - `weakening.rs`: `git_line_path` unquotes a C-quoted `diff --git "a/…" "b/…"` line, so a quoted, deleted binary test file is a `DeletedTestFile`. An unquoted line whose midpoint is not a character boundary is no longer split. `unquote` maps `\a \b \f \r \v`, as well as `\t` and `\n`, to their control characters.
  - `tiers::validate`: `{modules:}` with an empty template is refused *(superseded by C-7 item 6: the message is now `{modules:<template>} needs a % for the module name`, and it covers every template without an unescaped `%`)*.
- **Tests.**
  - New: `invalid_globs_are_full_never_ignored`, `docs_only_change_on_an_empty_graph_is_empty`, `an_empty_modules_template_is_refused`, `failing_names_keep_whole_names_and_every_failure_kind`, `failing_names_are_none_when_they_may_be_incomplete`, `a_test_printing_failures_is_not_read_as_the_name_list` and `quoted_paths_are_unquoted`.
  - Red before the fixes: the five tests of the findings. `docs_only_change_on_an_empty_graph_is_empty` and the `---`/`+++` part of `quoted_paths_are_unquoted` pin code that already held. Each went red under its mutation, and the code was restored from a backup: dropping `!closure.is_empty()` in rule 5; disabling the octal branch of `unquote`; `side_path` not unquoting; dropping the `\a` mapping.

#### Controller ruling C-7 on M9.1.6's re-review (2026-09-29), and round-2 fixes

- **Ruling C-7 amends decision 33 and binds M9.1.9: parsed failing names are a speed-up, never the verdict.**
  - When a red step's retry runs `single_test` name by name and every name passes, the executor then re-runs the **whole step** once.
  - The step is green, and each name that passed on retry is a flake, only if that whole run passes. Otherwise the step stays red: not cached, not flaky, and the bounce, rung or bisect follows as for any red step.
  - A retry without names, which re-runs the whole step, is unchanged.
  - Reason: real runner output can still name a strict subset of the failures, so a green by-name retry alone must never turn a step green.
  - The parser fixes below still matter: they avoid by-name runs that are wasted.
- **Parser fixes in `failing.rs`** (real output shapes the re-review described). Each test was red before its fix.
  1. **Colour.** Every line loses its ANSI CSI sequences (`ESC [` … final byte) and a trailing `\r` before anything reads it. Test: `coloured_output_is_read`, covering coloured pytest (with its coloured summary still counted) and coloured nextest, each mixed with plain libtest lines.
  2. **pytest runs of 60 s or more.** The summary's time may end in `(h:mm:ss)`: `2 failed in 65.12s (0:01:05)`. Before, such a line was not counted, and a `.pyi` id was not read. Test: `a_long_pytest_run_is_counted`, covering a mypy `.pyi` id next to a `.py` one, and a count above the names.
  3. **A crashed cargo test binary.** There are no names when any line has `process didn't exit successfully: … (signal:`, or when cargo's `error: test failed, to rerun pass …` and `error: doctest failed …` lines outnumber the `test result: FAILED` lines. Test: `a_crashed_test_binary_gives_no_names`, covering the `d_aborts` shape with and without the `Caused by` line, and a failed doctest target.
  4. **Go.** Every `FAIL\t<pkg>` line must follow at least one `--- FAIL:` since the previous package result line (`ok`, `FAIL` or `?`); otherwise there are no names. Test: `a_go_package_that_failed_without_a_test_gives_no_names`, covering a build failure, a `-timeout` panic and a `TestMain` exit. Its new fixtures, `go-build-failed.txt`, `go-timeout.txt` and `go-testmain.txt`, are hand-written and say so on their first line (C-2).
  5. **pytest ids.** A `FAILED`/`ERROR` line is pytest's when its id is `<path>::…`, where the path has no whitespace and its file name has an extension (a bare `.py` path is still accepted, for a collection error). Test: `pytest_ids_may_name_any_file`, covering `test_doc.txt::test_doc.txt` and `x.pyi::mypy`, with `ERROR could not connect::retry` and `FAILED to start` not read.
- **In `tiers/mod.rs`:**
  6. A `{modules:<t>}` template with no unescaped `%` is refused with `module_tests: {modules:<template>} needs a % for the module name`. This covers the empty template, so C-6's `needs a non-empty template` text is gone. Test: `a_modules_template_without_a_percent_is_refused` (it replaces `an_empty_modules_template_is_refused`), covering `{modules:}`, `{modules:-p}` and `{modules:-p%%}` refused, and `{modules:-p %}` and `{modules:-p%%%}` accepted.

### Task M9.1.7

`run/driver/graph.rs` (new, I/O, 291 lines; tests in `driver/graph_tests.rs`, a `#[path]` child as `usage_tests.rs` is): `module_graph` (decision 9, with the cache `<repo_dir>/module-graph.json`) and `toolchain` (decision 11). Both are blocking and are called only from their tests until M9.1.9's executor calls them on its blocking thread; `mod graph;` in `driver.rs` carries `#[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]` until then. `Run.toolchain` and `Run.graph_note` are new `#[serde(default)]` fields in `model.rs` (+8), set to `None` by `build_run` (`plan.rs` +2).

Deviations and choices:

- **Reused, not duplicated.** Commands run through `profile/verify_tiers.rs`'s `capture` (stdout to a file in the command's own `TMPDIR`, read at most 16 MiB; the process group killed on timeout by `exec::run_matching`), with `CARGO_METADATA` and `module_dirs`. `capture` and `module_dirs` are now `pub(crate)`, and `profile/mod.rs` declares `pub(crate) mod verify_tiers`. Parsing is `tiers::graph::{from_cargo_metadata, from_command_json}` (so duplicate module names are unknown), and the key hashing is `store::Fnv1a64` and `store::fingerprint`; the cache file is written with `store::write_atomic`.
- **Two parameters Interfaces' signatures do not list.** `module_graph` takes `manifests: &[String]` (decision 9 keys a command graph's cache on the `manifests` files, which `TierProfile` does not carry) and `env: &[(String, String)]`; `toolchain` takes `env` too. The graph and toolchain commands run "confined exactly as the job's commands", which have an environment (the profile's `env`, and M9.1.8's slot variables); the tests also use it to put a counting `cargo` first on `PATH` without touching the test process's environment.
- **The cargo key** is the FNV-1a of the tracked (`git ls-files -z --cached`, through `run::git::Git`, so `--no-optional-locks`, `NO_HOOKS` and the scrubbed environment) `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` files, path and `store::fingerprint` of the content read from the checkout, in path order. The content is what `cargo metadata` would read, so an edit counts even before it is committed. A command graph's key is the command text, the `manifests` fingerprints and, with `dir` names, the module directories (name and path), because the cached graph holds those directories and a new module directory changes them. Each key starts with `cargo` or `command`, so the two never collide. When the key cannot be computed (git fails), the graph is read and nothing is cached.
- **Cache behaviour.** Only a known graph is stored; storing an existing key moves it to the newest place; past 32 the oldest goes. A missing, unreadable, over-64 MiB or corrupt file reads as empty and is replaced by the next write. A failed write is logged at debug and ignored. Two jobs writing at once can lose one entry (last rename wins); the cache is advisory, so that costs one re-run.
- **Unknown reasons** (`GraphState::Unknown` holds the bare reason, decision 9's note wraps it): `module_graph is none`; `<cargo metadata|the graph command> timed out after <n>s`, `… exited <code>` or `… did not run`, each followed by `: <its last output line>` when there is one; `<label>'s output could not be read`; the parser's own text (`<dep> is not a module`, `two modules are named <name>`, `… is not JSON: …`).
- **The note, once.** `tiers::graph::note_once(&mut run.graph_note, &graph)` (pure, so the engine can call it in M9.1.13) sets `Run.graph_note` to `module graph unknown: <reason>; every tier runs check` the first time a run meets an unknown graph and returns that line to log; afterwards, and for a known graph, it returns `None`. It is in `tiers/graph.rs`, not `driver/graph.rs`, because the engine that applies a `TierOutcome` is pure and must not reach into the driver.
- **The toolchain id** hashes the exit code (4 bytes, little-endian) and the command's stdout (not stderr, which `capture` keeps only in the tail). Anything but exit 0 with readable stdout — a failure, a timeout, a command that could not be confined — is `unknown`.
- **Test bounds.** `module_graph_with` and `toolchain_with` take the bound and the runner; the public functions pass `GRAPH_TIMEOUT`/`TOOLCHAIN_TIMEOUT` and `capture`. The timeout test uses a 1 s bound and a script that records its pid and process group (`ps -o pgid=`), then asserts with a 10 s deadline loop that both are gone by `kill(pid, 0)`/`killpg(pgid, 0)` (signal 0 only asks; nothing is sent, and the group is checked not to be the test's own). No wall-clock assertion was added.
- **An extra test**, `the_graph_cache_keeps_the_newest_32_entries_and_replaces_a_corrupt_file`, carries the brief's "the cache keeps at most 32 entries" without running cargo 33 times, and runs where cargo is absent.
- **Fixture update:** `view_fields::old_run_json_loads` strips and checks `toolchain` and `graph_note` (both `null`), as for each earlier addition.
- **Red/green.** Against a stub `driver/graph.rs` (every graph unknown, every toolchain `unknown`, `cache_put` a no-op) and a stub `note_once` (always `None`), all six tests failed; with the implementation (restored from a backup) all six pass, and the daemon's 1880 library tests pass. `cargo_graph_is_read_offline_and_cached_by_manifest_hash` ran here with cargo 1.92.0 (not skipped).

#### Controller ruling C-8 on M9.1.7's review (2026-09-29), and fixes

1. **Two module directories with one last component** (`mods/a`, `other/a`) used to collapse into one module, and the graph came out known. Now `dirs` is built with a check, and a repeat gives `Unknown("two modules are named <n>")` before the command runs. Test: `two_module_directories_with_one_name_are_unknown`.
2. **A command graph's key could go stale.**
   - (a) Each `manifests` entry is a glob under the `owns` rules (`OwnsMatcher`: a literal entry covers everything below it, `*` never crosses `/`). It is matched against `git ls-files -z --cached --others --exclude-standard` in the checkout, and each match is fingerprinted by path and content. A `manifests` list that does not build as globs gives no key, so the graph is read and not cached.
   - (b) With an empty `manifests`, the key is the command text, the module directories and the checkout's `HEAD` tree (`git rev-parse --verify HEAD^{tree}`). This is correct, but the graph is re-read on every new tree. **Repositories should set `manifests` so a command graph is re-read only when the files it reads change.**
   - Tests: `a_manifests_glob_is_expanded_and_its_edit_re_runs_the_command` covers an edit, a new matching untracked file, and a file under `deps/sub/` that `deps/*.txt` does not match. `with_no_manifests_a_new_tree_re_runs_the_command` covers a commit that changes `graph.sh`.
3. **Toolchain id from stderr.** When the command's stdout is empty, the id hashes the exit code plus its stderr. `capture` sends stdout to a file, so the outcome's tail holds only stderr: the last 200 lines, each cut to 300 characters. This covers `java -version`. Test: `a_toolchain_that_prints_only_to_stderr_is_told_apart`.
4. **Untracked Cargo files.** The cargo key's file list is the same `--cached --others --exclude-standard` listing, so an untracked new crate changes the key. The cargo test's workspace now uses `members = ["crates/*"]`, and an untracked `crates/c/Cargo.toml` re-runs cargo and adds `ax-c`. (The ruling names `crates/b`; `b` is tracked in that test, so the new crate is `c`.)
5. **For M9.1.9:** add `TierSpec.manifests: Vec<String>`, taken from the resolved profile (`Profile.manifests`), and pass it to `module_graph`. Never pass `&[]` in place of the profile's value: an empty list switches the key to the `HEAD` tree, which is correct but re-reads the graph on every tree.
6. **The flaky timeout test.** `a_graph_command_that_times_out_is_unknown` now:
   - writes `$$` to its own file first, then `ps -o pgid=` to a second file, each through a temp file and `mv`;
   - reads both ids in a 10 s deadline loop;
   - uses a 3 s bound instead of 1 s. The old 1 s bound could kill the script while `ps` was still running, before the ids file was written.

   It passed 30 runs out of 30.

- **Red before the fixes:** tests 1–4 (4 as the new step of `cargo_graph_is_read_offline_and_cached_by_manifest_hash`) each failed on their assertions. After the fixes, all 10 `driver::graph` tests and the daemon's 1884 library tests pass.
- **Split:** the C-8 tests are in `driver/graph_tests_keys.rs` (a `#[path]` child of `graph.rs`), because `graph_tests.rs` would have grown to 685 lines. Its helpers are now `pub(super)`.
- **Other test changes:** `command_graph_reads_json_from_the_command` now runs in a git repository, since the `manifests` listing needs one.

#### Controller ruling C-9 on M9.1.7's re-review (2026-09-29), and fixes

- **Finding.** A `manifests` entry that matched no listed regular file left a key that never changed, so a stale graph was served across commits. Four shapes caused it: a gitignored manifest, an entry matching nothing, a symlinked manifest file, and a manifest inside a symlinked directory. In the last case git lists only the link, so the entry matches nothing.
- **Ruling C-9.** The key gains the checkout's `HEAD` tree id (`git rev-parse --verify HEAD^{tree}`) whenever the fingerprinted files are not all the graph is read from. An unborn `HEAD` in that case gives no key, and nothing is cached.
  - **Command graph:** the tree is added when any `manifests` entry, matched on its own, matches no listed file, or matches a path whose fingerprint is a symlink (`link:…`) or `missing`. `missing` is also what a path under a symlinked directory gives. An empty `manifests` keys on the tree as before (C-8 (2b)).
  - **Cargo: the exact detection was chosen.** The tree is added when:
    - any listed `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` is not a regular file;
    - the root `Cargo.toml` is not listed, or cannot be read or parsed;
    - a `workspace.members` directory's `Cargo.toml` is not listed, such as an ignored member or one under a symlinked directory;
    - a member entry cannot be expanded exactly. Only literal components and whole-`*` components are expanded. A `*` component matches every directory, hidden ones too, as cargo's glob does. Any other glob, an absolute path, or `..` counts as not expandable.

    `workspace.exclude` is not read, so an excluded directory without a listed `Cargo.toml` adds the tree. That errs on the safe side. A root package without `[workspace]` needs only its own listed `Cargo.toml`.
- **Tests** (`driver/graph_tests_keys.rs`). Each was red before the fix; after it, all 19 `driver::graph` tests and the daemon's 1893 library tests pass.
  - Command graph: `a_gitignored_manifest_keys_on_the_tree`, `a_symlinked_manifest_keys_on_the_tree`, `a_manifest_in_a_symlinked_directory_keys_on_the_tree` and `a_manifest_that_matches_nothing_keys_on_the_tree` (a commit changing `graph.sh`). Each re-runs the command after a commit.
  - `an_unborn_head_where_the_tree_is_needed_caches_nothing`.
  - Cargo: `an_ignored_cargo_member_keys_on_the_tree` and `a_symlinked_cargo_manifest_keys_on_the_tree`, run through a stub runner that counts calls, so they need no cargo.
  - Controls, to show the tree is not always added: `regular_manifests_do_not_key_on_the_tree` and `a_listed_cargo_workspace_does_not_key_on_the_tree`. An unrelated commit keeps the cached graph in both, and both passed before and after.
- **The timeout test** is unchanged apart from a one-line comment saying the 3 s bound is what protects the ids' writes.

### Task M9.1.8

`run/slots/` (new): `book.rs` (pure: `Priority`, `Want`, `SlotRequest`, `Ticket`, `SlotBook`, `SLOT_VARS`, `LOAD_VAR`, `slot_vars`, `worker_caps`) and `mod.rs` (`TestScheduler`, `SlotGrant`, `default_slots`, `stamp`), tests in `book_tests.rs` and `mod_tests.rs`. `RunService` holds one `Arc<TestScheduler>` (`RunService::scheduler()` for M9.1.9 and profile verification). A worker's environment carries the four caps before its `TMPDIR`. `grep -n "tokio\|std::sync\|std::time" crates/daemon/src/run/slots/book.rs` prints nothing. Standing rule 5 is in `docs/timing-budgets.md`, with a row for this task's deadline loops.

Deviations and choices:

- **`Half` rounds up** (ruled by C-10 below). Decision 24 says `max(1, slots / 2)`, which gives 1, 1, 2, 3 for 1, 3, 4, 7 slots. The task's test `half_and_one_round_as_decided` says 1, 2, 2, 4, which is `slots.div_ceil(2)` (at least 1). The test's numbers were followed: the brief's own acceptance test is concrete, and rounding up leaves no slot idle when two gates share an odd count.
- **Where the workers' `test_slots` comes from.** `worker_spec` is pure and built by the engine (`engine/dispatch.rs`), which has no access to the scheduler. `Run` gains `#[serde(default)] test_slots: u32`, and the driver stamps the scheduler's count on every run: `slots::stamp` sets `EventKind::Start`'s run in `RunService::handle`, beside `mark_killed`, and `RunService::restore` sets each loaded run (C-11 below; the first version claimed `handle` also stamped restored runs, which never pass through it). A restored run takes the current daemon's count, since `test_slots` is daemon-wide (decision 3). `build_run` sets 0 (`plan.rs` +1). `RunInfo.test_slots` can read it in M9.1.20. `view_fields::old_run_json_loads` strips and checks `test_slots` (`0`), as for each earlier addition.
- **`lifecycle.rs` is unchanged.** `RunService::new` builds the scheduler from `ctx.testing.test_slots`, else `default_slots()`. `lifecycle.rs` already puts the daemon's `[testing]` table into the context (`with_testing`, M9.1.4), so the scheduler is still built once per daemon from the config read at start. Profile verification (not yet scheduled) can reach it through `RunService::scheduler()`.
- **A profile value of a capped variable is replaced** in a worker's environment, as decision 26 has the slot variables win over the profile's for engine commands. `worker_caps` treats `max_writers = 0` as 1.
- **The decider is not in the launch test.** Its session is started with an empty environment (`decider/call.rs`, `HeadlessHandle::spawn(.., &[], ..)`) and never sees a `Run`, so it cannot get the caps. `worker_env_carries_the_caps` checks the worker, both reviewer runtimes, a planner and the orchestrator.
- **`ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD`** is computed with integers, rounded half up (3 of 4 held is `0.8`, 1 of 3 is `0.3`).
- **Tests added beyond the brief's list:** `a_request_gets_what_is_free_and_the_next_waits_its_turn`, `a_released_wait_gives_up_its_place`, `load_is_held_over_slots_to_one_decimal`, `worker_caps_are_test_slots_over_writers_at_least_one` (pure), and `a_dropped_acquire_gives_up_its_place` (async). A dropped `acquire` future gives its ticket back. Without that, an abandoned waiter at the head of the queue would block every later request, because the grant order is strict.
- **File budgets.** `driver.rs` 555 → 564 (+12 over M9.1.1's 552, budget +10); `plan.rs` 517 → 518 (M9.1.5 left it at its budget); `role_launch.rs` 586 → 589 (+4); `model.rs` +4.
- **Red/green.** Before the change, the tests did not compile (`run::slots` did not exist). Against a mutated build, 10 of the 12 slot tests failed, each on its own assertion. The mutations were: the book ignoring the class, exclusivity ignored, `Half` floored, `load` truncated, `worker_caps` uncapped, a waiter's drop not releasing, no `ANTHREX_TEST_LOAD` in `env()`, no wake-up on grant, `default_slots` not subtracting 2, and no caps in `worker_env`. The code was restored from a backup afterwards. The two that passed under the mutations were `a_request_gets_what_is_free_and_the_next_waits_its_turn` and `a_released_wait_gives_up_its_place`, which no mutation touched. After the change, the daemon's 1905 library tests pass, and so do `headless_env`, `run_journal`, `run_exec_env` and `run_e2e_worker_pins`.

#### Controller rulings C-10 and C-11 on M9.1.8's review (2026-09-29), and fixes

- **C-10.** `Half` is `max(1, ceil(slots / 2))`: the task's test wins over decision 24's formula. The implementation stands.
- **C-11 (findings 1 and 2): restored runs were never stamped.** The production restore steps `EventKind::Restore` through `restore_step` → `guarded_step`, never through `handle`. So `slots::stamp`'s `Restore` arm was dead code. A pre-9.1 run came back with `test_slots = 0`, which capped every worker at 1, and a 9.1 run kept the old daemon's count.
  - **Fix.** `RunService::restore` sets `run.test_slots = scheduler().slots()` on each loaded run before `as_loaded.insert`. The count lives in memory and is saved with the run's next real change, so B-5 (an unchanged restored run writes nothing) still holds.
  - `stamp` now handles `Start` only. Its doc and `Run.test_slots`' doc are corrected, and so is deviation 2 above.
  - **Tests** (`driver/tests.rs`, using a service whose `[testing] test_slots` is set):
    - `a_started_run_carries_the_schedulers_slots` (5).
    - `a_restored_run_carries_the_schedulers_slots_and_its_workers_caps`: a paused run saved with `test_slots = 0` is restored with 6. Its `worker_spec` (the pure builder dispatch calls, computed on the restored run instead of resuming it, so no session starts) carries `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` equal to `worker_caps(6, 2)`, which is `3`.
    - `restoring_an_unchanged_finished_run_writes_nothing` now also asserts the restored run carries `scheduler().slots()`.
  - **Red.** Before the fix, both restore tests failed (`left: 0`). The start test failed (`left: 0, right: 5`) with the `stamp` call removed from `handle`; the code was restored from a backup.
- **Minor 3.** `a_dropped_acquire_gives_up_its_place` now waits, in a deadline loop, until the book shows a waiting entry (`SlotBook::waiting()`, new) before it aborts. It ends by checking that nothing is held or waiting. It still fails when an abandoned waiter's drop does not release its ticket.
- **Accepted as they are:** the file budgets (`driver.rs` 564, `plan.rs` 518) and decision 24's strict order. By design, a waiting exclusive step can starve behind a steady stream of higher-priority requests.

### Task M9.1.9

`run/driver/tier.rs` (new, 314 lines: `run_tier`, `run_test_at`, the affected set, the graph and the toolchain) and `run/driver/tier_step.rs` (new, 462 lines: the isolated command, a job's slot wait and M8a's scratch checkout, and the step with its retry), tests in `driver/tier_tests.rs` and `driver/tier_tests_retry.rs` and one test binary, `crates/daemon/tests/run_tier_env.rs`. `OpKind::{Tier, TestAt}` and `OpResult::{Tier, TestAt}` are appended last in `engine/ops.rs`; `driver/ops.rs` routes both; `reconcile/mod.rs` reconciles both `NotStarted`. `git/tiers.rs` (new) has `changed_paths` and `tree_of`. `exec::run_matching`, `run_shell`, `run_confined` and `confine::confined` take `extra`, set after `env`; every existing caller passes `&[]`.

Deviations and choices:

- **Signatures.**
  - `run_tier` and `run_test_at` take `op: OpId`, which decision 28's `s<op>-<step>` needs. `ops::run` takes it too, and `driver/adapt.rs::run_op` passes it.
  - `run_tier` takes no `&TestCache` yet. M9.1.10 adds it with the type.
  - `run_matching` takes `env` and `extra` as one pair (`exec::Env`). A new `exec::run_lines` hands every whole output line (up to 64 KiB each) to a callback, so decision 32 reads the whole output, not the tail.
- **Where the types are.** `TierSpec`, `TestAtSpec`, `StepOutcome` and `TierOutcome` are in a new `run/tiers/spec.rs`, re-exported from `run::tiers`, because `tiers/mod.rs` is at 496 lines.
- **`TierSpec` fields.**
  - It gains `manifests: Vec<String>` (ruling C-8 (5)), passed unchanged to `module_graph`.
  - `diff_base` is the stage head alone. The executor reads `<diff_base>...<head>` for tier 1 and `<diff_base> <head>` for tier 2, so M9.1.13 sets `head` to the candidate before it calls `run_tier`.
- **Affected set.**
  - Tier 3's is always `Full("full suite")` (`tier::FULL_SUITE`), so its cache key never depends on a reason text (M9.1.6's note).
  - An untiered spec records `Full("untiered profile")`. It runs `check` as written, not through `steps::plan`, so no placeholder is substituted (ruling C-4). It gets no retry.
  - Ruling C-5 is applied after `affected`: with no `check` and a known graph, `Full` becomes `Modules(<every module of the graph>)`.
- **Retry (decision 33, ruling C-7).**
  - The by-name runs and the whole re-run after them hold the step's grant. A whole retry without names (none read, more than 10, or no `single_test`) waits for a new grant.
  - A by-name failure keeps the step red with the first run's names and tail.
  - A red whole re-run reports its own names, or the first run's when it has none.
  - A green retry lists the first run's names as `flaky`. With more than 10 names that is all of them.
  - Output past 16 MiB (`tier_step::OUTPUT_BYTES_MAX`) gives no names, so the step is retried whole.
- **The scratch checkout.**
  - M8a's contract (`OpKind::Check`, ruling T13-I3) is re-implemented in `Job::prepare` through the `GitQueue` the executor is given, since `run_tier` runs without a `RunService`.
  - Its `setup` waits for one slot (decision 24) and runs isolated in `s<op>-setup`.
- **Graph and toolchain.**
  - `module_graph` and `toolchain` each run on a bounded blocking thread with the job's `env`, confinement and a directory of their own (`s<op>-graph`, `s<op>-toolchain`). They take no slot, because decision 23 does not list them.
  - A graph read that does not finish is `Unknown(<the error>)`. A toolchain run that does not finish is `unknown`.
  - `TierOutcome.graph_note` is the bare unknown reason on every job that meets one; the engine's `note_once` (M9.1.7) keeps the note to one.
- **Probes.**
  - A `TestAt` probe holds one `FullStage`, `Half` grant for all its commands.
  - A timed-out command is red at once, without a retry (a hang is not a flake).
  - `OpResult::TestAt.failing` lists the commands that failed. The engine built them from `BisectRecord.tests` in order.
  - `show` stays `None` (M9.1.15).
- **Bounds.**
  - Every blocking call is `spawn_blocking` inside `tokio::time::timeout`:
    - a step: its timeout + 2 × `OUTPUT_GRACE` + 30 s;
    - a git read: 2 × `git_timeout` + 30 s;
    - the graph: `GRAPH_TIMEOUT` + 4 × `git_timeout` + 30 s;
    - the toolchain: `TOOLCHAIN_TIMEOUT` + 30 s.
  - A thread past its bound is abandoned, not killed. The command's own timeout ends it and kills its process group.
  - The git common directory is the confinement's when confined; otherwise one `rev-parse --git-common-dir` read.
- **File budgets.**
  - `exec.rs` would have grown by 43 lines against a budget of 12. Its unit tests moved to `exec_tests.rs` first, in a separate pure-move commit, so it is now 555 → 525.
  - `driver/ops.rs` is 487 → 512: +25 against +20. rustfmt wraps `run`'s new signature and each routing arm.
  - `driver.rs` is 564 → 566: two `mod` lines. It was already over its budget, accepted by C-10.
  - `engine/ops.rs` +19, `confine.rs` +2.
  - `tier.rs` passed 450 lines, so the retry and the isolation directory are in `tier_step.rs`, as the brief says. The tests are split into `tier_tests.rs` (480) and `tier_tests_retry.rs` (140).
- **Tests.**
  - Every executor test runs unconfined (`ctx.confine = None`).
  - The "daemon's own `ANTHREX_SOCKET`" half of `step_environment_puts_the_socket_and_data_dir_under_its_tmpdir` sets that variable in the process environment. So it is its own one-test binary, `run_tier_env.rs`: `an_unconfined_step_never_sees_the_daemons_socket_or_data_dir`, which calls `driver::tier_step::run_isolated`, the function every tier command goes through. For this, `driver::tier_step` is a `pub mod`.
  - Added beyond the brief's list: `a_by_name_pass_is_not_green_when_the_whole_step_still_fails` (ruling C-7). The names read (`tests::a`) are a strict subset of what fails: the step also fails on a line no name carries. The by-name run passes, and the step stays red with nothing flaky.
  - `test_at_checks_out_the_commit_and_runs_the_named_tests_twice_on_red` also checks that a command which fails once and then passes leaves the probe green.
- **Red/green.**
  - Against a stub executor (`run_tier` returning an empty green outcome, `run_test_at` a green probe), all nine executor tests failed. With the executor (restored from a backup) all nine pass.
  - Three mutations, each restored from a backup afterwards:
    - skipping C-7's whole re-run failed the C-7 test and the flaky test;
    - dropping the isolation variables failed the environment test;
    - the same mutation failed `run_tier_env`, where the step saw `/nonexistent/daemon.sock`.
  - After the change, the daemon's 1916 library tests pass, as do `run_exec`, `run_exec_env`, `run_confine{,_escape,_network,_protected}`, `run_journal` and `run_tier_env`.

Open, for later tasks:

- **`manifests` does not reach a run** (for M9.1.13, which builds `TierSpec`). Ruling C-8 (5) says to take `TierSpec.manifests` from the resolved profile (`Profile.manifests`). The run's frozen `Profile` (`model.rs`) has no `manifests`, and neither does `ProfileSpec`: only the stored `RepoProfile` carries it. `profile::resolve::run_profile` drops it when it builds the plan's spec.
  - The key has to travel with `ChosenProfile` into `build_run`, as `output_filter` and `filter_prefixes` do, and into `Profile` as a `#[serde(default)]` field.
  - Until then the engine can only pass `[]`, which C-8 forbids, and a command graph would be keyed on the `HEAD` tree.
- **M8a's commands do not go through the scheduler yet** (controller ruling 1, decision 23): `setup` after a worktree op, the proof, M8a's `Check`, the final check and the candidate check. None of this task's files run them, and no task's file list names `driver/gate_ops.rs`, `driver/merge.rs`'s check or `proof.rs` for it. M9.1.13 (which replaces the candidate check) and M9.1.21 (the regression run) are the likely owners; the controller should assign it.
- **The engine does not yet route `Tier` and `TestAt`.** `engine/results.rs` and `engine/restore.rs::lost` still send both to the `_ => {}` arm: a result is dropped, and a lost op's `gate_op`/`full_op`/bisect probe is not cleared. The engine emits neither until M9.1.13–M9.1.15, which own those rows (decision 29).

#### Controller ruling C-12 on M9.1.9's review (2026-09-30), and fixes

- **Ruling C-12.** The review confirmed C-7 holds and that `de4fa2e` is a pure move. The three items left open above are assigned:
  - (a) Carrying `manifests` from `RepoProfile` through `ChosenProfile`, `build_run` and `Profile` into `TierSpec` is owned by M9.1.13.
  - (b) Routing M8a's `setup`, proof, `Check`, final check and candidate check through the scheduler (decision 23) is owned by M9.1.13.
  - (c) The engine's routes for `Tier` and `TestAt` (`results.rs`, `restore::lost`) are owned by M9.1.13–M9.1.15.
- **Fix 1 (important): the memory cap, and parsing on a tokio worker.**
  - What was wrong:
    - The cap counted `line.len() + 1` per kept line, so 16 MiB of empty lines held about 16 M `String`s: 392 MB RSS, peaking at 776 MB.
    - `failing::names` ran over the whole `Vec` in the async `run_step`, on a tokio worker, while the step's grant was held.
  - Now each kept line counts `line.len() + size_of::<String>()` against `OUTPUT_BYTES_MAX` (16 MiB), and the lines are dropped as soon as the cap is passed.
  - `run_isolated` reads the names on its blocking thread. `StepRun` carries `names: Option<Vec<String>>` (`None`: not read, or past the cap) and never the lines, so the `Vec` is dropped on that thread.
  - Only a tiered test step collects (`StepCommand.collect`). An untiered check, `setup`, a by-name `single_test` run and a probe keep M8a's 200-line tail only.
  - Measured with a throwaway release-build test (not committed), as the process's peak `ru_maxrss`, starting from 6 MB:
    - 16,777,216 empty lines: 25 MB (names `None`);
    - then 650,000 empty lines, just under the cap: 40 MB (names `Some([])`).
- **Fix 2 (minor): ruling C-5 pinned.** `no_check_runs_every_modules_tests_on_a_full_set_with_a_known_graph` covers three cases:
  - A full trigger with no `check` and a known graph runs `build`, then the tests of `a`, `b` and `c` (`Modules({a,b,c})`).
  - With `check` set, it runs `check` alone.
  - With no `check` and an unknown graph, it runs `build_check` alone.

  It passed at once, since it pins code that already held. It went red with the C-5 block in `tier.rs` short-circuited (`if false && …`): `Full("full trigger: mods/b/lib.txt")` instead of `Modules`.
- **Fix 3 (minor): the past-the-cap fallback.** `output_past_the_cap_has_no_names_and_is_retried_whole` prints a named libtest failure followed by a million empty lines. With no names, the whole step runs again and nothing runs by name.
  - It was red before fix 1: the byte-only count stayed under the cap, so the step was retried by name.
  - It is red again with the cap check disabled after the fix.
- **Fix 4 (minor): a committed confined-step test.** `run_confine.rs::a_confined_tier_step_binds_its_own_socket_and_not_the_daemons` (macOS only) runs one step through `tier_step::run_isolated` under `world()`'s `ConfineSpec`. It checks that:
  - `TMPDIR` is writable;
  - `$ANTHREX_DATA_DIR` can be made;
  - `$ANTHREX_SOCKET` can be bound;
  - connecting to the test's own listener at the confinement's stand-in daemon socket is denied, and the listener accepts nothing;
  - the step directory is removed afterwards.

  It went red with the isolation variables dropped: `KeyError: 'ANTHREX_DATA_DIR'`, because confinement strips every `ANTHREX_*`.
- `run_tier_env.rs` now sets `collect: true` and checks that `names` is `Some([])`.

### Task M9.1.10

`run/test_cache.rs` (new, 336 lines; tests in `test_cache_tests.rs`): `TestCache`, `CacheEntry`, `CacheLine` (one line of the file: `{key, entry}`), `CACHE_FILE`, `CACHE_LINES_MAX`, and two constants the Interfaces entry does not list, `CACHE_LINES_KEPT` and `CACHE_TAIL_BYTES`. `RunService` holds one `TestCache`, built from `[testing] test_cache_days` (`RunService::test_cache()`); `driver/ops.rs` passes it to `run_tier`, which now takes `cache: &TestCache` after the scheduler. `driver/tier.rs` looks each step up before it asks for slots and stores each green step after it.

Deviations and choices:

- **`lifecycle.rs` is unchanged**, as in M9.1.8: `RunService::new` builds the cache from `ctx.testing.test_cache_days`, and `lifecycle.rs` already puts the daemon's `[testing]` into that context (`with_testing`, M9.1.4). So there is still one cache per daemon, built from the config read at start.
- **`TestCache` is a cheap `Clone` around an `Arc`**, so `run_tier` keeps Interfaces' `&TestCache` and still moves a handle onto the blocking thread (`spawn_blocking` needs `'static`).
- **The index keeps no tail, and a stored tail is capped.** Decision 30's value keeps "the last 200 lines". At 20 000 lines a repository, M8a's 200 lines of up to 300 characters would be about 1.2 GB in the daemon's memory, and a rewrite would read the whole file into memory. So:
  - the file's `tail` is the green run's last whole lines, at most 200 and at most `CACHE_TAIL_BYTES` (4 KiB); a single longer last line keeps its end;
  - the in-memory index holds each key and its entry with the tail cleared, and `lookup` returns that. Nothing reads a hit's tail: a green `StepOutcome` has none.
- **The cap trims to 18 000 lines** (`CACHE_LINES_KEPT`), not to 20 000. The file never holds more than 20 000 lines, and the oldest go first, but a full cache is rewritten once every 2 000 stores instead of at every store. A load that finds more than 20 000 lines (a hand-edited file) trims the same way.
- **What a load drops, and when it rewrites.** A line is dropped when it is older than `test_cache_days`, is not `ok`, does not parse, is not UTF-8 JSON, is longer than 1 MiB (skipped without being held in memory), or is a last line with no newline (a torn append). When any was dropped, the file is rewritten with `write_atomic`. A missing file is empty. A file that cannot be read (a directory in its place, say) is a miss, is not remembered as loaded, and makes a store fail; the executor logs a failed store at debug and goes on, since a lost entry costs one re-run.
- **Locking.** The index map is one `std::sync::Mutex` taken with `crate::lock`, only to read or update the index. Loads, appends and rewrites run with it released. Two loads of one repository at once both read the file, and the first to finish is kept. A store that races a rewrite can lose its line (the last rename wins); the index then follows the rewritten file. The cache is advisory, as M9.1.7's graph cache is.
- **`store` refuses an entry that is not `ok`**, and a line that says `ok: false` is never a hit (`a_red_entry_is_refused_and_a_red_line_is_never_a_hit`, added beyond the brief's list).
- **The executor's rules** (`tier.rs`):
  - The job's key context is `spec.cache`, but only when the profile is tiered. When this job ran `toolchain_id` itself (decision 11), its output replaces the context's toolchain. Any context whose toolchain is `"unknown"` (`graph::TOOLCHAIN_UNKNOWN`) turns the cache off for the job. So an untiered profile and an unknown toolchain neither read nor write, even when the engine passes a context.
  - A step is stored only when its outcome is `ok`. That is a first run that passed, or a retry whose whole re-run passed (ruling C-7). A by-name pass alone is never green, a red step and a timed-out step are never stored. The entry carries `ran.secs`, the `flaky` names, the green run's tail, `at`, the run id and the tier.
  - A hit is a `StepOutcome` with `cached: true`, `ok: true`, `code: Some(0)`, `secs: 0`, `granted: 0`, and no `flaky`. The flake was recorded when the step ran; repeating it on every hit would count one flake in several runs toward decision 34's quarantine proposal.
  - Each lookup and store runs on a blocking thread bounded by `SLACK` (30 s). A lookup that fails or does not finish is a miss.
  - Tier 3's key is `full:full suite` (M9.1.9's fixed value), so it never depends on a reason text.
- **`tier_step::run_step` now returns the tail of a green run too** (the run that decided the step), so the cache can keep it. Its only caller, `tier.rs`, still uses the tail only when the step is red.
- **Where the tests are.** The pure cache tests are in `test_cache_tests.rs` (added beyond the brief's list there: `a_long_tail_is_cut_to_its_last_lines`). The executor's tests are in `driver/tier_tests_cache.rs`, a `#[path]` child of `tier_tests.rs` (as `tier_tests_retry.rs` is), on M9.1.9's rig:
  - `red_and_timed_out_steps_are_never_cached` (a red step, a by-name pass whose whole re-run fails, a timed-out step, and a flaky step stored green with its name), `tier1_and_tier2_share_a_key_for_the_same_tree_and_scope` (tier 2 on tier 1's tree runs nothing; tier 3 on it runs);
  - added: `a_second_job_on_the_same_tree_runs_nothing`, `untiered_and_unknown_toolchain_jobs_neither_read_nor_write`.

  `Rig::tier` runs with the cache off (`TestCache::new(0)`), so M9.1.9's tests are unchanged. `Rig::tier_with` takes a cache.
  - `cache_key_changes_with_tree_command_profile_toolchain_and_env` reads the brief's "five" as tree, command, profile, toolchain and the profile's `env`. `env` reaches the key through `profile_hash`, whose JSON includes it; the test checks that the hash differs. It also checks `scope` and `affected`.
  - `cache_file_is_capped_at_twenty_thousand_lines` writes the 20 000 lines by hand rather than storing them one at a time, since each store is an `fsync`. It checks that the file is replaced by a rename (a new inode, no `.tmp` left).
- **Red/green.**
  - Against a stub cache (`lookup` always `None`, `store` a no-op), 10 of the 12 new tests failed. The two that passed pin "nothing is stored": `zero_days_disables_the_cache` and `a_red_entry_is_refused_and_a_red_line_is_never_a_hit`.
  - Six mutations, each restored from a backup afterwards:
    - the executor storing red steps too: `red_and_timed_out_steps_are_never_cached` failed;
    - the context not dropped for an untiered profile: `untiered_and_unknown_toolchain_jobs_neither_read_nor_write` failed;
    - the context kept with an `"unknown"` toolchain: the same test failed;
    - no rewrite on load: `entries_older_…_pruned` and `a_corrupt_line_is_skipped_not_fatal` failed;
    - no trim past the cap: `cache_file_is_capped_at_twenty_thousand_lines` failed;
    - no expiry check on lookup: `entries_older_…_pruned` failed.
  - After the change, the daemon's 1930 library tests pass, as do `run_tier_env`, `run_confine`, `run_journal` and `run_exec_env`. The 23 cache and tier tests passed 8 runs out of 8.
- **File budgets.** `driver.rs` 566 → 575 (+9; it was over budget before, accepted by C-10). `driver/ops.rs` 512 → 513. `driver/tier.rs` 314 → 399. `driver/tier_step.rs` 482 → 483. `tier_tests.rs` 531 → 541.
- **Followup recorded** (followups file, "From M9.1.10"): `sweep_leftovers` does not remove a crashed rewrite's temporary file for `test-cache.jsonl` or `module-graph.json`.

Open, for later tasks:

- **Tier 2's "skip `materialize` when every step hits"** (decision 16) is M9.1.13's (`driver/merge.rs`). `run_tier` checks the cache per step inside the job, after the job has already read its diff and graph. M9.1.13 needs a way to know every step's key before it materializes. One option is a `plan_keys` helper that computes the affected set and the steps without running anything, then calls `TestCache::lookup` for each key.
- **The engine does not yet build `TierSpec.cache`** (M9.1.13). It should set `CacheCtx { profile_hash: run.profile_hash, toolchain: run.toolchain or "none" }`, and pass `None` for an untiered profile.

#### Controller ruling C-13 on M9.1.10's review (2026-09-30), and fixes

1. **A result is stored only for the tree it ran on (important).** The key's tree is `tree_of(root, head)`, but the steps run in `spec.dir`, which may be at another commit, or dirty.
   - Lookups still use the head's tree, so M9.1.13's pre-check (before `materialize`) can compute every key.
   - Before each store, `git::checkout_tree` (new, `git/tiers.rs`) reads the checkout on a bounded blocking thread, through `Git` (`--no-optional-locks`, scrubbed environment): `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no -z` must be empty, and then `HEAD^{tree}`.
   - The step is stored only when that tree equals the key's tree. Otherwise nothing is stored, and the job logs it once, at `info`.
   - Test: `a_checkout_at_another_tree_stores_nothing`.
     - `scratch.commit = base` with `head = head` stores nothing. This was red before the fix: all three steps were stored.
     - A build step that edits a tracked file stores nothing. This was red with the clean check disabled.
2. **Steps are not independent (amends decision 31).**
   - Every step's key is looked up before anything runs, in one bounded blocking call (`lookup_all`).
   - A build step's hit counts only when every later step of the job also hits. When any step from the build step on misses, everything from the build step on runs (`usable_hits`), and each step that passes is stored.
   - Tests:
     - `red_and_timed_out_steps_are_never_cached` now requires the job after a red test step to run the build again. This was red before: the build was a hit.
     - `a_cached_build_runs_again_when_a_later_step_misses` (new; red before) keeps the build command and changes the test command. Both run, and a third job hits all three.
3. **Timestamps in the future.** An entry whose `at` is more than 300 s (`FUTURE_SECS`) ahead of now is expired: a lookup misses it, and a load drops it and rewrites the file. Test: `an_entry_from_the_future_is_expired_and_dropped`. An entry 60 s ahead is still a hit. This was red before.
4. **The index follows the file.**
   - Each lookup and store stats the cache file on its blocking thread. The stat compares device, inode, mtime (seconds and nanoseconds) and length.
   - When the stat differs from what the index last saw, the index is reloaded. A missing file gives an empty index.
   - The cache's own appends and rewrites record the new stat, so they cause no reload.
   - Test: `a_deleted_or_replaced_file_is_read_again`. It deletes the file (then a miss), replaces it by a rename (then the new key hits and the old one misses), and stores again. This was red before.
5. **`PATH` in the key (beyond the brief; a deviation).**
   - The key's toolchain part is now `<toolchain> path:<16-hex FNV-1a of PATH>` (`tiers::cache_key::with_path`).
   - The `PATH` is the steps' effective one: the last `PATH` in the job's `env` (the profile's), else the daemon's inherited `PATH`. A program found on another `PATH` is another toolchain.
   - The `"unknown"` check runs before the hash is added.
   - Test: `a_changed_path_misses`. A longer `PATH` runs every step again; the first `PATH` hits again. This was red before.
   - The toolchain assertions of `a_second_job_on_the_same_tree_runs_nothing` and `untiered_and_unknown_toolchain_jobs_neither_read_nor_write` now expect the `path:` suffix.
- After the fixes, the 28 cache and tier tests pass 5 runs out of 5; the daemon's 1935 library tests, `run_tier_env` and `run_confine` pass; fmt and both `+1.98` clippy runs (host, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`) are clean.

### Task M9.1.11

Decisions 43–45 and 54's plan rules, for every plan source, plus the two controller findings of M9.1.3's review.

- **Where the rules live.** A new `run/validate_stages.rs` (129 lines) holds all of them: `check_stage_fields` (the `stage-<n>` id, the 1–32 range, `atomic_reason`), called from `validate::check_fields`; `stage_rules` (dependencies across stages, contiguity, one atomic task per stage), called from `validate_graph::validate_tasks_with` after the per-task loop; and `reserved_new_id` (`fix<n>`). The brief put the rules in `validate_graph.rs` (+55) and `validate.rs` (+20); written there, they took +63 and +41. Now `validate.rs` is 512 → 519, `validate_graph.rs` 411 → 414.
- **The stage rules run over the whole list after every batch.** They do not run only over the tasks a batch touches, because moving one task can break the rule for another: moving `t2` to stage 2 is refused through its dependant `t3`, as `task t3: stage: stage 1 cannot depend on t2 in stage 2`. The dependency rule covers every unfinished task. Contiguity counts tasks in every state. The atomic rule counts every task that is not cancelled. A stage outside 1–32 gets only the range error, and it opens no gap in the contiguity check.
- **`fix<n>` is checked where tasks are added, not in `check_fields`.** The places are `build_run`, `add_task` and `split_task`'s new tasks. An amend runs `check_fields` again on an existing task, and decision 39 says an existing `fix<n>` task (the engine's own, or one an older `run.json` holds) keeps loading. So `fix1` can still be amended (`an_existing_fix_task_can_still_be_amended`). The engine's `fixes::add_fix` (M9.1.15) resolves its task with `resolve_task_lenient`, as `integration.rs` does, so it never meets this check. `stage-<n>` is in `check_fields`, beside `integration` and `running`, as decision 45 says.
- **Error texts.** `stage-<n>` shows the id itself, as `integration`'s refusal does: `stage-1 is reserved for stage branches`. `fix<n>` is decision 39's text, word for word, with the literal `<n>`.
- **`amend_task`'s `stage`.** It is added to `edits.rs`'s restricted fields. On a task that has started, it gets M8a's per-state refusal, `task t4 is working; stage can be amended only on pending, queued or blocked tasks`. Every restricted field gets one refusal, and nothing of the amend is applied. That fixes M9.1.3's silent drop: `{stage: 2, priority: 5}` on a working task is refused as a whole, and the priority stays as it was. `edit_log` still describes a rejected batch as it did, `amend t4 stage 2`, but only in a record with `accepted: false`, so the log never describes as applied a move that did not happen. The engine test asserts that record. On a task that has not started, a stage-only amend is applied and logged as `amended: stage`. A stage does not re-resolve the task, since no derived field depends on it. `edits_orch.rs`, which is in the brief's file list, needed no change: the rules run for every source through `apply_edits`.
- **Decision 54 is implemented here** (no task listed it). In `resolve_task_lenient`, `atomic` makes the task a hub task, so it runs alone, is `tdd` for code, has a frontier route and is reviewed at `Frontier`. It also skips rule 7.2.2's raise to L: an atomic task that spans modules with `interface_change` is raised to M (7.2.1), not L. Its note for the raise to M is `size raised from S to M: an atomic task is a hub task (rule 4.3)`. Test: `an_atomic_task_is_a_hub_task_that_keeps_its_size`. Its control, the same task without `atomic`, is refused at 7.2.4.
- **The decision-43 defect (controller ruling on M9.1.3).**
  - `tools_bounds.rs`: `EDIT` gains `stage`, and `TASK` gains `stage`, `atomic` and `atomic_reason` (1–300 characters). `atomic` is not an `amend_task` field: it is set when a task is added, and `AmendTask` has no such field.
  - `crates/mcp/src/tools_orch.rs`: `plan_task` gains `stage` (`{"type":"integer","minimum":1,"maximum":32}`), `atomic` (boolean) and `atomic_reason` (`text(300)`). `plan_edit` gains `stage`.
  - `orch_tools.json` gains the same fields in all six places: `plan_edit` twice, and `plan_task` four times, as `task` and `into` in both `edit_plan` and `submit_epic`.
  - `proto::STAGES_MAX = 32` is the one constant for the schema and the plan rule. Adding it changes no wire type, so there are no new round-trip tests, and PROTO stays 11.
- **The combinator guard.** `no_orchestrator_schema_combines_schemas_but_a_messages_to` walks every key of every orchestrator and sub-planner schema. The only `oneOf`, `anyOf` or `allOf` it may find is `edit_plan.properties.edits.items.properties.to.oneOf`, and its `submit_epic` twin. That is M9's `message` `to` (a list of ids, or `running` / `stage:<n>`). **That `oneOf` is the one recorded exception.** Every new field is a plain type.
- **Deferred to M9.1.12: `Run.stage_layout` and decision 46's layout rule.** The pinning test `plans_without_stages_are_one_stage` asserts that every task is in stage 1 with no `atomic`. It covers `EXAMPLE_PLAN`, a one-task plan and M8b's `m8b_run.json`. It cannot assert `stage_layout == Single`, because `StageLayout` belongs to `model_stages.rs`, which M9.1.12 creates. The layout rule has no field to read until then either: `this run was approved with one stage; its tasks stay in stage 1`. It needs setting at every approval site: `requests.rs:73, :80, :171` and `orch.rs:452`. **Until M9.1.12 lands, an approved run accepts edits that name stage 2.** M9.1.12 should add the field, set it at approval, add the rule to `stage_rules`, and extend the pinning test.
- **An existing test changed.** `validate_tests_fields.rs::a_task_id_cannot_look_like_a_stage_target` (M9, pinning) expected `stage-3` to be a valid id. It now expects decision 45's refusal of `stage-3` next to the `stage:3` pattern error.
- **Tests.**
  - New files:
    - `validate_tests_stages.rs`: `stage_dependency_on_a_later_stage_is_rejected`, `stages_must_be_contiguous_and_non_empty`, `stage_must_be_between_1_and_32`, `two_atomic_tasks_in_one_stage_are_rejected`, `atomic_needs_a_reason`, `an_atomic_task_is_a_hub_task_that_keeps_its_size`, and `plans_without_stages_are_one_stage` (pinning).
    - `edits_tests_stages.rs`: `amend_stage_only_before_start`, `stage_rules_count_cancelled_and_merged_tasks_as_decided`, `an_existing_fix_task_can_still_be_amended`.
    - `engine/tests/plan_stages.rs`, through the real tool parser, bounds and engine:
      - `stage_and_fix_ids_are_reserved_for_every_source`, over four sources: a plan file, the orchestrator's `edit_plan` with `submit`, an orchestrator `add_task` on a running run, and a sub-planner's `submit_epic`;
      - `stage_and_atomic_reach_edit_plan_and_submit_epic`, which includes the combined amend.
  - Added to existing files:
    - `tools_tests_bounds.rs`: `stage_and_atomic_fields_are_accepted`. The at-every-bound task now carries the three fields, and `atomic_reason` is checked at 301 characters.
    - `mcp/tools_orch_tests.rs`: `plan_task_and_plan_edit_take_stage_and_atomic` and the combinator guard.
  - Red before the change: every new test except the pinning test and the combinator guard, which pinned true behaviour. The bounds suite went red too, because every test built from `task()` hit `task: atomic: unknown field`. The MCP tests did not compile, because `STAGES_MAX` did not exist.
- **Left open for the controller:**
  1. **Split children do not inherit a stage.** `stage` defaults to 1 in serde, so a `split_task` child that names no stage lands in stage 1 even when its parent was in stage 2. The rules refuse the split only when a dependency then crosses stages. Otherwise the plan changes stage silently. Serde cannot tell an absent `stage` from `1`. Options: make `PlanTask.stage` an `Option` (a wire change), or have the contract text (M9.1.19) require `stage` on every split child.
  2. **Decision 41's implicit dependencies ignore stages.** For two overlapping tasks that have not started, the later one in plan order waits for the earlier one. That means a stage-1 task written after an overlapping stage-2 task waits for it, and then merges into stage 1 without its change. M9.1.12's `ready_in_stage` should decide whether an implicit dependency may point to a later stage.
- **File budgets:**
  - `validate.rs` +7 (budget +20).
  - `validate_graph.rs` +3 (+55).
  - `edits.rs` 560 → 570. That is +11 over M9.1.1's 559, against a budget of +10. The overshoot includes M9.1.3's line.
  - `plan.rs` +1 (+25).
  - `edits_orch.rs` +0 (+15).
  - `tools_bounds.rs` +7, `mcp/tools_orch.rs` +10. Neither file is in the budget table.
- **Test counts and checks.**
  - The daemon's library tests: 1948 pass. In one full run, `driver::orch_ops::tests::resolve_target_op_runs_git_off_the_worker_threads` failed under load: it is a wall-clock test around a real 9–10 s sleep, and this change does not touch it. It passes alone.
  - `anthrex-mcp` and `anthrex-proto` tests pass: 15 + 3 + 8 + 98.
  - `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and both `cargo +1.98 clippy` runs (host and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`) are clean.

#### Controller ruling C-14 on M9.1.11's review (2026-09-30), and fixes

The review of `e7dad40` approved it. Ruling C-14 decides the two items it left open and adds four more:

- **(a) Split children take their parent's stage.** A split cannot change stages. `validate_stages::split_child_stage` sets each child to its parent's stage. A child that names another stage is refused with `task <child>: split_task: children stay in their parent's stage` (rule `4.1`). `stage` defaults to 1 in serde, so a child naming `stage = 1` cannot be told apart from one naming none, and it takes the parent's stage too. Test: `split_children_take_their_parents_stage`, with the parent in stage 2. It was red before: the child landed in stage 1.
- **(b) Owned by M9.1.12; recorded here only.** Decision 41's implicit dependencies become stage-aware. A task implicitly depends only on overlapping tasks in its own or an earlier stage. A later-stage task depends on an overlapping earlier-stage task, whatever the plan order.
- **(c) Owned by M9.1.12; recorded here only.** Three things wait for it: `Run.stage_layout`; decision 46's one-stage rule after approval, together with the stage-layout assertion of `plans_without_stages_are_one_stage`; and the stage an epic's integration review goes into, which `engine/integration.rs:128` hard-codes to 1.
- **(d) A stage amend also needs `start_commit.is_none()`.** A `blocked(human)` or `blocked(conflict)` task that has a worktree has started. M8a's per-state text says blocked tasks may be amended, so it does not fit. Such an amend is refused as a whole with `task <id> has started: its stage cannot change`: field empty, rule `13`, displayed alone like the per-state refusals. Test: `a_blocked_task_with_a_start_commit_keeps_its_stage`. It covers both reasons, together with a priority. The control, the same task with no start commit, moves. It was red before: the batch applied.
- **(e) `stage-<n>` is reserved for new tasks only, as `fix<n>` is.** The check moves from `check_stage_fields` to `reserved_new_id`, which `build_run`, `add_task` and `split_task` call. A task an M9 run already holds under the name `stage-3` can still be amended. Test: `an_existing_stage_named_task_can_still_be_amended`, which also asserts that adding `stage-4` is still refused. It was red before: the amend was refused with `stage-3 is reserved for stage branches`.
- **(f) The dead assertion in `amend_stage_only_before_start` is replaced.** `assert_eq!(working, before)` compared a borrowed run that `apply_edits` never changes. The test now asserts that the combined `{stage, priority}` batch returns `Err` with only the stage refusal. The control, the priority alone on the same working task, applies. This pins true behaviour, so it passed at once.
- **Checks.**
  - The `run::edits`, `run::validate` and `plan_stages` tests pass (105).
  - The daemon's library tests pass (1951).
  - `cargo fmt --all --check` and both `cargo +1.98 clippy` runs (host and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`) are clean.
- **File sizes.** `edits.rs` is 570 → 581 (C-14 (a) and (d)), which puts it 12 lines over its +10 budget but still under 600. `validate_stages.rs` is 141.

### Task M9.1.12

Decisions 46–48 and 53, controller rulings C-14 (b) and (c).

- **What exists now.**
  - `run/model_stages.rs`: `StageLayout`, `StageRecord` (with `StageRecord::new`), `StageMerge`, `StageFull`, `BisectRecord`, `Probe`, `SyncState`, `FixOf`, `TierRecord`, exactly as "Interfaces" lists them.
  - `Run` gains `stage_layout` and `stages`, and the methods `stage`, `stage_head`, `stage_branch` and `full_path`. `Task` gains `stage()`.
  - `engine/stages.rs`: `set_stage_head` (the one writer of `run_head`), `ready_in_stage`, `create_pass`, `fix_layout`, `created` (the `StageCreated` result), `guard_list`, `task_merged`, `stage_of_branch`, `ensure_first`, `rebaseline`, and `Rebaseline`.
  - `git/refs_tx.rs`: `cas` (one `update-ref --no-deref --stdin` transaction), `create_branch` (create-only), and `alias_of` (the branch the integration worktree stays on).
  - `driver/stage_ops.rs` executes `CreateStageBranch`. `VerifyRefs` moved there from `driver/ops.rs`, which keeps `ops.rs` at 482 lines.
  - `OpKind::CreateStageBranch` and `OpResult::StageCreated` are added. `MergeCandidate` gains `guarded` and `also_integration`, and `VerifyRefs` gains `guarded`. All three new fields are `#[serde(default)]`, and an empty list is guarded as M8a's `[(run_branch, expected_run_head)]`.
  - `guard_refs` takes the list. It refuses an empty list, because the run id comes from the refs and with none every run's work would count.
  - Reconcile gains the `CreateStageBranch` row. The `MergeCandidate` row reattaches to the alias. There is no `Propagate` row yet: that op is M9.1.17's.
- **Deviations.**
  1. **`Run.stage_head(n)` of a `Single` run is `run_head` for every `n`.** In a `Single` run, `integration` is the one branch, so `run_head` is the stage-1 head. `set_stage_head` keeps `StageRecord(1).head` equal to it. The reason: many existing tests, and the `run_journal` rigs, write `run.run_head` directly, and a separate stage-1 head would drift from theirs. `Run::head_for(task)` is added (not in "Interfaces"): it gives the task's stage head, or `run_head` while that stage is not created. It is what every task-context site reads.
  2. **A `Multi` run records no stage until its branch exists.** At approval, `fix_layout` drops the stage-1 record that `build_run` made (it was on `integration`). `create_pass` then creates `stage-1` from `run_head` on the first running pass: in the same step for the gate's approval, and after `CreateRunBranch` for `--yes`. So `Run.stages` holds only created stages, and a lost `CreateStageBranch` is simply issued again. `fix_layout(run, now)` takes no `fx`, and `create_pass(run, fx)` takes no `now`.
  3. **`merge::merging` is also true while a `CreateStageBranch` is in flight, and `create_pass` does not run while a candidate is.** A new stage starts from the highest stage's head, which `integration` aliases. A merge landing meanwhile would leave `integration` behind the new stage. Decision 49 names only `MergeCandidate` and `Propagate`.
  4. **A stage whose tasks were all cancelled is created empty once a later stage has live work.** Without this, the stages above it would never be created. Test: `a_stage_of_cancelled_tasks_does_not_strand_the_stages_above`.
  5. **A failed `CreateStageBranch` halts the run, retryable by a plain `run resume`.** Otherwise the next pass would re-issue it at once, in a loop. A branch that exists at another commit halts it as a moved ref.
  6. **`EventKind::Resume.rebaseline` is now `Option<Rebaseline>`,** with `From<(String, String)>`. For a `Multi` run, the driver also reads every created stage ref. The engine then sets each stage head and takes `run_head` from the highest (decision 47). The `integration` head it read is used only for a `Single` run.
  7. **Merged work is recorded in the stage at merge time** (`task_merged`): `tasks_in`, `merges` and the stage's `last_green_candidate`. `Run.last_green_candidate` follows only the highest stage (decision 47's table). A merge into a lower stage leaves it alone, which the tests assert.
  8. **`.full` is salvaged under `_full`**, as `refs/anthrex/salvage/<run>/_full/1`. A ref component cannot start with `.`, and no task id can start with `_`.
  9. **The held-task hand-back (`holds.rs`) also waits for `ready_in_stage`,** not only `deps_done`. Otherwise, before its stage holds a dependency, it would hand back a stage head that does not contain it.
  10. **`Propagate`, `propagate_due`, `Run.{fix_seq, full_op, queue_idle_since}` and `Task.{origin, fixes, sync, signals}` are not added here.** Their tasks (M9.1.14–17) add them. Nothing in this task reads them.
- **C-14 (b), implicit dependencies.** `validate_graph::implicit_deps` compares stages first:
  - Across stages, the later-stage task waits for the earlier-stage one, whatever the plan order.
  - A started later-stage task gets no edge, and an earlier stage never waits for a later one.
  - Within a stage, decision 41 is unchanged.
  - Test: `implicit_deps_point_only_to_the_same_or_an_earlier_stage`, which covers both plan orders, one stage and a started task.
- **C-14 (c).**
  - `fix_layout` runs at the four approval sites: `requests.rs` for the fast path, `--yes` and the gate, and `orch.rs`'s `--yes` submit.
  - `validate_stages::single_layout_rule` runs in `apply_edits`. Once a `Single` run is past `planning` and `awaiting_approval`, any live task in a stage above 1 is refused with `this run was approved with one stage; its tasks stay in stage 1`.
  - `plans_without_stages_are_one_stage` now also asserts `Single`, one record and `stage_branch(1) == run_branch()`.
  - **The integration review's stage is the highest stage among the epic's own tasks,** and its target is `<base>..<that stage's head>` (decision 47's table). Test: `an_epic_integration_review_goes_into_its_highest_stage`.
- **Left open.**
  1. **Until M9.1.17's propagate, the highest stage's head does not contain an epic's merges into a lower stage.** A review target of `<base>..<stage head>` then leaves them out. M9.1.17 should make `integration::due` wait until the highest stage's `tasks_in` holds every merged task of the epic.
  2. **A `CreateStageBranch` that finds its branch elsewhere halts on each `run resume`, until the user moves or deletes that branch.** No record exists for it, so `--rebaseline` cannot adopt it.
- **The remaining uses of `run_head` in `crates/daemon/src/run`, none of them task-context:**
  - `plan.rs` (`build_run`): the initial value.
  - `snapshot.rs`: `RunInfo.run_head`, the `integration` head (unchanged, per decision 47).
  - `engine/stages.rs`: the writer, the guard list's `integration` entry, `ensure_first`, and stage 1's `from`.
  - `engine/complete.rs`:
    - `VerifyRefs` and `Accept.expected_run_head`: `integration`.
    - The final check's green test: the highest stage, per decision 37's untiered check.
  - `engine/merge.rs` and `engine/integration.rs`: only as the fallback for a stage not created.
  - `model.rs`: the `Single` stage head and the fallback of `head_for`.
  - `driver/requests.rs`: accept's classification of the base (the `integration` head).
  - The rest are op fields named `run_head` (`HandBack`, `VerifyDone`, `CountCommits`, `DiffSoFar`, `ResolutionAt`) and git-layer parameters. They now carry the task's stage head.
  - `grep -rn "run_head =" crates/daemon/src/run/engine` shows only `stages.rs`. That includes the tests: `Fixture::merge` and `gates_rounds.rs` now write through `set_stage_head`.
- **Existing tests changed.**
  - The eight `rebaseline: Some((..))` constructions take `.into()`.
  - The exact `MergeCandidate` and `VerifyRefs` in `merge.rs` and `merge_complete.rs` gain M8a's one-ref guard list.
  - `view_fields.rs::old_run_json_loads` removes the two new keys.
  - `guard_refs`' callers in `tests/run_git_merge.rs` and `run_git_base_guard.rs` pass a one-entry list.
  - The `run_journal` rigs' `MergeCandidate`s gain the two defaults.
  - No `crates/cli/tests/run_e2e_*` file changed.
- **Tests added.**
  - `engine/tests/stages.rs` has 12 tests:
    - `single_stage_run_has_no_stage_refs`;
    - `set_stage_head_moves_run_head_only_for_the_highest_stage` and `run_head_is_the_highest_stage_head`;
    - `stage_branch_is_created_from_the_lower_stage_head_when_its_first_task_is_ready` and `tasks_in_a_stage_wait_for_their_branch`;

    - `the_layout_is_fixed_at_the_gate_and_a_single_run_keeps_one_stage`;

    - `a_multi_run_rebaselines_every_stage_head`;
    - `an_epic_integration_review_goes_into_its_highest_stage`;
    - `a_stage_of_cancelled_tasks_does_not_strand_the_stages_above`;
    - `stage_ops_round_trip_through_the_journal`.
  - `git/refs_tx_tests.rs` has 4 tests, on real git: `cas_updates_every_ref_or_none`, the missing-ref and symbolic-ref case, `create_branch_is_create_only`, and the alias.
  - `driver/stage_ops_tests.rs` has 4 tests, on real git through `driver/merge.rs::candidate`:
    - the highest stage and `integration` move together;
    - a lower stage moves alone;
    - `guard_refuses_when_any_guarded_ref_moved`;
    - `CreateStageBranch`'s replay and moved-ref case.
  - `reconcile::tests::reconcile_create_stage_branch_is_done_when_the_ref_exists_at_from` (real git) covers all three rows.
  - `validate_tests_stages.rs` gains `implicit_deps_point_only_to_the_same_or_an_earlier_stage` and the layout assertions of the pinning test.
- **Red before green.**
  - The `refs_tx` tests failed against stubs.
  - Every engine, driver, reconcile and implicit-dependency test was checked by a mutation that removed the behaviour: `create_pass`, `ready_in_stage`, `fix_layout`, `ensure_first`, the guard list, the integration stage, the single-stage rule, the candidate's stage branch, the paired CAS, the driver's guard list, the alias reattach, the reconcile row, and the stage-aware implicit rule. Each mutation turned the tests that cover it red.
  - `m8a_run_json_restores_as_a_single_stage` is marked pinning, but it too fails without `ensure_first`.
